Collectively, segmentation and recursion ensure that all the request prompts are benign but can lead to malicious outcomes. We conduct experiments on the real-world text-to-image generation system ($i.e.$, DALL$\cdot$E 3) to validate the effectiveness of Inception. The results indicate that Inception surpasses the state-of-the-art by a 14\% margin in attack success rate.
- **Summary**: **Summary:** The paper titled "Inception: Jailbreak the Memory Mechanism of Text-to-Image Generation Systems" addresses the vulnerabilities in text-to-image generation systems, specifically focusing on memory mechanisms that enhance interaction efficacy. It introduces a novel attack method named Inception, which facilitates jailbreak attacks by embedding unsafe prompts through segmented communications during multi-turn interactions. Unlike previous methods that risk detection due to prompt fusion, Inception optimizes through a structured process involving prompt segmentation and recursion, allowing for the creation of benign inputs leading to hazardous outputs. Experiments conducted on DALL·E 3 demonstrate that Inception achieves a 14% higher attack success rate compared to existing methods. **Critical Evaluation:** The paper presents a compelling investigation into the security implications of memory mechanisms in T2I generation systems, adding valuable insight into an area that has not been adequately addressed in prior research. The novelty lies in its unique approach to jailbreak attacks, moving away from traditional single-prompt attacks to a more sophisticated multi-turn manipulation.  However, several aspects warrant consideration:  1. **Novelty**: The introduction of Inception as a multi-turn attack demonstrates a noteworthy advancement from previous methodologies, marking a significant contribution to security research in AI-generated content. However, the concept of breaking down prompts into segments is not entirely new in adversarial contexts, which somewhat dilutes the paper's innovation. 2. **Experimental Robustness**: While the experiments are reported as successful against DALL·E 3, the paper would benefit from a broader evaluation across multiple T2I systems to reinforce the generalizability of the findings. It raises concerns regarding the applicability and effectiveness of the approach in varied scenarios. 3. **Impact**: The implications of the research are substantial, especially with the increasing deployment of T2I systems in society. If widely adopted, the attacks proposed could lead to misuse and ethical dilemmas, highlighting the need for improved security measures. However, the potential for misuse of this research also necessitates caution in its dissemination. 4. **Clarity and Depth**: The explanations of methodology, particularly segmentation and recursion, could be more detailed to provide deeper insights into how these strategies work in practical scenarios. Greater technical clarity would strengthen the paper’s contribution. In conclusion, the paper offers a noteworthy advancement in understanding and exploiting vulnerabilities in text-to-image chat systems, presenting both innovative ideas and significant implications for security practices. However, its effectiveness and real-world applicability across systems could be better substantiated. **Score: 7**  This score reflects the paper’s strong foundation in exploring a crucial issue in T2I systems while acknowledging that further work is needed to fully establish its applicability and to explore broader consequences in different contexts.
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### FiLA-Video: Spatio-Temporal Compression for Fine-Grained Long Video Understanding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20384v1)
- **Authors**: Yanan Guo, Wenhui Dong, Jun Song, Shiding Zhu, Xuan Zhang, Hanqing Yang, Yingbo Wang, Yang Du, Xianing Chen, Bo Zheng
- **Abstract**: Recent advancements in video understanding within visual large language models (VLLMs) have led to notable progress. However, the complexity of video data and contextual processing limitations still hinder long-video comprehension. A common approach is video feature compression to reduce token input to large language models, yet many methods either fail to prioritize essential features, leading to redundant inter-frame information, or introduce computationally expensive modules.To address these issues, we propose FiLA(Fine-grained Vision Language Model)-Video, a novel framework that leverages a lightweight dynamic-weight multi-frame fusion strategy, which adaptively integrates multiple frames into a single representation while preserving key video information and reducing computational costs. To enhance frame selection for fusion, we introduce a keyframe selection strategy, effectively identifying informative frames from a larger pool for improved summarization. Additionally, we present a simple yet effective long-video training data generation strategy, boosting model performance without extensive manual annotation. Experimental results demonstrate that FiLA-Video achieves superior efficiency and accuracy in long-video comprehension compared to existing methods.
- **Summary**: ### Summary: The paper titled "FiLA-Video: Spatio-Temporal Compression for Fine-Grained Long Video Understanding" presents a new framework, FiLA-Video, aimed at improving the comprehension of long videos by addressing the challenges associated with video data complexity and tokenization in visual large language models (VLLMs). The authors recognize that traditional video feature compression methods often retain redundant information or are computationally expensive. To counter these issues, FiLA-Video employs a dynamic-weight multi-frame fusion strategy, which intelligently combines multiple frames into a single representative token without losing critical video information. Keyframe selection is emphasized to enhance the relevance of the frames chosen for compression. Additionally, the approach introduces an innovative long-video training data generation process that enhances model efficiency by minimizing the need for detailed manual annotations. Experimental results indicate that FiLA-Video significantly outperforms existing methods in terms of both efficiency and accuracy for long-video understanding tasks. ### Evaluation of Novelty and Significance: Upon reviewing the paper, several strengths and weaknesses can be identified: **Strengths:** 1. **Innovative Approach:** The dynamic-weight multi-frame fusion strategy is a novel contribution that seeks to address the dual problems of feature redundancy and computational cost, which are prevalent in existing frameworks. 2. **Keyframe Selection Strategy:** The emphasis on identifying informative frames is crucial in a field where context retention over longer durations is vital for video understanding. 3. **Training Data Generation:** The approach to long-video data generation holds the potential to reduce the burden of manual annotation, which can be a significant challenge in model training. 4. **Experimental Validation:** The paper presents experimental results that demonstrate both efficiency and accuracy improvements, providing empirical support for the proposed methods. **Weaknesses:** 1. **Contextual Application:** While the experiments indicate improved performance, it is essential to precisely understand how this approach applies across diverse video types, including those with varying pacing or action types. The performance across different genres or contexts of video is not thoroughly explored. 2. **Comparative Analysis:** A more extensive comparison with a broader range of existing methods, perhaps including those that leverage different techniques or architectures, would substantiate the claimed advantages of FiLA-Video. 3. **Real-World Applicability:** Practical implications and potential limitations of the method in deployed environments could be elaborated, especially concerning real-time applications. Despite these weaknesses, the paper makes a meaningful contribution to the field of video understanding and represents a step forward in addressing inefficiencies in processing long videos. ### Conclusion: Overall, FiLA-Video reflects a solid advancement in video understanding by introducing innovative techniques that tackle significant challenges. While there are some areas for improvement in scope and practical application, the advancements in feature compression and data generation make it a noteworthy addition to ongoing research efforts. **Score: 8**
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Partial Answer of How Transformers Learn Automata
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20395v1)
- **Authors**: Tiantian, Zhang
- **Abstract**: We introduce a novel framework for simulating finite automata using representation-theoretic semidirect products and Fourier modules, achieving more efficient Transformer-based implementations.
- **Summary**: ### Summary The paper titled "Partial Answer of How Transformers Learn Automata" presents a new framework that utilizes representation-theoretic semidirect products in conjunction with Fourier modules to simulate finite automata. The authors claim that this framework leads to more efficient implementations of Transformer architectures, which are widely used in various natural language processing (NLP) tasks. By leveraging these advanced mathematical concepts, the authors aim to shed light on the underlying mechanisms of Transformers in learning and processing formal languages represented by automata. ### Critical Evaluation **Novelty**: The introduction of representation-theoretic approaches combined with semidirect products and Fourier modules is an interesting twist on how finite automata can be simulated within Transformers. While there have been prior works that investigate the connection between neural networks and formal language theory, specifically finite automata, this paper attempts a more structured mathematical framework. This indicates some novelty, as it bridges theoretical constructs with practical implementations in machine learning. **Strengths**: 1. **Mathematical Rigor**: The use of advanced mathematical frameworks adds depth and potentially provides a robust foundation for future exploration. 2. **Potential Efficiency Gains**: If the claims about improved efficiency in Transformer implementations hold true, this could have practical applications in NLP tasks and beyond. 3. **Interdisciplinary Approach**: The blend of areas, such as representation theory and machine learning, may foster further interdisciplinary research. **Weaknesses**: 1. **Lack of Empirical Evidence**: The abstract does not mention experimental results validating the efficiency gains of the proposed framework. Without empirical data, the practicality of the framework remains questionable. 2. **Applicability**: The implications for how this framework can be generalized beyond finite automata are not addressed, which could limit its significance in broader contexts. 3. **Complexity**: The introduction of complex mathematical structures may deter practitioners in the field who may be more interested in heuristic or less complex models. **Potential Influence**: The impact of the paper will significantly depend on the empirical validation of its claims and the ability of the community to adopt these mathematical frameworks in practical settings. If it succeeds, it could reshape approaches to understanding the capabilities of Transformers regarding formal languages. ### Conclusion Given the strengths regarding mathematical innovation and potential practical applications, weighed against the weaknesses of a lack of empirical data and broader applicability issues, I would assign this paper a score of 7. This score reflects a solid contribution to the field with the potential for significant impact, contingent on further validation and exploration of the proposed theories. Score: 7
- **Classification**: cs.FL
- **Score**: 7/10

### SCOPE-MRI: Bankart Lesion Detection as a Case Study in Data Curation and Deep Learning for Challenging Diagnoses
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20405v1)
- **Authors**: Sahil Sethi, Sai Reddy, Mansi Sakarvadia, Jordan Serotte, Darlington Nwaudo, Nicholas Maassen, Lewis Shi
- **Abstract**: While deep learning has shown strong performance in musculoskeletal imaging, existing work has largely focused on pathologies where diagnosis is not a clinical challenge, leaving more difficult problems underexplored, such as detecting Bankart lesions (anterior-inferior glenoid labral tears) on standard MRIs. Diagnosing these lesions is challenging due to their subtle imaging features, often leading to reliance on invasive MRI arthrograms (MRAs). This study introduces ScopeMRI, the first publicly available, expert-annotated dataset for shoulder pathologies, and presents a deep learning (DL) framework for detecting Bankart lesions on both standard MRIs and MRAs. ScopeMRI includes 586 shoulder MRIs (335 standard, 251 MRAs) from 558 patients who underwent arthroscopy. Ground truth labels were derived from intraoperative findings, the gold standard for diagnosis. Separate DL models for MRAs and standard MRIs were trained using a combination of CNNs and transformers. Predictions from sagittal, axial, and coronal views were ensembled to optimize performance. The models were evaluated on a 20% hold-out test set (117 MRIs: 46 MRAs, 71 standard MRIs). The models achieved an AUC of 0.91 and 0.93, sensitivity of 83% and 94%, and specificity of 91% and 86% for standard MRIs and MRAs, respectively. Notably, model performance on non-invasive standard MRIs matched or surpassed radiologists interpreting MRAs. External validation demonstrated initial generalizability across imaging protocols. This study demonstrates that DL models can achieve radiologist-level diagnostic performance on standard MRIs, reducing the need for invasive MRAs. By releasing ScopeMRI and a modular codebase for training and evaluating deep learning models on 3D medical imaging data, we aim to accelerate research in musculoskeletal imaging and support the development of new datasets for clinically challenging diagnostic tasks.
- **Summary**: **Summary:** The paper titled "SCOPE-MRI: Bankart Lesion Detection as a Case Study in Data Curation and Deep Learning for Challenging Diagnoses" investigates the application of deep learning in the detection of Bankart lesions from MRIs. The study introduces ScopeMRI, a first-of-its-kind publicly available dataset containing 586 shoulder MRIs, which is annotated by experts to aid in the diagnosis of these subtle pathologies, typically detected through more invasive MRI arthrograms. By employing deep learning models, including convolutional neural networks (CNNs) and transformers, the authors developed diagnostic frameworks that were trained on both standard MRIs and MRAs, achieving impressive performance metrics with AUCs of 0.91 and 0.93, and sensitivity and specificity values nearing or surpassing those of expert radiologists. The results suggest that deep learning can effectively reduce the need for invasive procedures while maintaining high diagnostic accuracy. The paper contributes to the field by providing not only a novel dataset but also a structured approach to enhance deep learning models for musculoskeletal imaging tasks. **Critical Evaluation:** 1. **Novelty:**    The introduction of the ScopeMRI dataset is a significant advancement, as it is specifically curated for a clinically challenging diagnosis with previously limited resources. The focus on Bankart lesions, which involve subtle imaging characteristics often leading to diagnostic difficulties, adds a unique element, distinguishing the work from prior research which largely emphasizes more straightforward diagnoses in musculoskeletal imaging. 2. **Methodology:**    The use of state-of-the-art deep learning techniques, including CNNs and transformers, demonstrates the authors' commitment to leveraging recent advancements in machine learning. The rigorous method of combining predictions from multiple imaging perspectives (sagittal, axial, coronal) to enhance model performance also reflects a thoughtful and systematic approach to model development. 3. **Impact:**    By providing an AUC comparable to that of trained radiologists, the findings suggest that developing AI tools for identifying complex conditions may evolve clinical practices. This is particularly impactful considering diagnostic procedures such as MRAs can be invasive and costly. The promise of reduced reliance on invasive imaging could lead to better patient outcomes and more efficient healthcare delivery. 4. **Strengths:**    The study's strength lies in its comprehensive data sourcing, robust methodology, and the clear application of the findings toward a challenging clinical problem. The external validation steps indicate a commitment to generalizability, which is crucial for widespread adoption. 5. **Weaknesses:**    Despite the impressive results, the study could benefit from a more extensive discussion on limitations, including the potential biases in the dataset, the diversity of imaging protocols, and the need for further validation across different populations and settings. Additionally, while the initial results are promising, long-term clinical outcomes and implications for practice remain to be fully assessed. 6. **Potential Influence:**    The combination of providing a new dataset and a strong methodological framework positions this paper to influence both academic research and clinical practices. However, the broader impact will depend on continued validation studies and incorporation into clinical workflows. **Score: 8** The score of 8 reflects a robust contribution to the field with meaningful novelty and potential for significant impact. The creation of ScopeMRI and the demonstrated capabilities of deep learning in this challenging diagnostic context mark an important step towards enhancing diagnosis in musculoskeletal imaging. However, the paper would benefit from addressing potential limitations and further validating findings across diverse clinical settings to substantiate its claims more fully.
- **Classification**: eess.IV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Skill Discovery for Software Scripting Automation via Offline Simulations with LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20406v1)
- **Authors**: Paiheng Xu, Gang Wu, Xiang Chen, Tong Yu, Chang Xiao, Franck Dernoncourt, Tianyi Zhou, Wei Ai, Viswanathan Swaminathan
- **Abstract**: Scripting interfaces enable users to automate tasks and customize software workflows, but creating scripts traditionally requires programming expertise and familiarity with specific APIs, posing barriers for many users. While Large Language Models (LLMs) can generate code from natural language queries, runtime code generation is severely limited due to unverified code, security risks, longer response times, and higher computational costs. To bridge the gap, we propose an offline simulation framework to curate a software-specific skillset, a collection of verified scripts, by exploiting LLMs and publicly available scripting guides. Our framework comprises two components: (1) task creation, using top-down functionality guidance and bottom-up API synergy exploration to generate helpful tasks; and (2) skill generation with trials, refining and validating scripts based on execution feedback. To efficiently navigate the extensive API landscape, we introduce a Graph Neural Network (GNN)-based link prediction model to capture API synergy, enabling the generation of skills involving underutilized APIs and expanding the skillset's diversity. Experiments with Adobe Illustrator demonstrate that our framework significantly improves automation success rates, reduces response time, and saves runtime token costs compared to traditional runtime code generation. This is the first attempt to use software scripting interfaces as a testbed for LLM-based systems, highlighting the advantages of leveraging execution feedback in a controlled environment and offering valuable insights into aligning AI capabilities with user needs in specialized software domains.
- **Summary**: **Summary:** The paper presents a novel offline simulation framework aimed at improving the automation of software scripting through the use of Large Language Models (LLMs). Traditional scripting requires programming knowledge, which can be a barrier for many users. The proposed framework addresses this challenge by curating a verified skillset of software-specific scripts. It consists of two key parts: (1) task creation, which involves generating useful automation tasks by exploring API synergies and functionality, and (2) skill generation through trials, where scripts are refined using execution feedback. A Graph Neural Network (GNN)-based model aids in identifying connections between APIs to enhance the diversity of the skillset, particularly tapping into less commonly used APIs. Experiments with Adobe Illustrator illustrate that this approach leads to higher success rates, faster response times, and reduced costs compared to conventional methods, underscoring the benefits of executing feedback in a controlled environment for aligning AI tools with user needs in specialized software. **Evaluation:** **Novelty and Significance:**  This paper demonstrates a significant advancement in the field of software automation, particularly as it combines LLMs with controlled offline simulations. By proposing an approach that not only generates scripts but also validates them through execution feedback, it tackles a critical limitation in automated scripting solutions, where real-time execution often leads to unverified and insecure code.  **Strengths:** 1. **Innovation in Framework Design:** The offline simulation framework enhances the potential of LLMs by curating a verified skillset, addressing safety and efficiency issues prevalent in traditional runtime generation. 2. **API Exploration Methodology:** The use of a GNN-based model to predict API synergies is a compelling contribution that expands the versatility of generated scripts, showcasing a sophisticated understanding of the software landscape. 3. **Empirical Validation:** The use of a real-world application (Adobe Illustrator) provides concrete evidence for the effectiveness of the proposed framework, which can inspire further research and development. **Weaknesses:** 1. **Generalization Beyond Adobe Illustrator:** While the experiments yield promising results in one specific software application, the framework's applicability to other platforms and programming environments remains to be fully explored. 2. **Execution Feedback Limitations:** The reliance on execution feedback raises questions about the scalability of this approach, especially in complex software environments where feedback loops may be lengthy or difficult to implement. 3. **Comparative Analysis Scarcity:** While the paper claims improvements over traditional methods, a more in-depth comparative analysis with existing solutions could enhance the understanding of its unique contributions. **Potential Influence in the Field:**  The integration of LLMs with offline simulations could influence how automation tools are developed in various software domains. The approach opens avenues for future research in leveraging AI to bridge skill gaps for non-expert users, potentially transforming the landscape of software automation. Given these considerations, I assign this paper a score of **8**. The paper presents a well-structured approach with significant innovation and practical implications, though there remains potential for broader applications and deeper empirical comparisons.  **Score: 8**
- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 8/10

### ADiff4TPP: Asynchronous Diffusion Models for Temporal Point Processes
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20411v1)
- **Authors**: Amartya Mukherjee, Ruizhi Deng, He Zhao, Yuzhen Mao, Leonid Sigal, Frederick Tung
- **Abstract**: This work introduces a novel approach to modeling temporal point processes using diffusion models with an asynchronous noise schedule. At each step of the diffusion process, the noise schedule injects noise of varying scales into different parts of the data. With a careful design of the noise schedules, earlier events are generated faster than later ones, thus providing stronger conditioning for forecasting the more distant future. We derive an objective to effectively train these models for a general family of noise schedules based on conditional flow matching. Our method models the joint distribution of the latent representations of events in a sequence and achieves state-of-the-art results in predicting both the next inter-event time and event type on benchmark datasets. Additionally, it flexibly accommodates varying lengths of observation and prediction windows in different forecasting settings by adjusting the starting and ending points of the generation process. Finally, our method shows superior performance in long-horizon prediction tasks, outperforming existing baseline methods.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "ADiff4TPP: Asynchronous Diffusion Models for Temporal Point Processes":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel approach, ADiff4TPP, for modeling temporal point processes (TPPs) using asynchronous diffusion models.  Traditional diffusion models often operate on synchronous data, which is not ideal for TPPs where events occur irregularly.  ADiff4TPP addresses this by using an asynchronous noise schedule that diffuses each event in a sequence separately and provides flexible control over the prediction window length. The model leverages flow matching and a transformer architecture for future event prediction and demonstrates superior performance on several TPP benchmarks for both next-event prediction and long-horizon forecasting. The key idea is to generate better latent representations of TPP sequences using asynchronous noise schedules and carefully designed diffusion steps.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The asynchronous diffusion approach is a notable contribution. Adapting diffusion models for TPPs is not entirely new (several prior works have explored DMs for sequence data), but the asynchronous aspect is novel and addresses a core limitation of applying synchronous DMs to irregularly spaced event data. The use of asynchronous noise schedules to diffuse each event individually, which makes the model more flexible and adaptable for TPP modeling, is a key strength.

*   **Significance:** The paper demonstrates compelling empirical results, achieving state-of-the-art performance on several benchmark datasets. This empirical validation is a significant strength.  The improvements over existing methods suggest that asynchronous diffusion is a promising direction for TPP modeling. The code release is also helpful for reproducibility and further research.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Asynchronous Diffusion:** The core contribution of asynchronous diffusion is well-motivated and addresses a genuine challenge in applying diffusion models to TPPs.
    *   **Empirical Validation:**  Strong empirical results across multiple datasets and forecasting horizons demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed approach.
    *   **Clear Presentation:**  The paper is generally well-written and the methodology is clearly explained.
    *   **Code Availability:** Releasing the code promotes reproducibility and further development in the field.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Computational Cost:** Diffusion models, in general, are computationally expensive. The paper does not deeply analyze the computational complexity of ADiff4TPP compared to other TPP models. It would be beneficial to have a detailed analysis of the computational cost and provide optimizations for scalability, especially for long sequences.
    *   **Ablation Depth:** While the ablation studies are performed, some of the ablations are not as thorough as they could be. Further dissection of which components contribute most to the performance gains would improve the paper.
    *   **Theoretical Justification:** Although the paper provides a rationale for using asynchronous diffusion, a more in-depth theoretical analysis of why it works better for TPPs would be beneficial. Connecting the approach to the underlying properties of point processes (e.g., conditional intensity functions) could strengthen the paper.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has good potential to influence the field. The asynchronous diffusion concept could be adopted and extended by other researchers working on TPPs. The improved performance on benchmarks is likely to attract attention and encourage further investigation of diffusion models for event sequence modeling.

**Overall:**

The paper introduces a novel and effective approach for modeling TPPs using asynchronous diffusion models. The empirical results are compelling, and the code release is a valuable resource. While some aspects of the paper could be strengthened, the core contribution is significant and has the potential to advance the state of the art in TPP modeling.
Score: 7.  The novelty of the asynchronous diffusion scheme and the impressive empirical results warrant a relatively high score. However, the weaknesses related to computational cost analysis and greater theoretical depth prevent it from reaching a higher rating.
- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### CrashFixer: A crash resolution agent for the Linux kernel
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20412v1)
- **Authors**: Alex Mathai, Chenxi Huang, Suwei Ma, Jihwan Kim, Hailie Mitchell, Aleksandr Nogikh, Petros Maniatis, Franjo Ivančić, Junfeng Yang, Baishakhi Ray
- **Abstract**: Code large language models (LLMs) have shown impressive capabilities on a multitude of software engineering tasks. In particular, they have demonstrated remarkable utility in the task of code repair. However, common benchmarks used to evaluate the performance of code LLMs are often limited to small-scale settings. In this work, we build upon kGym, which shares a benchmark for system-level Linux kernel bugs and a platform to run experiments on the Linux kernel. This paper introduces CrashFixer, the first LLM-based software repair agent that is applicable to Linux kernel bugs. Inspired by the typical workflow of a kernel developer, we identify the key capabilities an expert developer leverages to resolve a kernel crash. Using this as our guide, we revisit the kGym platform and identify key system improvements needed to practically run LLM-based agents at the scale of the Linux kernel (50K files and 20M lines of code). We implement these changes by extending kGym to create an improved platform - called kGymSuite, which will be open-sourced. Finally, the paper presents an evaluation of various repair strategies for such complex kernel bugs and showcases the value of explicitly generating a hypothesis before attempting to fix bugs in complex systems such as the Linux kernel. We also evaluated CrashFixer's capabilities on still open bugs, and found at least two patch suggestions considered plausible to resolve the reported bug.
- **Summary**: ### Summary of the Paper The paper titled "CrashFixer: A crash resolution agent for the Linux kernel" presents a novel approach to utilizing large language models (LLMs) for software repair, specifically targeting bugs in the Linux kernel. The authors enhance the existing kGym platform to create kGymSuite, facilitating the evaluation of LLMs on system-level kernel bugs across a vast codebase of 50K files and 20M lines of code. The paper identifies essential capabilities for resolving kernel crashes, inspired by expert developer workflows, and emphasizes the importance of generating hypotheses prior to bug fixing. The evaluation demonstrates the effectiveness of various repair strategies, with preliminary results showing plausible patch suggestions for unresolved Linux kernel bugs. ### Evaluation of Novelty and Significance **Strengths:** 1. **Innovative Application of LLMs:** The paper explores the integration of LLMs into a domain—system-level kernel bugs—that is complex and traditionally challenging for automated tools. This focus on real-world, large-scale software systems significantly extends research on code repair. 2. **Benchmark Development:** By addressing the limitations of existing benchmarks and creating kGymSuite, the authors provide a valuable resource that may facilitate further research within the field. 3. **Practical Relevance:** The identification of real-world kernel developer workflows adds practical relevance to the study, making it an important contribution not only academically but also for practitioners in software maintenance. 4. **Initial Success with Real Bugs:** The evaluation's findings regarding plausible patches for existing bugs illustrate the practical potential of the proposed solution, highlighting its importance in the ongoing effort to automate software repair. **Weaknesses:** 1. **Limited Validation of Results:** While the paper presents initial evaluations, the early-stage nature of the results means that further validation and broader experimentation are necessary to establish the robustness and applicability of CrashFixer in diverse scenarios. 2. **Comparative Analysis:** There is an absence of comprehensive comparisons with existing state-of-the-art tools and techniques for resolving kernel bugs, which may undermine the perceived effectiveness of CrashFixer. 3. **Scalability and Performance Concerns:** The paper does not sufficiently address potential performance issues that LLMs might encounter when scaling to the vast codebase of the Linux kernel. Practical implementation challenges for real-time applications remain unexamined. **Potential Influence:** The findings could significantly influence future work in software repair, particularly within the context of operating systems and critical system software, leading to improved bug-fixing tools and methodologies. ### Score Given the strengths of the proposed framework for addressing complex bugs and its potential for practical application, while recognizing the need for further validation and comparative analysis, I assign a score of **8**. This score reflects a notable advancement in the field of software engineering that merits recognition but acknowledges areas needing development for greater impact. **Score: 8**
- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 8/10

### Enhancing Leakage Attacks on Searchable Symmetric Encryption Using LLM-Based Synthetic Data Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20414v1)
- **Authors**: Joshua Chiu, Partha Protim Paul, Zahin Wahab
- **Abstract**: Searchable Symmetric Encryption (SSE) enables efficient search capabilities over encrypted data, allowing users to maintain privacy while utilizing cloud storage. However, SSE schemes are vulnerable to leakage attacks that exploit access patterns, search frequency, and volume information. Existing studies frequently assume that adversaries possess a substantial fraction of the encrypted dataset to mount effective inference attacks, implying there is a database leakage of such documents, thus, an assumption that may not hold in real-world scenarios. In this work, we investigate the feasibility of enhancing leakage attacks under a more realistic threat model in which adversaries have access to minimal leaked data. We propose a novel approach that leverages large language models (LLMs), specifically GPT-4 variants, to generate synthetic documents that statistically and semantically resemble the real-world dataset of Enron emails. Using the email corpus as a case study, we evaluate the effectiveness of synthetic data generated via random sampling and hierarchical clustering methods on the performance of the SAP (Search Access Pattern) keyword inference attack restricted to token volumes only. Our results demonstrate that, while the choice of LLM has limited effect, increasing dataset size and employing clustering-based generation significantly improve attack accuracy, achieving comparable performance to attacks using larger amounts of real data. We highlight the growing relevance of LLMs in adversarial contexts.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores enhancing leakage attacks on searchable symmetric encryption (SSE) by using Large Language Models (LLMs) to generate synthetic data. The core idea is to improve the attacker's ability to infer information about the encrypted data, even when the attacker has limited access to real leaked data. The authors use GPT-4 variants to generate synthetic documents resembling the Enron email corpus and evaluate the performance of a Search Access Pattern (SAP) keyword inference attack.  They compare different approaches to synthetic data generation: using only real data, augmenting with randomly generated synthetic data, and augmenting with hierarchically clustered synthetic data. Their results suggest that increasing the dataset size and using clustering-based generation significantly improve attack accuracy, approaching the performance of attacks using more real data. They find that switching between different LLM variants has a limited effect.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The application of LLMs to enhance *attacks* on SSE is a relatively novel area, although LLMs have been used in security contexts before (e.g., for code vulnerability analysis or generating adversarial examples in other domains). The paper's novelty lies in specifically exploring how LLMs can be used to improve the attacker's capability in SSE scenarios when real data leakage is minimal. The idea of using synthetic data to approximate real data distributions for attack augmentation is insightful. However, similar ideas might exist in other security contexts.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the work stems from its practical implications. In real-world scenarios, attackers rarely have access to large amounts of leaked data. The paper addresses this realistic threat model by demonstrating how LLMs can bridge the gap and enhance the effectiveness of leakage attacks, even with limited real data. This highlights the need for stronger defenses against such attacks, considering the evolving capabilities of LLMs. This is significant because it shows that existing defenses built on assumptions about the amount of data an attacker possesses may be insufficient.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and motivation (realistic threat model).
    *   Well-defined methodology with clear descriptions of the dataset, data sanitization, data selection, and LLM models used.
    *   Comparison of different synthetic data generation approaches (random vs. clustered).
    *   Use of Jaccardian similarity as an objective evaluation metric.
    *   Statistical analysis (Wilcoxon signed-rank test and t-tests) to validate the results.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The evaluation is limited to the Enron email dataset. While Enron is a common benchmark, the generalizability of the results to other types of data (e.g., medical records, financial transactions) is not addressed.
    *   The choice of the SAP attack model might limit the scope of the findings. While SAP is relevant, other attack models exploiting different types of leakage (e.g., query pattern leakage) could have been considered.
    *   The cost constraints limiting experimentation across all ratios are a significant limitation.
    *   Model selection is confined to GPT-40 and GPT-40-mini. Exploring performance of other models like Llama or PaLM is recommended.
    *   The threat of data contamination is mentioned, but not fully addressed.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper raises awareness about the increasing relevance of LLMs in adversarial contexts within the field of searchable encryption.  It encourages researchers to explore more sophisticated attack models that leverage LLMs and to develop corresponding defenses. The focus on minimal data leakage scenarios is particularly important for real-world applicability.

*   **Areas for Improvement:**
    *   Expanding the evaluation to include more diverse datasets.
    *   Exploring different SSE attack models and leakage patterns.
    *   Investigating the impact of different LLM architectures and training paradigms.
    *   Developing defenses against LLM-enhanced leakage attacks.

**Score: 6**

**Rationale:**

The paper demonstrates a novel application of LLMs for enhancing attacks on SSE. While not groundbreaking, it addresses a relevant and realistic threat model, showing that LLMs can significantly improve attack effectiveness even when the attacker has limited real data. The use of clustered synthetic data is a notable contribution. However, the evaluation is limited by the reliance on a single dataset, the specific SAP attack model, and the limited exploration of different LLMs and their configurations. These limitations prevent the paper from achieving a higher score. The paper's impact is primarily in highlighting the potential of LLMs as attack amplifiers, motivating further research in this direction. However, the paper does not have a substantial theoretical contribution. Given those limitations and strengths, a score of 6 is appropriate.

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 6/10

### Plant Disease Detection through Multimodal Large Language Models and Convolutional Neural Networks
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20419v1)
- **Authors**: Konstantinos I. Roumeliotis, Ranjan Sapkota, Manoj Karkee, Nikolaos D. Tselikas, Dimitrios K. Nasiopoulos
- **Abstract**: Automation in agriculture plays a vital role in addressing challenges related to crop monitoring and disease management, particularly through early detection systems. This study investigates the effectiveness of combining multimodal Large Language Models (LLMs), specifically GPT-4o, with Convolutional Neural Networks (CNNs) for automated plant disease classification using leaf imagery. Leveraging the PlantVillage dataset, we systematically evaluate model performance across zero-shot, few-shot, and progressive fine-tuning scenarios. A comparative analysis between GPT-4o and the widely used ResNet-50 model was conducted across three resolutions (100, 150, and 256 pixels) and two plant species (apple and corn). Results indicate that fine-tuned GPT-4o models achieved slightly better performance compared to the performance of ResNet-50, achieving up to 98.12% classification accuracy on apple leaf images, compared to 96.88% achieved by ResNet-50, with improved generalization and near-zero training loss. However, zero-shot performance of GPT-4o was significantly lower, underscoring the need for minimal training. Additional evaluations on cross-resolution and cross-plant generalization revealed the models' adaptability and limitations when applied to new domains. The findings highlight the promise of integrating multimodal LLMs into automated disease detection pipelines, enhancing the scalability and intelligence of precision agriculture systems while reducing the dependence on large, labeled datasets and high-resolution sensor infrastructure. Large Language Models, Vision Language Models, LLMs and CNNs, Disease Detection with Vision Language Models, VLMs
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the application of Multimodal Large Language Models (LLMs), specifically GPT-4, and Convolutional Neural Networks (CNNs) for plant disease detection using a combination of visual data (plant leaf images) and textual information.  The authors fine-tune GPT-4 and ResNet-50 models on the PlantVillage dataset using different resolutions and few-shot learning techniques. The study aims to assess the potential of multimodal LLMs to improve accuracy, efficiency, and reduce the need for extensive expert annotations in plant disease identification. The paper explores different fine-tuning strategies, hyperparameters, and assesses performance across various plant types and resolutions.  The researchers compare the performance of LLMs with CNNs, analyzing computational cost, training time, and classification accuracy.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Novel Approach:** Exploring multimodal LLMs like GPT-4 for plant disease detection is a relatively novel approach. While there is work on deep learning in agriculture, the use of LLMs to explicitly integrate both image analysis and textual descriptions/metadata is interesting.
*   **Comprehensive Analysis:** The paper provides a thorough analysis by comparing LLM performance with CNNs (ResNet-50), varying image resolutions, and employing different fine-tuning strategies.  The experimental setup seems well-designed to evaluate the capabilities and limitations of the proposed methods.
*   **Practical Relevance:**  The application of AI in agriculture has real-world potential to improve crop yield and reduce costs. The paper's focus on reducing expert annotation requirements is a significant step towards making such systems more accessible.
*   **Detailed Evaluation Metrics:**  The use of various metrics (accuracy, recall, precision, F1-score) and cost analysis offers a well-rounded evaluation.
*   **Open Source Implementation:** Making the code and data publicly available enhances reproducibility and further research.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Multimodality Depth:** The multimodality is somewhat shallow. The paper primarily leverages GPT-4 as an image classifier with an API call. Deeper integration, leveraging the generative capabilities of LLMs to augment datasets or generate more informative feature representations, is missing. A richer multimodal approach, truly leveraging the LLM's natural language understanding, would be more impactful.
*   **Dataset Limitations:** Using PlantVillage, while a good starting point, is somewhat limiting. The images are relatively clean and controlled. Real-world scenarios involve noisy data, varying lighting, and complex backgrounds. The paper does not sufficiently address robustness to such conditions.
*   **Incremental Improvement over CNNs:** The results suggest that LLMs don't always significantly outperform CNNs, especially considering the computational overhead. While there are some advantages, the improvement often appears marginal. The paper should address the trade-off more explicitly.
*   **Concerns about OpenAI's Content Detection:** The paper mentions issues with OpenAI's content detection mechanism. This raises a serious concern about the reliability and reproducibility of the method, as the model's behavior can be inconsistent based on OpenAI's filters. This is a major hurdle.
*   **Overfitting and Data leakage Concerns:** The paper mentions that OpenAI´s image validation algorithms led to overfitting and other issues. Further research in this area would strengthen this work.

**Significance:**

The paper has some significance by exploring a novel approach, but its impact is somewhat limited by the aforementioned weaknesses. While the initial results are promising, there are concerns with over fitting and data leakage.  The benefits of LLMs may not outweigh the increased computational complexity when compared to CNNs.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the strengths and weaknesses, I assign the paper a score of **6**.

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a reasonably novel application of LLMs for plant disease detection. The experimental design is rigorous, and the evaluation is comprehensive. However, the paper falls short in several areas: the limited depth of multimodality, the dataset's constraints, and the questionable utility of LLMs in this domain given potential benefits does not always outweigh the disadvantages in some cases. The paper is not bad, it just would need to be addressed more thoroughly in order to provide a more insightful finding that would be valuable in both the machine learning and agricultural spaces.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### RV-Syn: Rational and Verifiable Mathematical Reasoning Data Synthesis based on Structured Function Library
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20426v1)
- **Authors**: Jiapeng Wang, Jinhao Jiang, Zhiqiang Zhang, Jun Zhou, Wayne Xin Zhao
- **Abstract**: The advancement of reasoning capabilities in Large Language Models (LLMs) requires substantial amounts of high-quality reasoning data, particularly in mathematics. Existing data synthesis methods, such as data augmentation from annotated training sets or direct question generation based on relevant knowledge points and documents, have expanded datasets but face challenges in mastering the inner logic of the problem during generation and ensuring the verifiability of the solutions. To address these issues, we propose RV-Syn, a novel Rational and Verifiable mathematical Synthesis approach. RV-Syn constructs a structured mathematical operation function library based on initial seed problems and generates computational graphs as solutions by combining Python-formatted functions from this library. These graphs are then back-translated into complex problems. Based on the constructed computation graph, we achieve solution-guided logic-aware problem generation. Furthermore, the executability of the computational graph ensures the verifiability of the solving process. Experimental results show that RV-Syn surpasses existing synthesis methods, including those involving human-generated problems, achieving greater efficient data scaling. This approach provides a scalable framework for generating high-quality reasoning datasets.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and a critical evaluation of the RV-Syn paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces RV-Syn, a novel approach for synthesizing high-quality mathematical reasoning data for training Large Language Models (LLMs). RV-Syn addresses limitations in existing data synthesis methods, such as a lack of control over the reasoning process and difficulty in verifying solution correctness. The method constructs a structured mathematical function library from existing seed problems by extracting code-based solutions and organizing them into a graph. New computational graphs are then generated by sampling and combining functions from this library, ensuring solution correctness due to the executability of the graphs. These graphs are subsequently translated back into complex mathematical problems. Experimental results show that RV-Syn outperforms existing synthesis methods, including those involving human-generated problems, and allows for efficient data scaling. The paper claims that the new approach improves the inner logic of problem generation and better ensures verifiability of solutions, leading to more effective LLM training.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's central contribution – the structured function library and graph-based synthesis approach – has some degree of novelty, though the underlying concepts are not entirely new. Existing work has explored code-based approaches and knowledge graphs in various contexts. The innovative aspect lies in the specific application of these techniques to the *mathematical reasoning data synthesis* problem, particularly focusing on verifiable solutions. The modular and graph-based approach allows for better controlled reasoning process generation. A weakness lies in the fact that the initial function library relies on seed problems and their associated code-based solutions, thus inherently limiting the diversity of functions from the very beginning. Also, while the function merging and node connecting methods aid in better graph building, they might lead to issues down the line. The authors could have looked at the possibility of expanding the library with newly generated mathematical functions to truly allow for a diverse structure, as well as making the library completely independent of previous problems.
* **Significance:** The work addresses a critical bottleneck in LLM development: the scarcity of high-quality reasoning data. The ability to automatically synthesize verifiable and diverse mathematical problems is a significant contribution that could accelerate progress in mathematical reasoning for LLMs. The paper attempts to bring a level of rigor from education to LLM training by carefully curating mathematical skills to build problems step-by-step. The experimental results indicating improved scaling and performance compared to existing methods bolster the significance of the work.
* **Strengths:**
    *   **Solution-Guided Logic-Aware Generation:** This is a strong point. The problem generation is driven by a logically consistent solution process, leading to more coherent and verifiable problems.
    *   **Graph-based Synthesis:** The function library and graph representation enable a more structured and controlled synthesis process.
    *   **Verifiability:** The method provides a mechanism to ensure the correctness of the synthesized data, addressing a key limitation of many existing approaches.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The experimental results support the claims of improved performance and scaling.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Dependency on Initial Seed Problems:** As previously mentioned, the synthesis approach is bounded by the seed problems and function library, which may limit the diversity of the generated data and require a large enough seed to not limit the function pool from the start.
    *   **Generalization to Other Domains:** The paper primarily focuses on mathematical reasoning. While the approach may be applicable to other domains, this is not explored in detail. The experiments are solely focused on math problems, but no further information is given about the versatility of this algorithm. The lack of discussion of limitations or challenges in adapting it to other reasoning tasks is also a weakness.
    *   **Lack of Function Generation:** Instead of purely relying on previous function types, the model needs to have the functionality to create new types of operations that can further expand the scope of operations.
    *   **LLM Biases & Hallucinations:** The prompts heavily rely on existing LLMs. While authors mention that the LLM is used to generate *correct* problems, the reliance on a powerful LLM for core components such as problem and function labeling introduces biases that need to be discussed and addressed.

* **Potential Impact:** The RV-Syn method has the potential to significantly impact the field by providing a more scalable and reliable framework for generating reasoning data. This could lead to the development of more capable and robust LLMs for mathematical problem-solving. Furthermore, the approach could inspire new data synthesis techniques in other domains.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the strengths and weaknesses, I assign a score of 7. The core idea of graph-based, solution-guided synthesis is a valuable contribution, addressing key limitations in the field. The improved scalability and performance demonstrated are significant. However, the reliance on seed problems and a lack of broader evaluation, LLM reliance, and discussion of biases hold back the paper from a higher score. While this does provide better synthetic data generation, other researchers need to be aware of the inherent biases when relying on proprietary models. It is also important to consider whether the novelty is truly *novel* and not a variation of existing techniques that use knowledge graphs. Although, the paper's focus on verifiable synthesis and detailed performance is promising.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Learning Laplacian Positional Encodings for Heterophilous Graphs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20430v1)
- **Authors**: Michael Ito, Jiong Zhu, Dexiong Chen, Danai Koutra, Jenna Wiens
- **Abstract**: In this work, we theoretically demonstrate that current graph positional encodings (PEs) are not beneficial and could potentially hurt performance in tasks involving heterophilous graphs, where nodes that are close tend to have different labels. This limitation is critical as many real-world networks exhibit heterophily, and even highly homophilous graphs can contain local regions of strong heterophily. To address this limitation, we propose Learnable Laplacian Positional Encodings (LLPE), a new PE that leverages the full spectrum of the graph Laplacian, enabling them to capture graph structure on both homophilous and heterophilous graphs. Theoretically, we prove LLPE's ability to approximate a general class of graph distances and demonstrate its generalization properties. Empirically, our evaluation on 12 benchmarks demonstrates that LLPE improves accuracy across a variety of GNNs, including graph transformers, by up to 35% and 14% on synthetic and real-world graphs, respectively. Going forward, our work represents a significant step towards developing PEs that effectively capture complex structures in heterophilous graphs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Learning Laplacian Positional Encodings for Heterophilous Graphs" addresses the challenge of effectively representing node positions in graphs, especially in heterophilous graphs where nodes tend to connect to nodes with different labels.  Current positional encodings (PEs) often struggle in such scenarios. The authors introduce a novel positional encoding called Learnable Laplacian Positional Encoding (LLPE). LLPE leverages the full spectrum of the graph Laplacian, enabling it to capture both homophily and heterophily. They theoretically prove LLPE's ability to approximate a general class of graph distances and empirically demonstrate its effectiveness across diverse datasets. The results suggest that LLPE improves accuracy in node classification tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper offers a novel positional encoding specifically designed for heterophilous graphs, where existing methods often falter. Leveraging the *full spectrum* of the Laplacian is a key innovation. This is particularly important because many real-world graphs exhibit a mix of homophily and heterophily, and most PEs are either tuned for homophily or are random. The "learnable" component is also a common extension in the PE literature, but the combination of the Laplacian's full spectrum with learnability makes this method stand out.

*   **Significance:** Node positional encoding is a fundamental problem in graph neural networks (GNNs).  Improving PEs, especially for the more realistic (and often more challenging) heterophilous setting, can have a broad impact on the performance of GNNs in various applications. The authors tackle a real problem that hinders the performance of existing methods, and provide a principled theoretical backing for their approach. The experimental results are a crucial component, demonstrating that LLPE works in practice.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Principled Approach:** The LLPE is theoretically well-grounded, with proofs demonstrating its approximation capabilities.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** Experiments show LLPE's effectiveness across a variety of datasets with varying degrees of homophily and heterophily.
    *   **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-written and the method is explained in detail.
    *   **Addresses a Limitation:** The paper directly targets a known limitation of existing positional encodings.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Computational Cost:** Exploiting the full Laplacian spectrum *might* come at a computational cost. Although the authors mentioned this, a more thorough analysis and comparison (e.g., runtime vs. other PEs) would be helpful.  The experimental section does address computational complexity, but in the supplementary material.
    *   **Parameter Sensitivity:** It is possible the performance might be sensitive to the number of eigenvectors used, or how they are combined, requiring some careful hyperparameter tuning.
    *   **Limited Comparison:** While the authors do compare with other position encodings, adding comparison to other methods for handling heterophily in graphs would strengthen the paper.

*   **Potential Influence:** This paper has the potential to significantly impact GNN research. By offering a more effective PE for heterophilous graphs, it could lead to improved performance in a wide range of applications where graphs exhibit complex relationships between nodes. It may inspire further research on positional encodings that adapt to the specific characteristics of the underlying graph structure.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper tackles an important problem with a novel and well-justified solution. The approach is supported by both theoretical analysis and empirical results. While there are some weaknesses related to computational costs and parameter sensitivity, the strengths of the paper outweigh its limitations. It provides a clear improvement over existing methods for heterophilous graphs, and has the potential to influence future research in GNNs.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### ARCS: Agentic Retrieval-Augmented Code Synthesis with Iterative Refinement
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20434v1)
- **Authors**: Manish Bhattarai, Miguel Cordova, Javier Santos, Dan O'Malley
- **Abstract**: In supercomputing, efficient and optimized code generation is essential to leverage high-performance systems effectively. We propose Agentic Retrieval-Augmented Code Synthesis (ARCS), an advanced framework for accurate, robust, and efficient code generation, completion, and translation. ARCS integrates Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) with Chain-of-Thought (CoT) reasoning to systematically break down and iteratively refine complex programming tasks. An agent-based RAG mechanism retrieves relevant code snippets, while real-time execution feedback drives the synthesis of candidate solutions. This process is formalized as a state-action search tree optimization, balancing code correctness with editing efficiency. Evaluations on the Geeks4Geeks and HumanEval benchmarks demonstrate that ARCS significantly outperforms traditional prompting methods in translation and generation quality. By enabling scalable and precise code synthesis, ARCS offers transformative potential for automating and optimizing code development in supercomputing applications, enhancing computational resource utilization.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "ARCS: Agentic Retrieval-Augmented Code Synthesis with Iterative Refinement":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ARCS, a new framework for code synthesis that integrates retrieval-augmented generation (RAG), iterative refinement, and execution-based feedback. The system uses an agentic approach to retrieve relevant code snippets, generate candidate solutions using a large language model (LLM), execute the code in a sandbox environment, and iteratively refine the solution based on the execution feedback. The framework is structured in a hierarchical manner (Small, Medium, and Large tiers) to balance computational efficiency with solution quality. Experimental results on HumanEval, TransCoder, and a custom-built dataset show that ARCS improves code generation and translation quality compared to baseline RAG systems. The paper emphasizes the importance of prompt engineering principles, hierarchical design, and iterative refinement for effective code synthesis.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of combining agentic RAG with iterative refinement and execution feedback for code synthesis is a valuable contribution. While each individual component (RAG, CoT, execution feedback) has been explored separately in prior work, ARCS represents a novel and effective *integration* of these techniques. The formalization of the iterative process as a state-action search is a solid contribution.

*   **Significance:** The paper targets an important problem: improving the accuracy and robustness of code synthesis using LLMs. The gains reported on standard benchmarks like HumanEval and TransCoder are significant and showcase the potential of the approach. The application to a domain-specific (LANL GitHub Corpus) highlights the practical relevance of ARCS for high-performance computing. The results demonstrate that ARCS can transform automated code generation practices and enable precise, robust solutions to complex programming problems.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Well-defined framework:** ARCS provides a structured and modular architecture that can be adapted for different tasks and computational resources.
    *   **Rigorous evaluation:** The paper presents comprehensive experimental results on multiple datasets and evaluates the impact of different components of ARCS.
    *   **Practical relevance:** The framework is applied to a real-world domain (high-performance computing), demonstrating its potential for practical applications.
    *   **Formalization:** The paper models the code synthesis process as a Markov Decision Process.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Lack of direct comparison to all relevant baselines:** While the paper compares ARCS to a RAG baseline, it would be beneficial to see a comparison against other state-of-the-art code synthesis systems, including those that employ iterative refinement or agent-based approaches.
    *   **Potential overcomplication for simple tasks:** As the results indicate, the Large Tier can sometimes lead to unnecessary complexity for straightforward tasks. The automatic difficulty evaluation should be strengthened.
    *   **Dependence on prompt engineering:** The performance of ARCS is highly dependent on the quality of the prompts. While the paper provides guidelines for prompt engineering, more research is needed to develop automated methods for prompt optimization.

*   **Impact:** ARCS has the potential to significantly impact the field of code synthesis and automated software development. It can enable more efficient and reliable code generation for a wide range of applications, from simple scripting tasks to complex scientific simulations. It points towards building more robust and "intelligent" AI-driven code synthesis tools. The system offers transformative potential for automating and optimizing code development, enhancing computational resource utilization.

*   **Justification:**

The paper presents a significant advance in the field of code synthesis. The system combines the best qualities of different approaches, with a theoretical formalization. The claims made are backed up with experiments on solid benchmarks.  While there are some areas that could be improved, the framework's well-defined architecture and strong performance make it a promising approach for a variety of coding tasks.
Considering the strengths, the novelty of integrated architecture, and the significance, the paper merits a high score.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 8/10

### GaLore 2: Large-Scale LLM Pre-Training by Gradient Low-Rank Projection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20437v1)
- **Authors**: DiJia Su, Andrew Gu, Jane Xu, Yuandong Tian, Jiawei Zhao
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) have revolutionized natural language understanding and generation but face significant memory bottlenecks during training. GaLore, Gradient Low-Rank Projection, addresses this issue by leveraging the inherent low-rank structure of weight gradients, enabling substantial memory savings without sacrificing performance. Recent works further extend GaLore from various aspects, including low-bit quantization and higher-order tensor structures. However, there are several remaining challenges for GaLore, such as the computational overhead of SVD for subspace updates and the integration with state-of-the-art training parallelization strategies (e.g., FSDP). In this paper, we present GaLore 2, an efficient and scalable GaLore framework that addresses these challenges and incorporates recent advancements. In addition, we demonstrate the scalability of GaLore 2 by pre-training Llama 7B from scratch using up to 500 billion training tokens, highlighting its potential impact on real LLM pre-training scenarios.
- **Summary**: **Concise Summary:** GaLore 2 addresses the memory constraints associated with training large language models (LLMs) by utilizing a Gradient Low-Rank Projection approach. This method capitalizes on the low-rank characteristics of weight gradients, offering memory efficiency without compromising performance. While previous iterations of GaLore introduced various enhancements, challenges remained concerning the computational burden of singular value decomposition (SVD) during updates and integration with advanced training techniques like Fully Sharded Data Parallel (FSDP). GaLore 2 resolves these issues and successfully demonstrates its scalability and effectiveness by pre-training the Llama 7B model with up to 500 billion tokens, marking a significant advance in the efficiency of LLM pre-training. **Critical Evaluation:** The novelty of GaLore 2 lies in its unique approach to mitigating memory bottlenecks during the training of LLMs through a low-rank representation of gradients. This innovation is significant given the growing size of LLMs and the associated computational resources required, which are often a limiting factor in model training and accessibility. The integration with modern training parallelization strategies further enhances its relevance in contemporary AI research, as efficient training mechanisms are crucial for deploying large-scale models. Strengths of the paper include: - **Innovative Approach:** The use of low-rank gradient projections is a clever way to tackle memory issues inherent in training large models. - **Practical Application:** The successful pre-training of Llama 7B with substantial token counts illustrates the approach's practicality and applicability in real-world scenarios. - **Comprehensive Tackling of Challenges:** It addresses key challenges from the original GaLore implementation, including SVD overhead and compatibility with recent parallelization techniques. Weaknesses include: - **Computational Complexity:** While the paper claims to address computational challenges, the actual performance metrics related to training speed or resource utilization compared to state-of-the-art methods are not detailed. - **Dependence on Specific Architectures:** The efficacy of GaLore 2 may vary depending on the architecture of the LLM being trained, which limits its generalizability. The influence of this paper on the field is expected to be significant, particularly as researchers aim to maximize the efficiency of LLM training. The paper’s contribution to making LLMs more accessible for various applications cannot be understated, particularly in resource-constrained environments. Given these factors, I assign the paper a score of **8**. This score reflects strong novelty and significant impact within the domain, while also acknowledging that more empirical validation and broader applicability could strengthen its overall contribution. **Score: 8**
- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 8/10

### PixelHacker: Image Inpainting with Structural and Semantic Consistency
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20438v1)
- **Authors**: Ziyang Xu, Kangsheng Duan, Xiaolei Shen, Zhifeng Ding, Wenyu Liu, Xiaohu Ruan, Xiaoxin Chen, Xinggang Wang
- **Abstract**: Image inpainting is a fundamental research area between image editing and image generation. Recent state-of-the-art (SOTA) methods have explored novel attention mechanisms, lightweight architectures, and context-aware modeling, demonstrating impressive performance. However, they often struggle with complex structure (e.g., texture, shape, spatial relations) and semantics (e.g., color consistency, object restoration, and logical correctness), leading to artifacts and inappropriate generation. To address this challenge, we design a simple yet effective inpainting paradigm called latent categories guidance, and further propose a diffusion-based model named PixelHacker. Specifically, we first construct a large dataset containing 14 million image-mask pairs by annotating foreground and background (potential 116 and 21 categories, respectively). Then, we encode potential foreground and background representations separately through two fixed-size embeddings, and intermittently inject these features into the denoising process via linear attention. Finally, by pre-training on our dataset and fine-tuning on open-source benchmarks, we obtain PixelHacker. Extensive experiments show that PixelHacker comprehensively outperforms the SOTA on a wide range of datasets (Places2, CelebA-HQ, and FFHQ) and exhibits remarkable consistency in both structure and semantics. Project page at https://hustvl.github.io/projects/PixelHacker.
- **Summary**: ### Summary of the Paper: The paper introduces **PixelHacker**, a novel approach to image inpainting that emphasizes both structural and semantic consistency in the generated images. The authors identify limitations in existing state-of-the-art (SOTA) methods, particularly in handling complex structures and maintaining semantic coherence, which often lead to visible artifacts in the generated outputs.  To confront these challenges, the authors propose the **latent categories guidance** framework, and develop a diffusion-based model, PixelHacker. They compile a substantial dataset of 14 million image-mask pairs that cover potential foreground and background categories, enhancing the model's ability to understand and inpaint images realistically. Through the use of separate embeddings for encoding foreground and background representations and integrating these features into the denoising process, they achieve impressive results. PixelHacker is pretrained on their dataset and then fine-tuned on existing benchmarks. The results indicate it outperforms SOTA methods, demonstrating significant improvements in structural and semantic consistencies across various datasets including Places2, CelebA-HQ, and FFHQ. ### Evaluation of Novelty and Significance: **Strengths:** 1. **Dataset Contribution:** The construction of a large and specifically annotated dataset (14 million pairs) for image inpainting is a significant asset. The scale and attention to foreground and background categorizations provide a robust foundation for training and benchmarking. 2. **Novelty of Approach:** The introduction of latent categories guidance and the innovative use of linear attention in the denoising process is a distinct advancement, addressing critical challenges in the inpainting domain. 3. **Performance Gains:** Empirical results demonstrate that PixelHacker not only matches but exceeds the performance of current leading methods, showing potential utility in practical applications. **Weaknesses:** 1. **Complexity of Explanation:** While the methodology detailing latent categories guidance is novel, the underlying mechanisms may be abstract and challenging for practitioners to implement without thorough explanation. 2. **Generality of Results:** Although the results are impressive across specified datasets, the paper would benefit from discussing generalizability to other domains or types of imagery not included in their benchmarks. 3. **Potential Limitations of Focus:** The paper might be criticized for focusing predominantly on quantitative performance metrics without addressing qualitative assessments of inpainting results, which are critical for user satisfaction. Overall, PixelHacker makes a noteworthy contribution to the field of image inpainting with its innovative methodologies and strong empirical support. However, several opportunities for enhancing clarity and generalizability remain, which may affect its immediate adoption in diverse applications. **Score: 8** This score reflects the substantial advancements presented in the paper while acknowledging areas where clarity and broader applicability could be improved. The innovative methodologies combined with the comprehensive dataset and reported performance gains position this work favorably within the ongoing evolution in image inpainting research, though further validation across various domains would solidify its impact.
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Enhancing News Recommendation with Hierarchical LLM Prompting
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20452v1)
- **Authors**: Hai-Dang Kieu, Delvin Ce Zhang, Minh Duc Nguyen, Min Xu, Qiang Wu, Dung D. Le
- **Abstract**: Personalized news recommendation systems often struggle to effectively capture the complexity of user preferences, as they rely heavily on shallow representations, such as article titles and abstracts. To address this problem, we introduce a novel method, namely PNR-LLM, for Large Language Models for Personalized News Recommendation. Specifically, PNR-LLM harnesses the generation capabilities of LLMs to enrich news titles and abstracts, and consequently improves recommendation quality. PNR-LLM contains a novel module, News Enrichment via LLMs, which generates deeper semantic information and relevant entities from articles, transforming shallow contents into richer representations. We further propose an attention mechanism to aggregate enriched semantic- and entity-level data, forming unified user and news embeddings that reveal a more accurate user-news match. Extensive experiments on MIND datasets show that PNR-LLM outperforms state-of-the-art baselines. Moreover, the proposed data enrichment module is model-agnostic, and we empirically show that applying our proposed module to multiple existing models can further improve their performance, verifying the advantage of our design.
- **Summary**: **Summary of the Paper:** The paper introduces PNR-LLM, a novel approach that utilizes Large Language Models (LLMs) to enhance personalized news recommendation systems. It identifies the limitations of traditional systems that rely on superficial content like titles and abstracts and proposes a new module called News Enrichment via LLMs. This module generates deeper semantic representations and relevant entities from news articles, thus enriching the original content and improving its representation. A unique attention mechanism is employed to effectively aggregate the enriched semantic and entity-level data, resulting in more accurate user-news embeddings. The authors demonstrate through extensive experiments on the MIND dataset that PNR-LLM outperforms current state-of-the-art recommendation systems. Furthermore, the data enrichment module is highlighted as model-agnostic, suggesting its adaptability and potential to enhance performance across various existing models. **Critical Evaluation:** The paper's novelty lies in its introduction of the PNR-LLM method that combines LLMs' generative capabilities with a new attention mechanism for news recommendation.
