This contribution is significant, as it addresses a fundamental challenge in the field: the insufficient capture of user preferences due to reliance on shallow textual representations. By generating enriched semantic information and leveraging attention mechanisms to unify user and news embeddings, the approach appears to present a meaningful advancement in the recommendation system design. Strengths of the paper include: 1. **Innovative Approach**: Using LLMs for content enrichment represents a promising direction for overcoming limitations in existing recommendation systems. 2. **Robust Evaluation**: Extensive experiments conducted on the MIND dataset provide convincing evidence of improved performance over state-of-the-art baselines. 3. **Model-Agnostic Design**: The adaptability of the proposed data enrichment module across various models adds practical value to the contribution. However, there are some weaknesses to consider: 1. **Dependence on LLMs**: The performance improvements are contingent upon the quality and capability of the underlying LLMs. Limitations in LLM performance could impact the overall effectiveness of the recommendation system. 2. **Potential Overfitting**: While the method shows promise in experiments, it remains crucial to evaluate how well it generalizes across different datasets and real-world applications. 3. **Complexity**: The introduction of a hierarchical structure and additional components may introduce complexity that could hinder implementation or scalability in practice. Overall, the paper presents a noteworthy and innovative contribution to the field of personalized news recommendation. Its promise lies in both theoretical advancements and empirical validation. Given these factors, I evaluate the paper's novelty and significance as follows: **Score: 8**  This score reflects a solid contribution, though it falls short of a 10 due to questions surrounding application and scalability, as well as dependency on LLMs' performance. The findings encourage further exploration and adaptation in various recommendation contexts, marking it as an impactful work in ongoing research.
- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 8/10

### Reviving Any-Subset Autoregressive Models with Principled Parallel Sampling and Speculative Decoding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20456v1)
- **Authors**: Gabe Guo, Stefano Ermon
- **Abstract**: In arbitrary-order language models, it is an open question how to sample tokens in parallel from the correct joint distribution. With discrete diffusion models, the more tokens they generate in parallel, the less their predicted distributions adhere to the originally learned data distribution, as they rely on a conditional independence assumption that only works with infinitesimally small timesteps. We find that a different class of models, any-subset autoregressive models (AS-ARMs), holds the solution. As implied by the name, AS-ARMs can generate tokens in any order, and in parallel. Moreover, AS-ARMs support parallelized joint probability density estimation, allowing them to correct their own parallel-generated token distributions, via our Any-Subset Speculative Decoding (ASSD) algorithm. ASSD provably enables generation of tokens from the correct joint distribution, with the number of neural network calls upper bounded by the number of tokens predicted. We empirically verify that ASSD speeds up language generation, without sacrificing quality. Furthermore, we provide a mathematically justified scheme for training AS-ARMs for generation, and show that AS-ARMs achieve state-of-the-art performance among sub-200M parameter models on infilling benchmark tasks, and nearly match the performance of models 50X larger on code generation. Our theoretical and empirical results indicate that the once-forgotten AS-ARMs are a promising direction of language modeling.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Reviving Any-Subset Autoregressive Models with Parallel Sampling and Speculative Decoding":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel approach to accelerate text generation from autoregressive language models (ARMs) called "Any-Subset Speculative Decoding" (ASSD). It focuses on a class of autoregressive models called "Any-Subset Autoregressive Models" (AS-ARMs). These models can be conditioned on arbitrary subsets of the previous tokens, allowing for more flexible parallel token generation.  ASSD leverages this capability by sampling multiple tokens in parallel from the AS-ARM and then uses a standard autoregressive model to verify (or reject) these speculated tokens. By generating and verifying tokens in parallel, the authors aim to speed up the decoding process compared to sequential autoregressive decoding. The paper provides theoretical justification for the approach and demonstrates its effectiveness through experiments on various text generation tasks, showing improvements in decoding speed while maintaining generation quality.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The novelty lies in the specific combination of AS-ARMs with speculative decoding in an any-subset context. While both AS-ARMs and speculative decoding exist, applying the speculative decoding framework to AS-ARMs and enabling parallel sampling from arbitrary subsets of tokens is a clear advance. The "any-subset" aspect is crucial as it allows parallel token generation, which is the key to speedup. The paper is also innovative because it provides a justification based on a more efficient use of the model for generation. By decoupling the generation and verification step, more of the computation that the model did during training can be utilized for generating, and not for just predicting one token at a time in a sequential fashion. The technique allows multiple tokens to be proposed at the same time.

*   **Significance:** The paper is significant because it addresses the critical problem of slow decoding speed in large language models. Improving decoding efficiency is crucial for making these models more practical for real-world applications. If ASSD proves to be widely applicable and scalable to very large models, it could have a substantial impact on the field. The authors demonstrated performance comparable with other autoregressive generation methods. Also, they reduced the computational costs when running the method.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear and well-motivated problem statement.
    *   A theoretically grounded approach. The paper outlines the theoretical basis of the approach clearly.
    *   Solid experimental results. The authors demonstrate the effectiveness of ASSD on several benchmarks.
    *   Detailed experiments, reproducibility details and proofs of properties in the appendix.
    *   The method's general form allows it to be applied to multiple token generation methods, and it does not add to the computational costs of other more expensive methods.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The results indicate the largest speedups are achieved by using models that are of orders of magnitude smaller than current state-of-the-art models.
    *   The paper lacks a comparison with other speculative decoding strategies, which are common practices to accelerate the autoregressive process.
    *   The technique might have limitations in the types of tokens that can be produced, such as the length of speculative token segments it can reliably handle or the complexity of inter-token dependencies.
    *   The implementation of the approach, the exact neural network architecture that enables the "any-subset" functionality, and the details of the training process are not completely spelled out. The paper requires the reader to consult the appendix for more information, and there are references to some of the proofs that need to be checked to see how strong of claims can be made.

*   **Potential Influence:** If the method can be scaled to large models, it could become a standard technique for accelerating text generation. The any-subset autoregressive modeling paradigm could also influence the design of future autoregressive models. The approach is likely to influence other research on improving the efficiency of generative language models.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and potentially significant approach to accelerate text generation from autoregressive language models. The any-subset autoregressive model paradigm and its integration with speculative decoding are promising, and the initial experimental results are encouraging. However, it is uncertain how the performance of the approach scales to very large models, its sensitivity to various hyper-parameters, and the degree to which it will work in the presence of various sampling strategies. While the approach is promising, there are limitations with the experimental set up and model size, and there might be more recent speculative decoding methods to be aware of. The paper is not a complete breakthrough that revolutionizes text generation.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### SAS-Prompt: Large Language Models as Numerical Optimizers for Robot Self-Improvement
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20459v1)
- **Authors**: Heni Ben Amor, Laura Graesser, Atil Iscen, David D'Ambrosio, Saminda Abeyruwan, Alex Bewley, Yifan Zhou, Kamalesh Kalirathinam, Swaroop Mishra, Pannag Sanketi
- **Abstract**: We demonstrate the ability of large language models (LLMs) to perform iterative self-improvement of robot policies. An important insight of this paper is that LLMs have a built-in ability to perform (stochastic) numerical optimization and that this property can be leveraged for explainable robot policy search. Based on this insight, we introduce the SAS Prompt (Summarize, Analyze, Synthesize) -- a single prompt that enables iterative learning and adaptation of robot behavior by combining the LLM's ability to retrieve, reason and optimize over previous robot traces in order to synthesize new, unseen behavior. Our approach can be regarded as an early example of a new family of explainable policy search methods that are entirely implemented within an LLM. We evaluate our approach both in simulation and on a real-robot table tennis task. Project website: sites.google.com/asu.edu/sas-llm/
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "SAS-Prompt" (Summarize, Analyze, Synthesize Prompt), a novel approach for robot self-improvement that leverages the inherent numerical optimization capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs). The core idea is to use a single LLM prompt to (1) summarize previous robot behavior from execution traces, (2) analyze the impact of control parameters on task performance, and (3) synthesize new, unseen behavior by iteratively optimizing these parameters. The method is demonstrated in a robot table tennis task, both in simulation and on a real robot, showing the LLM's ability to translate human targeting instructions into control parameters and improve performance over time without explicit reward functions or external optimization loops.  The authors frame this as a new family of explainable policy search methods fully implemented within an LLM.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper makes a reasonable claim to novelty by demonstrating that LLMs can be used, with suitable prompting, to perform *gradient-free* optimization of robot control parameters directly from execution traces. This is a subtle but important distinction from prior work.  Existing methods either require external optimization loops or rely on explicit demonstrations.  The paper shows that LLMs possess an *emergent* capability for numerical optimization which can be exploited for robot self-improvement, essentially framing the problem as an in-context learning task.  The SAS Prompt itself is a contribution, providing a specific structure for prompting an LLM to achieve self-improvement.

* **Significance:** The significance stems from the potential to simplify robot learning and adaptation. By using LLMs as numerical optimizers, the need for hand-designed reward functions, complex control architectures, or extensive training data can be potentially reduced. The explainability of the approach is also a significant advantage, as the LLM provides natural language justifications for its parameter choices. This can aid in debugging and understanding robot behavior.  The work provides a glimpse into how LLMs can be more tightly integrated into the robot learning loop, going beyond simply high-level task planning and into low-level control parameter optimization.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly defines the problem of robot self-improvement through iterative optimization and positions its approach within the existing literature.
    * **Well-Defined Methodology:** The SAS Prompt is described in sufficient detail, allowing for potential replication.
    * **Empirical Validation:** The paper includes both simulation and real-world experiments, providing evidence for the effectiveness of the proposed approach.
    * **Interesting Results:** The results show that the SAS Prompt can effectively improve robot performance in the table tennis task, demonstrating the LLM's ability to reason about the effect of parameters and synthesize better values.
    * **Explainability:** The LLM's ability to provide natural language justifications for its parameter choices enhances the transparency and understanding of the learning process.
    * **Comparison to optimization baselines:** The comparison of the LLM-based optimization against standard optimizers, without gradient information being passed to the LLM, is an important demonstration of the novel capabilities identified in the paper.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Scope:** The evaluation is primarily focused on a specific table tennis task. While this allows for in-depth analysis, it's important to note that the generalizability of the SAS Prompt to other, more complex robotics tasks is not fully explored.
    * **Reliance on LLM Capabilities:** The performance of the SAS Prompt heavily relies on the reasoning and language understanding capabilities of the underlying LLM.  Different LLMs may yield varying results. The paper doesn't adequately explore the sensitivity of the approach to different LLM architectures or prompt variations.
    * **In-Context Cache Size:** The paper mentions the increasing context window size, but the effect of in-context cache size on performance is not thoroughly discussed or analyzed.  The number of in-context examples is a hyperparameter that needs to be carefully considered and likely has a significant impact on the learning process.
    * **Lack of Theoretical Analysis:** The paper lacks a theoretical analysis of the convergence properties of the proposed approach. While the empirical results are promising, a more formal analysis would strengthen the claims and provide a deeper understanding of the method.

* **Potential Influence:**  The paper has the potential to influence the field by opening up new avenues for robot learning and adaptation using LLMs. It encourages researchers to explore the emergent capabilities of LLMs beyond task planning and consider them as potential numerical optimizers.  The explainable nature of the approach can also lead to the development of more transparent and understandable robot learning systems. The main contribution lies in the demonstrated capability of *gradient-free* optimization that is performed entirely within an LLM.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a novel and interesting approach to robot self-improvement by leveraging the numerical optimization capabilities of LLMs. The empirical results are promising, and the explainability of the approach is a significant advantage. However, the evaluation is limited in scope, and the paper lacks a theoretical analysis of the convergence properties. The reliance on LLM capabilities and in-context cache size also requires further investigation. Nonetheless, the paper has the potential to influence the field by opening up new avenues for robot learning and adaptation using LLMs. The strong comparison against optimization algorithms without the LLM seeing gradient information lends significant support to the conclusions. A higher score would require more in-depth analysis or expansion of the experiments to demonstrate the generality of the results to different robotics tasks or LLM architectures.

- **Classification**: cs.RO
- **Score**: 7/10

### TAMO:Fine-Grained Root Cause Analysis via Tool-Assisted LLM Agent with Multi-Modality Observation Data
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20462v1)
- **Authors**: Qi Wang, Xiao Zhang, Mingyi Li, Yuan Yuan, Mengbai Xiao, Fuzhen Zhuang, Dongxiao Yu
- **Abstract**: With the development of distributed systems, microservices and cloud native technologies have become central to modern enterprise software development. Despite bringing significant advantages, these technologies also increase system complexity and operational challenges. Traditional root cause analysis (RCA) struggles to achieve automated fault response, heavily relying on manual intervention. In recent years, large language models (LLMs) have made breakthroughs in contextual inference and domain knowledge integration, providing new solutions for Artificial Intelligence for Operations (AIOps). However, Existing LLM-based approaches face three key challenges: text input constraints, dynamic service dependency hallucinations, and context window limitations. To address these issues, we propose a tool-assisted LLM agent with multi-modality observation data, namely TAMO, for fine-grained RCA. It unifies multi-modal observational data into time-aligned representations to extract consistent features and employs specialized root cause localization and fault classification tools for perceiving the contextual environment. This approach overcomes the limitations of LLM in handling real-time changing service dependencies and raw observational data and guides LLM to generate repair strategies aligned with system contexts by structuring key information into a prompt. Experimental results show that TAMO performs well in root cause analysis when dealing with public datasets characterized by heterogeneity and common fault types, demonstrating its effectiveness.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces TAMO, a tool-assisted LLM agent designed for fine-grained root cause analysis (RCA) in complex, distributed systems like those built with microservices and cloud-native technologies. TAMO addresses limitations of directly applying LLMs to RCA, namely: text input constraints, dynamic service dependency hallucinations, and context window limitations.  It employs specialized tools: a dual-branch diffusion model for unifying multi-modal observational data (logs, traces, metrics), a root cause localization tool using frequency-domain attention, and a fault classification tool. An LLM agent then integrates the outputs of these tools with system context to provide comprehensive fault analysis and remediation recommendations.  The paper presents experimental results on public datasets, demonstrating TAMO's effectiveness compared to existing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's main contribution lies in the architecture of TAMO and the synergistic approach of integrating specialized tools with an LLM for RCA. The novelty is not in the individual components (e.g., diffusion models, GNNs) *per se*, but in their specific application and orchestration within the TAMO framework to overcome the limitations of using LLMs directly for RCA. Using frequency domain information for causal relationship identification is a strong point. The application of a diffusion model to integrate multimodal data into a unified time-series representation is novel within the context of RCA. The paper convincingly demonstrates how TAMO tackles dynamic dependencies and context limitations that plague existing LLM-based solutions.

*   **Significance:** RCA in distributed systems is a crucial and challenging problem. TAMO's ability to automate and improve the accuracy of fault analysis and remediation has significant practical implications for reducing downtime, minimizing economic losses, and improving system reliability. The method directly integrates with how operators currently perform RCA, thereby demonstrating ease of integration. By decoupling the LLM from raw data and complex dependency modeling, TAMO offers a more scalable and robust solution. The experimental results demonstrate TAMO's superiority over state-of-the-art methods. It provides a clear roadmap for researchers and practitioners looking to leverage LLMs for AIOps. The approach helps to bridge the gap between theoretical LLM capabilities and practical RCA requirements. The paper adequately explores real-world case studies, and justifies their choice of datasets well.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clearly defined problem and limitations of existing LLM-based approaches.
    *   Well-structured and well-motivated TAMO architecture.
    *   Comprehensive experimental evaluation on relevant datasets.
    *   Ablation studies effectively demonstrate the importance of each component.
    *   The use of a real-world case study to validate the practical applicability.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While TAMO addresses context limitations, the LLM agent still faces inherent constraints from its context window. The system context provided in the prompt, though informed by specialized tools, still needs to be limited in size. The paper does not describe if there are ways to handle very large system contexts that exceed the LLM context window, and may introduce an additional pre-processing step.
    *   The reliance on GPT-4 may limit reproducibility and accessibility for some researchers. It would be better if it was possible to use other, more accessible LLMs.
    *   Although the paper integrates various modalities, it should show the effectiveness of TAMO in other modalities as well. For example, audio logs and security logs.
    * The authors could expand upon the limitations of their research, and potential directions for improvement.
    * Given that the approach uses LLMs, the costs associated with operating the system in terms of both compute and potentially API calls to the LLM should be discussed.

*   **Potential Influence:** TAMO has the potential to influence the development of more robust and scalable LLM-based AIOps solutions. It provides a framework for effectively integrating domain-specific tools with LLMs, paving the way for more accurate and automated RCA in complex systems. The dual-branch diffusion model and the frequency-domain attention mechanism are promising techniques that could be adopted in other AIOps tasks. The general architecture can also be adapted to many other areas as well.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a valuable and novel contribution to the field of AIOps by addressing key limitations of existing LLM-based RCA approaches. The TAMO architecture demonstrates a promising way to leverage LLMs for complex system analysis. The experimental results are convincing, and the ablation studies support the design choices. While there are minor weaknesses, the paper offers a significant step forward in automating and improving RCA in distributed systems. Therefore, I believe a score of 8 is warranted.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 8/10

### A Summary on GUI Agents with Foundation Models Enhanced by Reinforcement Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20464v1)
- **Authors**: Jiahao Li, Kaer Huang
- **Abstract**: Graphical User Interface (GUI) agents, driven by Multi-modal Large Language Models (MLLMs), have emerged as a promising paradigm for enabling intelligent interaction with digital systems. This paper provides a structured summary of recent advances in GUI agents, focusing on architectures enhanced by Reinforcement Learning (RL). We first formalize GUI agent tasks as Markov Decision Processes and discuss typical execution environments and evaluation metrics. We then review the modular architecture of (M)LLM-based GUI agents, covering Perception, Planning, and Acting modules, and trace their evolution through representative works. Furthermore, we categorize GUI agent training methodologies into Prompt-based, Supervised Fine-Tuning (SFT)-based, and RL-based approaches, highlighting the progression from simple prompt engineering to dynamic policy learning via RL. Our summary illustrates how recent innovations in multimodal perception, decision reasoning, and adaptive action generation have significantly improved the generalization and robustness of GUI agents in complex real-world environments. We conclude by identifying key challenges and future directions for building more capable and reliable GUI agents.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and critical evaluation of the paper based on the OCR'd text.

**Summary:**

The paper provides a structured overview of recent advancements in GUI (Graphical User Interface) agents, focusing on those that leverage Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) and are enhanced by Reinforcement Learning (RL). It formalizes GUI agent tasks as Markov Decision Processes (MDPs), discusses typical execution environments and evaluation metrics, and reviews the modular architecture of (M)LLM-based GUI agents, covering perception, planning, and acting modules. The paper also categorizes GUI agent training methodologies into Prompt-based, Supervised Fine-Tuning (SFT)-based, and RL-based approaches. Finally, it identifies key challenges and future directions for building more capable and reliable GUI agents, including issues around perception in dynamic/noisy environments, long-horizon planning, data efficiency and generalization, and better evaluation benchmarks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Overview:** The paper provides a relatively comprehensive overview of a rapidly developing field. It covers key aspects, including task formulation, architecture, training methodologies, and challenges.
    *   **Structured Approach:** The paper adopts a well-organized structure (defining problem, methods, challenges), making it easier for readers to understand the key concepts and advancements.
    *   **Highlights Key Trends:** The paper correctly identifies and highlights the shift towards MLLMs and RL for GUI agent development, showcasing how these advancements are addressing limitations of earlier approaches.
    *   **Identifies Key Challenges:** The paper effectively points out the main challenges that need to be addressed for further progress in the field.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Lack of Deep Dive:** While it covers a wide range of topics, the paper often lacks a deeper, more critical analysis of each individual method or architecture. It mostly *summarizes* existing work without in-depth comparison or assessment of trade-offs.
    *   **Limited Novelty:** The paper primarily acts as a survey or review.  It doesn't present any original research, novel architectures, or significantly new insights.
    *   **Uneven Depth:** Some sections, like the one on specific benchmarks, could be more elaborated on with quantitative comparisons and analysis.  It reads as a qualitative assessment rather than a comprehensive evaluation.
    *   **Limited Discussion of Ethical Considerations:** While perception raises privacy concerns, a more complete analysis will discuss security issues that may arise if GUI agents are hacked or not robust to adversarial examples.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

The paper's value lies in its role as a structured overview of recent developments. It's useful for researchers and practitioners who are new to the field and want to get a quick grasp of the landscape. However, it doesn't offer any significant novelty in terms of original research or breakthrough insights. The significance is moderate as a useful starting point for further research. But because of its primarily review-based nature, the impact is limited.

*   **Potential Influence:**

The paper could serve as a helpful resource for newcomers to the field, potentially influencing their research directions. However, its lack of original contributions and deep analysis limits its potential for significantly reshaping the field. Researchers more familiar with GUI agents will not find it very useful.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the strengths and weaknesses outlined above, and the primary nature of the paper as a summary of existing work, a score of 5 is appropriate. It provides a reasonably good, structured overview, but it lacks the depth, critical analysis, and original contributions required for a higher score. Its usefulness is mainly limited to those new to the field seeking a high-level understanding.

**Score: 5**

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 5/10

### Fane at SemEval-2025 Task 10: Zero-Shot Entity Framing with Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20469v1)
- **Authors**: Enfa Fane, Mihai Surdeanu, Eduardo Blanco, Steven R. Corman
- **Abstract**: Understanding how news narratives frame entities is crucial for studying media's impact on societal perceptions of events. In this paper, we evaluate the zero-shot capabilities of large language models (LLMs) in classifying framing roles. Through systematic experimentation, we assess the effects of input context, prompting strategies, and task decomposition. Our findings show that a hierarchical approach of first identifying broad roles and then fine-grained roles, outperforms single-step classification. We also demonstrate that optimal input contexts and prompts vary across task levels, highlighting the need for subtask-specific strategies. We achieve a Main Role Accuracy of 89.4% and an Exact Match Ratio of 34.5%, demonstrating the effectiveness of our approach. Our findings emphasize the importance of tailored prompt design and input context optimization for improving LLM performance in entity framing.
- **Summary**: **Summary:** The paper titled "Fane at SemEval-2025 Task 10: Zero-Shot Entity Framing with Large Language Models" investigates the ability of large language models (LLMs) to classify how news narratives frame entities—a process critical to understanding media influence on public perception. The authors conduct experiments to analyze various factors, including input context and prompting strategies, in order to enhance the classification of framing roles. They propose a hierarchical classification method, breaking down the task into broad and then fine-grained roles, which outperforms conventional single-step methods. The results showcase a Main Role Accuracy of 89.4% and an Exact Match Ratio of 34.5%, underscoring the efficacy of tailored prompt designs and specific task-related strategies to optimize performance in entity framing. **Critical Evaluation:** The novelty of the paper lies in its focused exploration of hierarchical classification techniques for entity framing using LLMs in a zero-shot manner. This approach is significant as it pioneers a method that recognizes the complexity inherent in framing roles, a topic that hasn't been exhaustively addressed in the literature to date. The robust empirical results and systematic examination of different methodologies also contribute to its relevance.  However, there are some weaknesses. The paper might fall short in providing a comprehensive comparison with existing techniques and frameworks beyond a basic evaluation. Moreover, while the paper demonstrates impressive statistics, the relatively low Exact Match Ratio of 34.5% raises questions about the granularity of the classification and the practical implications of these splits when considering real-world applications. This might suggest that while the hierarchical approach works, it may not capture the intricate details of chain reasoning or multilevel contexts as effectively as needed for nuanced entity framing. Overall, the paper's methodological advances and empirical results contribute meaningfully to the fields of NLP and media studies, especially concerning entity framing in news narratives. However, the impact could be limited by the aforementioned weaknesses, which highlight areas for further development and refinement.  Based on this rigorous rationale, I assign the paper a score of 7, as it presents valuable insights and a promising framework while also calling for improvements in execution and further comparative analysis.  **Score: 7**
- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Robustness via Referencing: Defending against Prompt Injection Attacks by Referencing the Executed Instruction
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20472v1)
- **Authors**: Yulin Chen, Haoran Li, Yuan Sui, Yue Liu, Yufei He, Yangqiu Song, Bryan Hooi
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) have demonstrated impressive performance and have come to dominate the field of natural language processing (NLP) across various tasks. However, due to their strong instruction-following capabilities and inability to distinguish between instructions and data content, LLMs are vulnerable to prompt injection attacks. These attacks manipulate LLMs into deviating from the original input instructions and executing maliciously injected instructions within data content, such as web documents retrieved from search engines. Existing defense methods, including prompt-engineering and fine-tuning approaches, typically instruct models to follow the original input instructions while suppressing their tendencies to execute injected instructions. However, our experiments reveal that suppressing instruction-following tendencies is challenging. Through analyzing failure cases, we observe that although LLMs tend to respond to any recognized instructions, they are aware of which specific instructions they are executing and can correctly reference them within the original prompt. Motivated by these findings, we propose a novel defense method that leverages, rather than suppresses, the instruction-following abilities of LLMs. Our approach prompts LLMs to generate responses that include both answers and their corresponding instruction references. Based on these references, we filter out answers not associated with the original input instructions. Comprehensive experiments demonstrate that our method outperforms prompt-engineering baselines and achieves performance comparable to fine-tuning methods, reducing the attack success rate (ASR) to 0 percent in some scenarios. Moreover, our approach has minimal impact on overall utility.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Robustness via Referencing: Defending against Prompt Injection Attacks by Referencing the Executed Instruction":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the vulnerability of Large Language Models (LLMs) to prompt injection attacks, where malicious instructions injected within data content can manipulate the LLM to deviate from its original task.  Instead of trying to suppress the LLM's inherent instruction-following ability, the authors propose a novel defense method that leverages it. Their approach prompts the LLM to generate responses that include both the answers and explicit references (tags) to the specific instruction they are executing. A filtering mechanism is then used to remove responses that are not linked to the original input instruction. The authors present comprehensive experiments demonstrating that their method outperforms prompt-engineering baselines and achieves performance comparable to fine-tuning methods, with minimal impact on overall utility.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of explicitly referencing the executed instruction during response generation is indeed a novel approach to prompt injection defense.  Most existing defenses focus on suppressing instruction-following, which, as the authors point out, is difficult and can degrade utility. The paper presents a clever alternative that embraces the LLM's instruction-following tendencies but in a controlled and traceable manner.

*   **Significance:**  Prompt injection is a critical problem in real-world LLM applications, as LLMs become increasingly integrated into systems that interact with external data sources.  A robust defense mechanism is essential. While the paper's solution isn't a complete silver bullet, it offers a significant step forward in addressing the problem, particularly compared to simple prompt-engineering based defenses. The demonstration of maintaining utility while mitigating attacks is also significant.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Statement and Motivation:** The paper clearly articulates the problem of prompt injection and motivates the need for alternative defense strategies.
    *   **Well-Defined Approach:**  The proposed method is clearly described and easily understandable. The use of tags and filtering makes the process transparent.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The authors conduct extensive experiments across various attack types (direct and indirect), LLMs (both open-source and closed-source), and datasets. This thorough evaluation strengthens the credibility of their claims.
    *   **Minimal Utility Impact:** The paper highlights that the proposed method has minimal impact on the LLM's general performance, which is crucial for practical applications.
    *   **Defending Against Gradient-Based Attacks:** The evaluation included gradient based attacks which are more challenging to address compared to the more standard prompt engineering-based attacks.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on Structured Output:** The approach relies on the LLM's ability to consistently generate structured output (with the defined tags). While the authors address this using in-context learning examples, it remains a potential weakness. If the LLM deviates from the required output format, the filtering mechanism may fail.
    *   **Scalability and Generalizability of Tags:** The paper assumes that the original instruction can be uniquely identified by a tag or slight paraphrasing. In extremely complex or multi-turn interactions, ensuring the tag accurately and reliably identifies the intended instruction may be more challenging.
    *   **Defense Function's Complexity:** The need to split input text into smaller chunks and add tagging requires significant overhead and changes to the initial input. These requirements may hinder the adoption of the method in existing LLM pipelines.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper's approach has the potential to influence the development of more robust and trustworthy LLM applications. The idea of leveraging instruction-following abilities in a controlled manner could inspire new defense strategies. The detailed evaluation also provides valuable insights for researchers working on prompt injection defense. The work could also encourage future research into improving the robustness of LLMs to structured output generation.

**Rigorous Rationale for Score:**

The paper offers a valuable contribution to prompt injection defense with a novel and well-evaluated method. While the reliance on structured output and tag reliability pose limitations, the comprehensive experimental results demonstrate significant improvements over existing prompt-engineering defenses and comparable results with fine-tuning strategies, all while minimizing the impact on general model utility.

Given the significance of the problem, the novelty of the approach, and the strong experimental results, I assign a score of:

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Enhancing LLM Language Adaption through Cross-lingual In-Context Pre-training
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20484v1)
- **Authors**: Linjuan Wu, Haoran Wei, Huan Lin, Tianhao Li, Baosong Yang, Weiming Lu
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) exhibit remarkable multilingual capabilities despite English-dominated pre-training, attributed to cross-lingual mechanisms during pre-training. Existing methods for enhancing cross-lingual transfer remain constrained by parallel resources, suffering from limited linguistic and domain coverage. We propose Cross-lingual In-context Pre-training (CrossIC-PT), a simple and scalable approach that enhances cross-lingual transfer by leveraging semantically related bilingual texts via simple next-word prediction. We construct CrossIC-PT samples by interleaving semantic-related bilingual Wikipedia documents into a single context window. To access window size constraints, we implement a systematic segmentation policy to split long bilingual document pairs into chunks while adjusting the sliding window mechanism to preserve contextual coherence. We further extend data availability through a semantic retrieval framework to construct CrossIC-PT samples from web-crawled corpus. Experimental results demonstrate that CrossIC-PT improves multilingual performance on three models (Llama-3.1-8B, Qwen2.5-7B, and Qwen2.5-1.5B) across six target languages, yielding performance gains of 3.79%, 3.99%, and 1.95%, respectively, with additional improvements after data augmentation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes CrossIC-PT, a novel pre-training method to improve cross-lingual transfer in Large Language Models (LLMs).  The key idea is to leverage semantically related bilingual texts (English and a target language) via a simple next-word prediction task.  It addresses input window length limitations by using a [SPLIT] token and an optimized sliding window mechanism to maintain contextual coherence. To further extend the training data, the authors develop a cross-lingual semantic retrieval framework to incorporate web-crawled text.  Experiments on three LLMs (Llama-3.1-8B, Qwen2.5-7B, Qwen2.5-1.5B) across six target languages demonstrate improved multilingual performance. Data augmentation further enhances performance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core idea of using semantically related bilingual texts for pre-training is moderately novel. Prior work has explored cross-lingual transfer using parallel corpora, code-switching, and entity linking. However, using *in-context* learning in the pre-training phase with semantically related, but not necessarily parallel, texts presents a new approach. The window splitting and sliding optimization strategy is a practical contribution, but not inherently groundbreaking. The cross-lingual retrieval framework for data augmentation provides a practical extension.
* **Significance:** The paper addresses a significant challenge in multilingual NLP: improving the performance of LLMs on non-English languages without excessive amounts of training data. The proposed method offers a scalable and relatively simple approach. The improvements in multilingual performance reported are non-trivial and demonstrate the potential of CrossIC-PT.
* **Strengths:**
    * **Simplicity and Scalability:** The method doesn't rely on parallel corpora, making it more scalable and applicable to a wider range of languages.  It is also easy to implement by only requiring CPT and LoRA.
    * **Systematic Segmentation Policy:** the systematic segmentation policy addresses the input window constraints of pre-trained models.
    * **Experimental Validation:** The paper provides a comprehensive experimental evaluation across multiple LLMs, languages, and tasks. The ablation study on the sliding window mechanism is insightful. The data augmentation experiments prove the generalizability.
    * **Reproducibility:** The paper provides sufficient details to allow for replication of the method.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Semantic Relatedness Definition:** The semantic relatedness relies on shared titles in Wikipedia, which might be limiting. The retrieval-based data augmentation step addresses this issue partially, but the reliance on title keywords for retrieval introduces a potential bias towards certain types of texts.
    * **English-centric bias:** By using English as the primary "guide" language, the method might inadvertently reinforce biases present in English data. More investigation would be warranted.  Though the study checks English performance to ensure catastrophic forgetting is not happening, more could be done.
    * **Incremental improvements:** While the gains are consistent, they are not dramatic. Compared to a simpler approach, such as Mix-PT, the margin is decent.
    * **Limited Task Diversity:** Focuses mostly on tasks adapted from English to other languages rather than exploring the full richness of cross-lingual capabilities.

* **Potential Impact:** The CrossIC-PT method has the potential to be adopted by researchers and practitioners looking to improve the multilingual capabilities of LLMs, particularly for languages with limited resources. The method's simplicity and scalability are significant advantages.
* **Comparisons and Baselines** The paper could have benefited from comparisons with other cross-lingual transfer methods beyond EMMA and LEIA.

**Justification:**

The paper makes a solid contribution to the field of multilingual NLP by providing a practical and scalable method for improving cross-lingual transfer in LLMs. The consistent performance gains across multiple languages and tasks, along with the insightful ablation study, support the effectiveness of the proposed approach. While the reliance on English as the primary language and the modest magnitude of performance gains somewhat limit the novelty and potential impact, the simplicity, scalability, and reproducibility of CrossIC-PT make it a valuable addition to the field. Therefore, a score of 7 reflects this.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Token-Efficient Prompt Injection Attack: Provoking Cessation in LLM Reasoning via Adaptive Token Compression
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20493v1)
- **Authors**: Yu Cui, Yujun Cai, Yiwei Wang
- **Abstract**: While reasoning large language models (LLMs) demonstrate remarkable performance across various tasks, they also contain notable security vulnerabilities. Recent research has uncovered a "thinking-stopped" vulnerability in DeepSeek-R1, where model-generated reasoning tokens can forcibly interrupt the inference process, resulting in empty responses that compromise LLM-integrated applications. However, existing methods triggering this vulnerability require complex mathematical word problems with long prompts--even exceeding 5,000 tokens. To reduce the token cost and formally define this vulnerability, we propose a novel prompt injection attack named "Reasoning Interruption Attack", based on adaptive token compression. We demonstrate that simple standalone arithmetic tasks can effectively trigger this vulnerability, and the prompts based on such tasks exhibit simpler logical structures than mathematical word problems. We develop a systematic approach to efficiently collect attack prompts and an adaptive token compression framework that utilizes LLMs to automatically compress these prompts. Experiments show our compression framework significantly reduces prompt length while maintaining effective attack capabilities. We further investigate the attack's performance via output prefix and analyze the underlying causes of the vulnerability, providing valuable insights for improving security in reasoning LLMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel prompt injection attack, termed "Reasoning Interruption Attack," targeting the "thinking-stopped" vulnerability in reasoning-focused Large Language Models (LLMs), specifically DeepSeek-R1. Unlike existing methods that require complex mathematical word problems and high token consumption, this attack utilizes simple standalone arithmetic tasks and adaptive token compression. The authors demonstrate that their approach can efficiently trigger the vulnerability, causing the model to halt its reasoning process and produce an empty response, while significantly reducing token costs. The paper also analyzes the underlying causes of the vulnerability through output prefix-based attacks, offering insights into improving the security of reasoning LLMs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper demonstrates novelty in several aspects. First, it identifies a simplified trigger condition for the "thinking-stopped" vulnerability, showing that simple arithmetic tasks are effective. This contrasts with prior research focusing on complex word problems. Second, the adaptive token compression framework leverages LLMs to automatically compress attack prompts, reducing token consumption. Third, the systematic approach for prompt acquisition, requiring only a small number of search iterations, is also novel. Overall, the attack simplification and token consumption reduction are significant.
*   **Significance:** The paper highlights a critical security vulnerability in reasoning LLMs that can be exploited to disrupt their functionality. The "thinking-stopped" vulnerability poses a serious threat to LLM-integrated applications, as it compromises the model's ability to produce any output, rather than just generating undesirable outputs. The paper's findings demonstrate the importance of addressing this vulnerability to ensure the reliability and security of LLMs. The efficiency of the proposed attack makes it easier to study and mitigate this class of vulnerability.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   The paper provides a clear and well-defined description of the "Reasoning Interruption Attack."
    *   The adaptive token compression framework is a valuable contribution, as it reduces the cost of studying and exploiting the vulnerability.
    *   The analysis of the attack's performance through output prefix-based attacks provides valuable insights into the underlying causes of the vulnerability.
    *   The experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed approach in reducing token consumption while maintaining high attack success rates.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The evaluation is primarily focused on DeepSeek-R1, limiting the generalizability of the findings. While the paper mentions other models, a more comprehensive evaluation across different LLMs would strengthen the conclusions.
    *   The analysis of the underlying causes of the vulnerability is speculative and requires further investigation to validate the proposed mechanisms.
    *   The ethical considerations section is brief. A more thorough discussion of the potential misuse of the proposed attack and the measures taken to prevent it would be beneficial.
*   **Impact:** The paper has the potential to significantly impact the field of LLM security. The proposed attack provides a more efficient method for studying and mitigating the "thinking-stopped" vulnerability. The insights gained from the analysis of the attack's performance can inform the design of more robust model architectures. The work could drive further research into the security of reasoning LLMs and the development of effective defense mechanisms.
*   **Justification:** The paper's score reflects the significant novelty in attack simplification and adaptive compression along with its potential impact on LLM security. The weaknesses, such as the limited model evaluation and speculative analysis, detract from the paper's overall strength, preventing a higher score. The significance lies in demonstrating an effective way to break sophisticated LLMs and the insights provided on where vulnerabilities could exist.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### UniDetox: Universal Detoxification of Large Language Models via Dataset Distillation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20500v1)
- **Authors**: Huimin Lu, Masaru Isonuma, Junichiro Mori, Ichiro Sakata
- **Abstract**: We present UniDetox, a universally applicable method designed to mitigate toxicity across various large language models (LLMs). Previous detoxification methods are typically model-specific, addressing only individual models or model families, and require careful hyperparameter tuning due to the trade-off between detoxification efficacy and language modeling performance. In contrast, UniDetox provides a detoxification technique that can be universally applied to a wide range of LLMs without the need for separate model-specific tuning. Specifically, we propose a novel and efficient dataset distillation technique for detoxification using contrastive decoding. This approach distills detoxifying representations in the form of synthetic text data, enabling universal detoxification of any LLM through fine-tuning with the distilled text. Our experiments demonstrate that the detoxifying text distilled from GPT-2 can effectively detoxify larger models, including OPT, Falcon, and LLaMA-2. Furthermore, UniDetox eliminates the need for separate hyperparameter tuning for each model, as a single hyperparameter configuration can be seamlessly applied across different models. Additionally, analysis of the detoxifying text reveals a reduction in politically biased content, providing insights into the attributes necessary for effective detoxification of LLMs.
- **Summary**: **Summary:** The paper introduces UniDetox, a novel method aimed at reducing toxicity in large language models (LLMs) using a universal detoxification approach. In contrast to previous model-specific detoxification methods that require extensive hyperparameter tuning, UniDetox offers a dataset distillation technique that employs contrastive decoding to generate synthetic text data for detoxification. This distilled data can then fine-tune various models, including larger ones such as OPT, Falcon, and LLaMA-2, without the need for individual tuning configurations. The results indicate that the detoxified text significantly reduces politically biased content, illustrating the potential for more effective detoxification in LLMs. **Evaluation of Novelty and Significance:** **Strengths:** 1. **Universal Applicability:** One of the main strengths of UniDetox is its ability to apply a single detoxification method across multiple LLMs without requiring model-specific adjustments. This flexibility is significant given the rapidly evolving landscape of LLMs. 2. **Efficiency of Dataset Distillation:** The novel approach of using dataset distillation via contrastive decoding for the generation of detoxified synthetic text is an innovative technique. This could streamline the detoxification process, which is especially critical as LLMs are increasingly deployed in sensitive contexts. 3. **Reduction in Bias:** The reported reduction in politically biased content is an important contribution to the field, addressing one of the critical ethical concerns associated with AI and its societal impacts. **Weaknesses:** 1. **Limited Scope of Evaluation:** While the paper demonstrates effectiveness using specific LLMs (like GPT-2 and larger models), it would benefit from broader evaluation across diverse and real-world applications, as well as different types of toxicity beyond political bias. 2. **Lack of Comparative Analysis:** The paper does not sufficiently compare UniDetox with existing detoxification approaches quantitatively, making it difficult to gauge relative effectiveness and performance trade-offs. 3. **Theoretical Justification:** While the practical application of the method is shown, a deeper theoretical grounding on why this approach works universally across models would strengthen the paper's contributions. **Potential Influence on the Field:** UniDetox presents a significant step towards standardizing practices for mitigating toxicity in LLMs, thus potentially influencing future research and practices in AI safety and ethics. If widely adopted, it could ease the implementation phase for developers and researchers working to create safer AI systems. Overall, while the paper introduces several strong ideas and promises significant practical implications, it lacks thorough comparative analysis and broader applicability checks that would affirm its robustness and generalizability. **Score: 7**  This score reflects a good level of novelty and a positive impact potential, but acknowledges limitations in validation and depth that prevent it from being deemed exceptional.
- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### MuRAL: A Multi-Resident Ambient Sensor Dataset Annotated with Natural Language for Activities of Daily Living
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20505v1)
- **Authors**: Xi Chen, Julien Cumin, Fano Ramparany, Dominique Vaufreydaz
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in Large Language Models (LLMs) have shown promising potential for human activity recognition (HAR) using ambient sensors, especially through natural language reasoning and zero-shot learning. However, existing datasets such as CASAS, ARAS, and MARBLE were not originally designed with LLMs in mind and therefore lack the contextual richness, complexity, and annotation granularity required to fully exploit LLM capabilities. In this paper, we introduce MuRAL, the first Multi-Resident Ambient sensor dataset with natural Language, comprising over 21 hours of multi-user sensor data collected from 21 sessions in a smart-home environment. MuRAL is annotated with fine-grained natural language descriptions, resident identities, and high-level activity labels, all situated in dynamic, realistic multi-resident settings. We benchmark MuRAL using state-of-the-art LLMs for three core tasks: subject assignment, action description, and activity classification. Our results demonstrate that while LLMs can provide rich semantic interpretations of ambient data, current models still face challenges in handling multi-user ambiguity and under-specified sensor contexts. We release MuRAL to support future research on LLM-powered, explainable, and socially aware activity understanding in smart environments. For access to the dataset, please reach out to us via the provided contact information. A direct link for dataset retrieval will be made available at this location in due course.
- **Summary**: **Summary:** The paper introduces MuRAL, a novel dataset aimed at improving human activity recognition (HAR) using large language models (LLMs) and ambient sensors. Recognizing the limitations of existing datasets, which lack the context and fine-grained annotation needed for LLM applications, MuRAL provides over 21 hours of multi-user sensor data from smart home settings, enriched with natural language descriptions, resident identities, and high-level activity labels. The authors benchmark the dataset against LLMs in three key tasks: subject assignment, action description, and activity classification, revealing that current LLMs struggle with multi-user ambiguity and context under-specification. The paper presents MuRAL as a valuable resource for advancing explainable and socially aware activity understanding in smart environments. **Critical Evaluation:** **Novelty:** The introduction of MuRAL represents a significant advancement in the HAR domain by addressing specific limitations of prior datasets not designed for LLM integration. The focus on multi-resident contexts with fine-grained linguistic annotations is especially novel and necessary for developing more sophisticated and accurate HAR systems. **Significance:** This dataset has the potential to drive research forward in harnessing the capabilities of LLMs for activity recognition. By providing a realistic and richly annotated dataset, MuRAL could facilitate the development of models that can offer richer semantic interpretations and address issues related to multi-user scenarios. **Strengths:** - The dataset's design caters explicitly to the requirements of LLM applications, filling a notable gap in the existing literature. - The comprehensive nature—covering over 21 hours of data from 21 sessions—provides a robust foundation for future research. - Benchmarking against state-of-the-art LLMs helps contextualize the dataset’s capabilities and limitations in practical scenarios. **Weaknesses:** - While the dataset addresses the gap in LLM-specific challenges, the paper briefly touches on the specific limitations that the state-of-the-art models face, leaving room for more in-depth analysis. - The release mechanism for accessing the dataset is currently unclear, which may hinder immediate usability for interested researchers. - Further discussion on the implications of these challenges in real-world applications would enhance the paper's impact. **Conclusion:** Overall, MuRAL presents a meaningful contribution to the field of HAR by integrating natural language, multi-resident dynamics, and LLM compatibility. The paper lays essential groundwork for future research while identifying critical areas for model improvement. The strengths of the dataset and its potential applications lend substantial value to ongoing studies in activity recognition.  **Score:** 8
- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 8/10

### SteelBlastQC: Shot-blasted Steel Surface Dataset with Interpretable Detection of Surface Defects
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20510v1)
- **Authors**: Irina Ruzavina, Lisa Sophie Theis, Jesse Lemeer, Rutger de Groen, Leo Ebeling, Andrej Hulak, Jouaria Ali, Guangzhi Tang, Rico Mockel
- **Abstract**: Automating the quality control of shot-blasted steel surfaces is crucial for improving manufacturing efficiency and consistency. This study presents a dataset of 1654 labeled RGB images (512x512) of steel surfaces, classified as either "ready for paint" or "needs shot-blasting." The dataset captures real-world surface defects, including discoloration, welding lines, scratches and corrosion, making it well-suited for training computer vision models. Additionally, three classification approaches were evaluated: Compact Convolutional Transformers (CCT), Support Vector Machines (SVM) with ResNet-50 feature extraction, and a Convolutional Autoencoder (CAE). The supervised methods (CCT and SVM) achieve 95% classification accuracy on the test set, with CCT leveraging transformer-based attention mechanisms and SVM offering a computationally efficient alternative. The CAE approach, while less effective, establishes a baseline for unsupervised quality control. We present interpretable decision-making by all three neural networks, allowing industry users to visually pinpoint problematic regions and understand the model's rationale. By releasing the dataset and baseline codes, this work aims to support further research in defect detection, advance the development of interpretable computer vision models for quality control, and encourage the adoption of automated inspection systems in industrial applications.
- **Summary**: ### Summary: The paper titled "SteelBlastQC: Shot-blasted Steel Surface Dataset with Interpretable Detection of Surface Defects" introduces a new dataset consisting of 1654 labeled RGB images of shot-blasted steel surfaces, classified for quality control as either "ready for paint" or "needs shot-blasting." The dataset includes various surface defects typical in manufacturing, like discoloration and corrosion, making it ideal for training computer vision models aimed at automating quality control processes. It evaluates three classification methods: Compact Convolutional Transformers (CCT), Support Vector Machines (SVM) with feature extraction from ResNet-50, and Convolutional Autoencoders (CAE). The supervised methods (CCT and SVM) achieve impressive 95% test set accuracy, with CCT utilizing transformer attention and SVM documenting computational efficiency. While CAE sets a baseline for unsupervised methods, it is less effective compared to the supervised techniques. Notably, all models provide interpretable outputs that highlight defective regions, offering transparency in decision-making for industrial users. The authors aim for their dataset and methodologies to promote further research in defect detection and facilitate the implementation of automated inspection systems. ### Critical Evaluation: **Strengths:** 1. **Dataset Quality and Relevance:** The creation of a large, well-labeled dataset specifically for shot-blasted steel surfaces is a significant contribution, as datasets for industrial applications are often lacking. This can facilitate future research, helping bridge the gap between academic and practical applications. 2. **Interpretable Machine Learning:** The focus on interpretability in the model’s predictions is crucial for industrial applications, where understanding the model’s decision-making can lead to greater trust and usability in a production environment. This aspect is often overlooked in traditional machine learning approaches. 3. **Competitive Performance of Models:** The reported high accuracy of the CCT and SVM methods not only demonstrates the capabilities of the proposed approaches but also sets a standard for comparison for future research in defect detection. **Weaknesses:** 1. **Limited Scope of Defects:** Although this study addresses several types of surface defects, the generalization of findings to other types of steel or different manufacturing contexts may be limited. The dataset’s focus on a specific category might restrict broader applicability. 2. **Comparison to Existing Benchmarks:** The paper lacks a comprehensive review of existing datasets and models in the industrial defect detection domain. A comparative analysis could contextualize the advancements made and highlight the novelty more clearly. 3. **Single-Task Focus:** The paper’s focus solely on classification and its interpretation mechanism may limit innovation in other critical areas such as regression analyses for defect severity, the integration of temporal data, or real-time performance metrics. **Novelty and Significance:** This paper presents a valuable dataset and methods for defect detection, which is a necessity in today’s automated manufacturing landscape. While the novelty primarily lies in the dataset and the emphasis on interpretability, the methodologies, although effective, are somewhat standard within the realm of computer vision applications. The potential for real-world impact through facilitating more efficient and interpretable quality control systems is significant, yet the work does not address the breadth of industrial quality control challenges fully. Overall, the paper advances the field by contributing a pertinent dataset and addressing the critical aspect of explainability. Therefore, given the strengths, weaknesses, and the potential influence on the field, I would assign the paper a score of **Score: 7**. This reflects a solid contribution to the field with clear implications for industrial practices, but it also points to areas for improvement in terms of broader applicability and contextual benchmarking.
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Dynamic Attention Analysis for Backdoor Detection in Text-to-Image Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20518v1)
- **Authors**: Zhongqi Wang, Jie Zhang, Shiguang Shan, Xilin Chen
- **Abstract**: Recent studies have revealed that text-to-image diffusion models are vulnerable to backdoor attacks, where attackers implant stealthy textual triggers to manipulate model outputs. Previous backdoor detection methods primarily focus on the static features of backdoor samples. However, a vital property of diffusion models is their inherent dynamism. This study introduces a novel backdoor detection perspective named Dynamic Attention Analysis (DAA), showing that these dynamic characteristics serve as better indicators for backdoor detection. Specifically, by examining the dynamic evolution of cross-attention maps, we observe that backdoor samples exhibit distinct feature evolution patterns at the $<$EOS$>$ token compared to benign samples. To quantify these dynamic anomalies, we first introduce DAA-I, which treats the tokens' attention maps as spatially independent and measures dynamic feature using the Frobenius norm. Furthermore, to better capture the interactions between attention maps and refine the feature, we propose a dynamical system-based approach, referred to as DAA-S. This model formulates the spatial correlations among attention maps using a graph-based state equation and we theoretically analyze the global asymptotic stability of this method. Extensive experiments across five representative backdoor attack scenarios demonstrate that our approach significantly surpasses existing detection methods, achieving an average F1 Score of 79.49% and an AUC of 87.67%. The code is available at https://github.com/Robin-WZQ/DAA.
- **Summary**: ### Summary The paper titled "Dynamic Attention Analysis for Backdoor Detection in Text-to-Image Diffusion Models" addresses the vulnerability of text-to-image diffusion models to backdoor attacks, which manipulate model outputs through subtle textual triggers. Unlike previous methods focusing on static features of backdoor samples, the authors propose a novel approach called Dynamic Attention Analysis (DAA) that leverages the dynamic characteristics of diffusion models to enhance detection efficacy. They identify that backdoor samples exhibit unique dynamic evolution patterns in the cross-attention maps, particularly at the $<$EOS$>$ token. The study introduces two methods: DAA-I, which assesses the dynamic features of attention maps using the Frobenius norm for independent analysis, and DAA-S, which utilizes a dynamical system approach to explore spatial correlations among attention maps through a graph framework. The proposed methods demonstrate significant performance improvements over existing detection techniques through experiments across various backdoor attack scenarios, achieving an average F1 Score of 79.49% and an AUC of 87.67%. The source code is provided online for further research and validation. ### Critical Evaluation **Novelty:** The novelty of the paper lies in its focus on the dynamic aspects of diffusion models for backdoor detection. While the idea of utilizing attention maps is not new, the distinct shift towards analyzing their temporal evolution to distinguish benign from backdoored outputs represents a fresh perspective in backdoor detection techniques. This differentiation from static analyses is commendable and showcases a deeper understanding of the underlying mechanics of diffusion models. **Significance:** Given the increasing use of text-to-image diffusion models in various applications, ensuring their security against backdoor threats is critical. The methods proposed—especially the dynamic emphasis—could have implications for developing more robust AI systems. The paper shows empirical evidence of effectiveness, suggesting practical applications and encouraging future work on the topic. **Strengths:** 1. **Innovative Approach:** Introduces a method of analysis focused on the dynamic nature of attention rather than static attributes. 2. **Solid Empirical Validation:** Demonstrates effectiveness through extensive experimentation across multiple scenarios. 3. **Potential for broader impact:** The findings may inspire future research on model security in similar architectures. **Weaknesses:** 1. **Complexity of Proposed Methods:** While DAA-S introduces a sophisticated graph-based approach, it may introduce computational overhead that can be a barrier for adoption in real-world applications. 2. **Limited Generalizability:** The focus on five specific backdoor attack scenarios may limit the generalization of the findings. Future work should explore a wider range of attacks and model types. **Conclusion:** Overall, the paper presents a meaningful contribution to the field of AI security, particularly concerning text-to-image models. The unique perspective on dynamic attention maps for anomaly detection is likely to stimulate further research. However, some complexity and scope limitations must be addressed to enhance both the method's accessibility and its applicability across diverse scenarios. **Score: 8**   This score reflects the paper's innovative approach and significant contributions while noting room for improvement in complexity and generalizability.
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### TriniMark: A Robust Generative Speech Watermarking Method for Trinity-Level Attribution
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20532v1)
- **Authors**: Yue Li, Weizhi Liu, Dongdong Lin
- **Abstract**: The emergence of diffusion models has facilitated the generation of speech with reinforced fidelity and naturalness. While deepfake detection technologies have manifested the ability to identify AI-generated content, their efficacy decreases as generative models become increasingly sophisticated. Furthermore, current research in the field has not adequately addressed the necessity for robust watermarking to safeguard the intellectual property rights associated with synthetic speech and generative models. To remedy this deficiency, we propose a \textbf{ro}bust generative \textbf{s}peech wat\textbf{e}rmarking method (TriniMark) for authenticating the generated content and safeguarding the copyrights by enabling the traceability of the diffusion model. We first design a structure-lightweight watermark encoder that embeds watermarks into the time-domain features of speech and reconstructs the waveform directly. A temporal-aware gated convolutional network is meticulously designed in the watermark decoder for bit-wise watermark recovery. Subsequently, the waveform-guided fine-tuning strategy is proposed for fine-tuning the diffusion model, which leverages the transferability of watermarks and enables the diffusion model to incorporate watermark knowledge effectively. When an attacker trains a surrogate model using the outputs of the target model, the embedded watermark can still be learned by the surrogate model and correctly extracted. Comparative experiments with state-of-the-art methods demonstrate the superior robustness of our method, particularly in countering compound attacks.
- **Summary**: **Summary:** The paper introduces TriniMark, a novel robust generative speech watermarking method designed to ensure the authentication and traceability of AI-generated speech content, primarily addressing the challenges posed by increasingly sophisticated generative models. The authors present a lightweight watermark encoder that operates in the time domain, embedding watermarks into the speech waveform while maintaining quality. It also utilizes a temporal-aware gated convolutional network in the decoder for accurate watermark extraction. A key innovation is the waveform-guided fine-tuning strategy, which enables diffusion models to inherently learn watermark knowledge. The method demonstrates significant robustness against compound attacks, outpacing existing techniques in watermarking, especially when the original model is targeted by surrogate models. **Critical Evaluation:** The novelty of the TriniMark method lies primarily in its dual approach of watermark embedding and extraction, along with the innovative fine-tuning strategy for generative models. This is a notable contribution, addressing a crucial gap in current AI generative speech developments—namely, the lack of robust watermarking techniques to protect intellectual property in the face of advancing deepfake detection capabilities. The authors provide a sound theoretical backing for their approach and present comparative experimental results that highlight the advantages of their method over existing state-of-the-art watermarking technologies. However, while the method shows considerable promise, the paper could benefit from several improvements. The implementation details related to the architecture of both the encoder and decoder are not extensively described, which may hinder reproducibility efforts. Additionally, the experimental scenarios, while evidently comprehensive, may not cover all potential real-world applications and attack vectors, limiting the generalizability of the reported results. Furthermore, the assessment of robustness—although superior—could be bolstered by a more diverse set of attack conditions. In terms of significance, the paper contributes to an increasingly important area of research at the intersection of generative models and content protection. As AI-generated content becomes more pervasive, establishing mechanisms that verify authenticity will be paramount. This study provides a practical approach to watermarking that could influence future work and applications significantly. Taking into account the above strengths and weaknesses, the contribution of the paper is substantial, but not without its limitations. Therefore, I would assign a score of **Score: 7**. This score reflects a solid advancement in the field, especially in a timely area of research, while also recognizing the potential improvements that could further enhance the impact of this work.
- **Classification**: cs.MM
- **Score**: 7/10

### SNR-aware Semantic Image Transmission with Deep Learning-based Channel Estimation in Fading Channels
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20557v1)
- **Authors**: Mahmoud M. Salim, Mohamed S. Abdalzaher, Ali H. Muqaibel, Hussein A. Elsayed, Inkyu Lee
- **Abstract**: Semantic communications (SCs) play a central role in shaping the future of the sixth generation (6G) wireless systems, which leverage rapid advances in deep learning (DL). In this regard, end-to-end optimized DL-based joint source-channel coding (JSCC) has been adopted to achieve SCs, particularly in image transmission. Utilizing vision transformers in the encoder/decoder design has enabled significant advancements in image semantic extraction, surpassing traditional convolutional neural networks (CNNs). In this paper, we propose a new JSCC paradigm for image transmission, namely Swin semantic image transmission (SwinSIT), based on the Swin transformer. The Swin transformer is employed to construct both the semantic encoder and decoder for efficient image semantic extraction and reconstruction. Inspired by the squeezing-and-excitation (SE) network, we introduce a signal-to-noise-ratio (SNR)-aware module that utilizes SNR feedback to adaptively perform a double-phase enhancement for the encoder-extracted semantic map and its noisy version at the decoder. Additionally, a CNN-based channel estimator and compensator (CEAC) module repurposes an image-denoising CNN to mitigate fading channel effects. To optimize deployment in resource-constrained IoT devices, a joint pruning and quantization scheme compresses the SwinSIT model. Simulations evaluate the SwinSIT performance against conventional benchmarks demonstrating its effectiveness. Moreover, the model's compressed version substantially reduces its size while maintaining favorable PSNR performance.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**
