The paper proposes a new Joint Source-Channel Coding (JSCC) paradigm called Swin Semantic Image Transmission (SwinSIT) for wireless image transmission in fading channels. The key contributions are:

1.  Using Swin Transformers for both semantic encoding and decoding of images.
2.  Introducing an SNR-aware module inspired by Squeeze-and-Excitation networks to enhance the semantic map based on channel conditions. This module operates in two phases, enhancing the map at both the encoder and decoder.
3.  Developing a CNN-based Channel Estimator and Compensator (CEAC) using a DnCNN (Denoising CNN) architecture to estimate fading coefficients and mitigate channel effects.
4.  Implementing a joint pruning and quantization scheme to compress the SwinSIT model for resource-constrained IoT devices.

The paper evaluates the performance of SwinSIT against benchmarks like BPG+LDPC, Deep JSCC, and WITT, showing improved PSNR and MS-SSIM results, even with compression.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper introduces a novel architecture that combines Swin Transformers with an SNR-aware module and a DnCNN-based channel estimator specifically for image transmission. While Swin Transformers have been used in image processing, their integration with the proposed SNR-aware mechanism and the DnCNN for channel estimation for semantic communication is a novel contribution. The joint pruning and quantization scheme is a standard technique, but its application specifically for the SwinSIT architecture adds to the practical contribution. It builds upon the foundations established by WitT, while adding improvements in channel estimation and more effective SNR awareness.

*   **Significance:** The work addresses a key challenge in semantic communications: adapting to varying channel conditions while maintaining acceptable image quality under resource constraints. The two-phase SNR-aware module offers a potential improvement over existing techniques, particularly in dynamic channel environments. The successful compression of the model to a small size (5.3 MB) further enhances its practical applicability, especially for IoT.
    The experimental results consistently demonstrate the superiority of SwinSIT. The paper provides a good empirical basis for the claims made about its performance.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Good Integration:** The core strength of the paper is the well-integrated architecture, combining various components like Swin Transformers, SNR awareness, and channel estimation in a coherent manner.
    *   **Comprehensive evaluation:** The empirical evaluation is solid, covering different datasets, SNR ranges, and comparisons with other models.
    *   **Practical Relevance:** The model compression aspects directly address the limitations of deploying complex deep learning models on IoT devices.
    * The paper also explains the modules, algorithm steps and equations quite clearly.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental Improvement:** While the architecture has good contributions, some components are not radically new. The integration is a bigger contribution rather than individual novel components.
    *   **Channel Model Simplification:**  The reliance on perfect SNR knowledge at the transmitter and a simplified Rayleigh fading channel model might limit the real-world applicability of the results. More realistic channel models and imperfect SNR knowledge should have been discussed.
    *   **Limited Ablation Study:** A more detailed ablation study would have been beneficial to isolate the specific contribution of each component (e.g., SNR-aware vs. CEAC) in the overall performance gain.
    * The choice of the 60% threshold isn't explained, nor is any experimentation done with alternative threshold values.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has good potential influence due to its practical relevance and performance improvements over current benchmarks. It offers a good framework for further research in adaptive semantic communication systems and resource-efficient deep learning for IoT.

**Overall Score and Justification:**

Considering the novelty, significance, strengths, and weaknesses, a score of **7.5** is warranted.

*   It demonstrates good performance. However, the improvements are incremental, and the reliance on perfect SNR knowledge weakens the paper's argument. A more realistic and in-depth analysis of the limitations of the individual modules is necessary to give a higher score.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.IT
- **Score**: 7/10

### Generate more than one child in your co-evolutionary semi-supervised learning GAN
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20560v1)
- **Authors**: Francisco Sedeño, Jamal Toutouh, Francisco Chicano
- **Abstract**: Generative Adversarial Networks (GANs) are very useful methods to address semi-supervised learning (SSL) datasets, thanks to their ability to generate samples similar to real data. This approach, called SSL-GAN has attracted many researchers in the last decade. Evolutionary algorithms have been used to guide the evolution and training of SSL-GANs with great success. In particular, several co-evolutionary approaches have been applied where the two networks of a GAN (the generator and the discriminator) are evolved in separate populations. The co-evolutionary approaches published to date assume some spatial structure of the populations, based on the ideas of cellular evolutionary algorithms. They also create one single individual per generation and follow a generational replacement strategy in the evolution. In this paper, we re-consider those algorithmic design decisions and propose a new co-evolutionary approach, called Co-evolutionary Elitist SSL-GAN (CE-SSLGAN), with panmictic population, elitist replacement, and more than one individual in the offspring. We evaluate the performance of our proposed method using three standard benchmark datasets. The results show that creating more than one offspring per population and using elitism improves the results in comparison with a classical SSL-GAN.
- **Summary**: ### Summary: The paper titled "Generate more than one child in your co-evolutionary semi-supervised learning GAN" introduces a novel approach to enhancing the performance of semi-supervised learning (SSL) using Generative Adversarial Networks (GANs). The authors propose the Co-evolutionary Elitist SSL-GAN (CE-SSLGAN), which diverges from traditional co-evolutionary strategies by implementing a panmictic population structure, elitist replacement, and the generation of multiple offspring per evolutionary cycle. These modifications aim to more effectively leverage the benefits of evolutionary algorithms in training GANs for SSL tasks. The experimental results across three benchmark datasets indicate that CE-SSLGAN outperforms classical SSL-GANs, highlighting the advantages of their proposed methodological alterations in generating more than one offspring and employing elitism in population replacement. ### Critical Evaluation: The novelty of the paper lies in its proposed modifications to the established framework of co-evolutionary algorithms in the context of SSL-GANs. By shifting from a single offspring generation to multiple offspring and adopting an elitist replacement strategy, the authors aim to improve the diversity and quality of the generated outputs. This methodological shift is significant as it addresses a limitation in prior research that could lead to stagnation in evolutionary improvement due to a lack of genetic diversity. However, the paper does exhibit certain weaknesses that need critical attention. For one, while the proposed changes are promising, the justification for their superiority over existing approaches could be strengthened with more comprehensive theoretical underpinning. The authors could have explored the impacts of specific hyperparameters in relation to offspring generation and elitism, which would provide greater insight into the conditions under which CE-SSLGAN excels. Additionally, the discussions on potential limitations of the new approach, such as computational overhead or the risk of overfitting, are somewhat superficial, which could lead to a lack of understanding of real-world applicability. Moreover, while the experimental results show improvements, a more extensive set of comparisons with varied architectures or other state-of-the-art methods could bolster claims and provide a clearer picture of the contribution of CE-SSLGAN to the field. In terms of significance, the paper's contributions have the potential to influence ongoing research in evolutionary algorithms and semi-supervised learning. By proposing an efficient mechanism to enhance the training process of GANs, it opens up paths for future research that could investigate hybrid models or different evolutionary strategies tailored to SSL tasks. Therefore, while the paper does present a novel approach with potential for impact, it would benefit from further validation and discussion regarding its claims and applicability. Score: 7
- **Classification**: cs.NE
- **Score**: 7/10

### ReCIT: Reconstructing Full Private Data from Gradient in Parameter-Efficient Fine-Tuning of Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20570v1)
- **Authors**: Jin Xie, Ruishi He, Songze Li, Xiaojun Jia, Shouling Ji
- **Abstract**: Parameter-efficient fine-tuning (PEFT) has emerged as a practical solution for adapting large language models (LLMs) to custom datasets with significantly reduced computational cost. When carrying out PEFT under collaborative learning scenarios (e.g., federated learning), it is often required to exchange model updates (or gradients) across parties. These gradients, even with limited dimensions, can cause severe breach of data privacy. Recent works have shown that both contextual prefixes and personally identifiable information (PII) can be exposed through gradients. However, \emph{simultaneously} and \emph{accurately} recovering both components from the same training instance remains infeasible due to the following challenges: 1) limited number of PEFT parameters; 2) high-dimensional token spaces; and 3) large batch sizes. We propose ReCIT, a novel privacy attack that addresses all challenges, and achieves recovery of \emph{full} private data from PEFT gradients with high fidelity. Specifically, ReCIT proposes to enhance the memorization capability of the pre-trained model through malicious fine-tuning with Personal Notes; ReCIT also proposes a novel filter-based token extraction technique and a token pairing mechanism, to accurately reconstruct tokens from the training sequences with large batch sizes. Extensive evaluations show that ReCIT consistently outperforms state-of-the-art gradient inversion and memorization-based attacks across different PEFT paradigms. It achieves up to 10$\times$ higher PII recovery rates and remains effective across varying batch sizes, especially in settings where prefix reconstruction is intractable for conventional approaches. These findings highlight an urgent need to reassess the privacy guarantees of PEFT, especially in decentralized or shared training environments.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "ReCIT: Reconstructing Full Private Data from Gradient in Parameter-Efficient Fine-Tuning of Large Language Models" addresses the privacy risks associated with parameter-efficient fine-tuning (PEFT) in decentralized or federated learning scenarios. The authors demonstrate that gradients shared during PEFT, even with limited dimensions, can leak private data, including both contextual prefixes and Personally Identifiable Information (PII). The paper proposes ReCIT, a novel privacy attack that combines analytic reasoning and memorization-based techniques to reconstruct full private data from PEFT gradients. ReCIT introduces malicious pre-training using "Personal Notes" to enhance PII memorization, a filter-based token extraction technique, and a token pairing mechanism to handle large batch sizes. The authors evaluate ReCIT across different PEFT paradigms and model architectures, showing that it outperforms existing gradient inversion and memorization attacks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its holistic approach to recovering both contextual prefixes *and* PII from PEFT gradients simultaneously. Previous work mainly focused on prefix reconstruction. ReCIT's hybrid attack framework, combining analytic reasoning and memorization, is also a novel contribution. The specific techniques, such as using "Personal Notes" for pre-training and the filter-based token extraction with pairing, contribute further to its novelty. The systematic evaluation across diverse PEFT methods and models also adds value.
*   **Significance:** The paper highlights a critical and often overlooked vulnerability in federated learning using PEFT. The ability to recover PII with high fidelity poses a significant threat to data privacy. The findings emphasize the need to reassess the privacy guarantees of PEFT and develop more robust defenses. The study's practical implications are substantial, as it reveals the vulnerability of systems widely adopted for collaborative LLM training. The paper effectively connects a known vulnerability (GIA) to a rising technique (PEFT) and highlights its specific weaknesses. The exploration of defenses, while not a primary focus, points towards future research directions.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   The problem is well-defined and highly relevant.
    *   ReCIT's design addresses the limitations of previous attacks.
    *   The experiments are comprehensive, covering various PEFT methods, model architectures, datasets, and batch sizes.
    *   The results are clearly presented and demonstrate ReCIT's effectiveness.
    *   The runtime analysis provides insights into the scalability of ReCIT.
    *   The ablation study effectively isolates the contributions of different components of ReCIT.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The attack relies on knowledge of the PEFT algorithm and batch size, which might not always be available. However, the paper addresses the practical aspects of this requirement.
    *   The PNote generation relies on synthetic data. While effective, it doesn't completely address the potential for unexpected or more subtle data leakage patterns in real-world scenarios.
    *   While the results are compelling, further investigation of real-world defenses against such attacks is warranted.

*   **Potential Influence:** This paper is likely to influence the field by:
    *   Raising awareness about the privacy risks of PEFT in federated learning.
    *   Motivating the development of more robust privacy-preserving techniques for PEFT.
    *   Providing a baseline for evaluating the security of federated learning systems.
    *   Stimulating further research on gradient inversion attacks against LLMs.

**Rigorous Rationale:**

The paper delivers a clear and concerning message: PEFT, as currently implemented, is vulnerable to data extraction attacks. The attack is convincingly demonstrated through extensive experiments, with clear advantages over baselines. The techniques proposed are novel and address practical challenges. The weakness of relying on knowing PEFT hyperparameters is acknowledged. While the reliance on synthetic PNote data is a limitation, this is common in this area, and can always be overcome by further work.

Score: 8

The score reflects the paper's significant contribution to understanding and addressing the privacy risks of PEFT. The novelty, comprehensive evaluation, and practical implications justify a high score. While the paper has some limitations, its strengths outweigh its weaknesses, making it an important contribution to the field.

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 8/10

### Reinforcement Learning for Reasoning in Large Language Models with One Training Example
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20571v1)
- **Authors**: Yiping Wang, Qing Yang, Zhiyuan Zeng, Liliang Ren, Lucas Liu, Baolin Peng, Hao Cheng, Xuehai He, Kuan Wang, Jianfeng Gao, Weizhu Chen, Shuohang Wang, Simon Shaolei Du, Yelong Shen
- **Abstract**: We show that reinforcement learning with verifiable reward using one training example (1-shot RLVR) is effective in incentivizing the math reasoning capabilities of large language models (LLMs). Applying RLVR to the base model Qwen2.5-Math-1.5B, we identify a single example that elevates model performance on MATH500 from 36.0% to 73.6%, and improves the average performance across six common mathematical reasoning benchmarks from 17.6% to 35.7%. This result matches the performance obtained using the 1.2k DeepScaleR subset (MATH500: 73.6%, average: 35.9%), which includes the aforementioned example. Similar substantial improvements are observed across various models (Qwen2.5-Math-7B, Llama3.2-3B-Instruct, DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Qwen-1.5B), RL algorithms (GRPO and PPO), and different math examples (many of which yield approximately 30% or greater improvement on MATH500 when employed as a single training example). In addition, we identify some interesting phenomena during 1-shot RLVR, including cross-domain generalization, increased frequency of self-reflection, and sustained test performance improvement even after the training accuracy has saturated, a phenomenon we term post-saturation generalization. Moreover, we verify that the effectiveness of 1-shot RLVR primarily arises from the policy gradient loss, distinguishing it from the "grokking" phenomenon. We also show the critical role of promoting exploration (e.g., by adding entropy loss with an appropriate coefficient) in 1-shot RLVR training. As a bonus, we observe that applying entropy loss alone, without any outcome reward, significantly enhances Qwen2.5-Math-1.5B's performance on MATH500 by 27.4%. These findings can inspire future work on RLVR data efficiency and encourage a re-examination of both recent progress and the underlying mechanisms in RLVR. Our code, model, and data are open source at https://github.com/ypwang61/One-Shot-RLVR
- **Summary**: ### Summary of the Paper The paper presents a novel approach to enhancing the mathematical reasoning capabilities of large language models (LLMs) through a method termed **1-shot reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (1-shot RLVR)**. The authors demonstrate that using a single training example significantly boosts the performance of the Qwen2.5-Math-1.5B model, raising its accuracy on the MATH500 benchmark from 36.0% to 73.6%. Additionally, the average performance over six common benchmarks improved from 17.6% to 35.7%. They found similar improvements across different models (including various architectures and sizes) and reinforcement learning algorithms (GRPO and PPO), indicating robustness in their approach. Key observations include the phenomena of **cross-domain generalization** and **post-saturation generalization**, where model performance improves even after training accuracy has plateaued. The authors identified that the policy gradient loss is critical to the success of 1-shot RLVR, distinguishing their method from traditional "grokking." Furthermore, they noted the importance of promoting exploration in training, particularly through adding entropy loss, which also enhanced model performance when used alone. Importantly, the authors make their code, model, and data publicly available to facilitate further research in this area. ### Evaluation of Novelty and Significance The paper makes a significant contribution to the field of reinforcement learning and LLMs in several important ways: 1. **Innovative Methodology**: The application of 1-shot RLVR is a fresh approach that leverages minimal training data to achieve substantial gains in model performance. This contrasts with traditional RL techniques that often require extensive datasets, highlighting a potential shift towards data-efficient learning in machine learning. 2. **Cross-Model Applicability**: Demonstrating efficacy across various models and reinforcement learning algorithms suggests that this methodological advancement could have broad applicability and influence on future research in mathematical reasoning with LLMs. 3. **Identification of New Phenomena**: The authors introduce intriguing concepts such as post-saturation generalization, which could spur additional inquiry into learning dynamics in neural networks—notably regarding how model behavior changes over different training regimes. However, there are some weaknesses: 1. **Lack of Extensive Comparisons**: While findings are promising, the paper could strengthen its arguments by providing more extensive comparisons with existing methods beyond what is stated. Insights from past techniques would help contextualize the importance of their improvements. 2. **Potential Overfitting**: Utilizing a single training example could raise concerns about overfitting, especially in complex tasks. The authors mention performance improvements but do not extensively discuss the robustness of these results in broader, more varied contexts. 3. **Clarification of Mechanisms**: While the paper notes several novel phenomena, it stops short of delivering a diagnostic understanding or theoretical underpinning for these observations. Further exploration into why these changes occur could enhance the paper's contributions. In conclusion, the novelty and significance of the contribution are substantial, as it challenges existing paradigms in model training with a focus on efficiency through reinforcement learning. The observations and opening up of new lines of inquiry can significantly impact the ongoing research in this area. Score: **8**
- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 8/10

### Information Retrieval in the Age of Generative AI: The RGB Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20610v1)
- **Authors**: Michele Garetto, Alessandro Cornacchia, Franco Galante, Emilio Leonardi, Alessandro Nordio, Alberto Tarable
- **Abstract**: The advent of Large Language Models (LLMs) and generative AI is fundamentally transforming information retrieval and processing on the Internet, bringing both great potential and significant concerns regarding content authenticity and reliability. This paper presents a novel quantitative approach to shed light on the complex information dynamics arising from the growing use of generative AI tools. Despite their significant impact on the digital ecosystem, these dynamics remain largely uncharted and poorly understood. We propose a stochastic model to characterize the generation, indexing, and dissemination of information in response to new topics. This scenario particularly challenges current LLMs, which often rely on real-time Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) techniques to overcome their static knowledge limitations. Our findings suggest that the rapid pace of generative AI adoption, combined with increasing user reliance, can outpace human verification, escalating the risk of inaccurate information proliferation across digital resources. An in-depth analysis of Stack Exchange data confirms that high-quality answers inevitably require substantial time and human effort to emerge. This underscores the considerable risks associated with generating persuasive text in response to new questions and highlights the critical need for responsible development and deployment of future generative AI tools.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Information Retrieval in the Age of Generative AI: The RGB Model":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel stochastic model (the "RGB model") to analyze the dynamics of information retrieval (IR) as generative AI (GAI) tools become more prevalent.  The model characterizes the generation, indexing, and dissemination of information, particularly focusing on new topics where LLMs often rely on Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG). The core idea is to represent answers as combinations of three primary colors (Red, Green, Blue) representing varying quality and diversity.  The model includes compartments representing external sources, the web, training sets, search engines, and LLMs, tracking the flow of information (represented as 'coupons') between them.  The authors use simulations to predict future scenarios and analyze metrics like the fraction of irrelevant used answers (FIUA) and AI responsibility index (AIRI). They complement the model with an analysis of Stack Exchange data to examine the temporal dynamics of high-quality answer emergence, noting the significant time and human effort required. The central finding suggests that rapid adoption of GAI can outpace human verification, increasing the risk of inaccurate information.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper presents a new, quantitative framework for understanding the complex interplay between information generation, retrieval, and dissemination in the age of GAI.  While compartmental models are not new in general (e.g., in epidemiology), its specific application to IR, particularly with the RGB color representation to quantify answer quality/diversity, and the integration of GAI elements (RAG, auto-phagy considerations), constitutes a novel contribution. The approach is unique in incorporating algorithmic and human behavioral aspects within the model.  The investigation of Stack Exchange data provides empirical support, adding to the novelty. However, the abstraction level of the RGB model means it oversimplifies real-world dynamics significantly.

**Significance:**  The paper addresses a timely and critical problem: the potential for GAI to negatively impact information quality and reliability. The proposed model provides a tool for exploring these risks and forecasting potential scenarios, which is valuable for researchers and policymakers.  The focus on "new topics" is also significant, as this is where LLMs are most vulnerable.  The analysis of Stack Exchange, highlighting the time and effort required to generate quality answers, reinforces the importance of human validation and careful deployment of GAI.

**Strengths:**

*   **Novel Framework:** The RGB model offers a unique approach to quantifying and analyzing information dynamics in the context of GAI.
*   **Relevance:** The paper addresses a highly relevant and pressing issue in the age of generative AI.
*   **Quantitative Approach:** The stochastic modeling allows for simulations and predictions, going beyond qualitative discussions.
*   **Empirical Validation:** The Stack Exchange analysis provides real-world evidence supporting the model's insights.
*   **Clear Problem Formulation:**  The paper clearly defines the problem and research questions.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Oversimplification:** The RGB color representation is a significant simplification of the complex characteristics of information quality and diversity. Representing answers with only three parameters limits the model's ability to capture nuanced aspects of information.
*   **Parameter Estimation:**  The paper acknowledges the difficulty of estimating parameter values for the model. The choice of parameters significantly impacts the simulation results, and lack of robust justification is concerning. This reduces the practical applicability of the quantitative forecasts.
*   **Limited Validation:** The validation is mainly focused on Stack Exchange, which, while useful, represents a specific type of online environment. It is unclear how well the model would generalize to other information ecosystems (e.g., news websites, social media platforms).
*   **Model Complexity:** While offering insights, the model involves numerous parameters and flows, which may make it challenging for others to replicate or extend.
*   **Lack of Comparative Baselines:** There's no direct comparison to other IR models or simulations that attempt to capture similar dynamics. Showing improvement upon established baselines could strengthen the claims.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper could influence research in the following areas:

*   Development of more sophisticated models for understanding information dynamics in the age of GAI.
*   Design of responsible GAI tools and policies.
*   Creation of better evaluation metrics for IR systems that incorporate GAI.
*   Awareness among users and developers about the potential risks associated with GAI.

Despite the valuable contribution, the significant limitations associated with model oversimplification and parameter estimation warrant a cautious assessment of its significance.

**Score: 6.5**

The paper provides a useful starting point and framework for understanding information dynamics and generative AI. However, the oversimplifications in the RGB model and reliance on arbitrary parameter choices limit the quantitative insights that can be gleaned. The qualitative insights are helpful, but the paper's overall impact is currently limited by the previously stated concerns. Further research is needed to refine the model and validate its predictions with more comprehensive data, moving toward better practical applications and robustness.

- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 6/10

### The Hidden Risks of LLM-Generated Web Application Code: A Security-Centric Evaluation of Code Generation Capabilities in Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20612v1)
- **Authors**: Swaroop Dora, Deven Lunkad, Naziya Aslam, S. Venkatesan, Sandeep Kumar Shukla
- **Abstract**: The rapid advancement of Large Language Models (LLMs) has enhanced software development processes, minimizing the time and effort required for coding and enhancing developer productivity. However, despite their potential benefits, code generated by LLMs has been shown to generate insecure code in controlled environments, raising critical concerns about their reliability and security in real-world applications. This paper uses predefined security parameters to evaluate the security compliance of LLM-generated code across multiple models, such as ChatGPT, DeepSeek, Claude, Gemini and Grok. The analysis reveals critical vulnerabilities in authentication mechanisms, session management, input validation and HTTP security headers. Although some models implement security measures to a limited extent, none fully align with industry best practices, highlighting the associated risks in automated software development. Our findings underscore that human expertise is crucial to ensure secure software deployment or review of LLM-generated code. Also, there is a need for robust security assessment frameworks to enhance the reliability of LLM-generated code in real-world applications.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

This paper investigates the security risks associated with web application code generated by Large Language Models (LLMs) like ChatGPT, DeepSeek, Claude, Gemini, and Grok. The authors create a security checklist and use it to evaluate the LLMs' generated code across several security parameters: authentication, input validation, session management, secure storage, error handling, and HTTP security headers.  The study reveals that while some models implement limited security measures, none fully align with industry best practices. The paper performs a risk assessment of LLM-generated code and concludes that human expertise remains crucial for ensuring secure software deployment. It suggests improvements to LLMs and promotes security testing.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Relevant and Timely:** The paper addresses a highly relevant and increasingly important issue: the security implications of AI-generated code. As LLMs become more prevalent in software development, understanding their security shortcomings is critical.
    *   **Systematic Approach:** The creation of a security checklist provides a structured framework for evaluating LLM-generated code. This allows for a more comprehensive and consistent analysis compared to ad-hoc evaluations.
    *   **Comparative Analysis:** Evaluating multiple LLMs provides valuable insights into the relative strengths and weaknesses of different models, which is helpful for developers choosing which tools to use.
    *   **Risk Assessment:** Including a risk assessment helps to quantify the potential impact of security vulnerabilities in LLM-generated code.
    *   **Practical Recommendations:** The recommendations for improving LLMs and securing generated code offer concrete steps for developers and LLM providers.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope of Applications:** The paper's focus is primarily on simple web application authentication and user management. While important, it doesn't address the security of code generated for more complex applications or other domains (e.g., cloud infrastructure, embedded systems). This limits the generalizability of the findings.
    *   **Prompt Dependence:**  The paper acknowledges the importance of prompts. However, the degree to which prompts influence the generation of secure code isn't explored exhaustively.  It is possible that with careful prompt engineering, LLMs could be guided to produce more secure code, which could change the conclusions. The robustness of the LLMs against adversarial prompts isn't thoroughly assessed.
    *   **Lack of Real-World Validation:** The analysis is primarily based on static code analysis. There is no testing to confirm the exploitability of the identified vulnerabilities. Real-world deployment and testing would provide a more realistic assessment of the security risks.
    *   **Subjectivity in Evaluation:** Applying the checklist can involve a degree of subjectivity, especially in determining whether a particular security parameter is "adequately" implemented. More precise, objectively verifiable criteria would strengthen the evaluation.
    *   **Limited Novelty of Findings:** Many of the identified vulnerabilities (e.g., lack of CSRF protection, missing HTTP security headers) are well-known web security issues. While the paper demonstrates that LLMs often fail to implement these protections, the fundamental vulnerabilities themselves are not new discoveries.
    * **The paper does not explain why those LLMs were chosen as opposed to others.**

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    The paper's primary contribution lies in its systematic evaluation of LLM-generated web application code using a predefined security checklist and risk assessment framework. While the individual security vulnerabilities identified are not novel, the application of a structured methodology to assess multiple LLMs and highlight their shortcomings in adhering to security best practices provides a valuable contribution. However, this novelty is only moderate. The field of AI-assisted software development is very active, with new papers frequently examining security aspects. It also draws heavily on existing security frameworks and best practices.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a worthwhile contribution by highlighting the security risks in LLM-generated code and providing a structured approach for evaluating different models. However, the limited scope of applications, the prompt dependence that requires further exploration, and the fact that the underlying security vulnerabilities are already well-known, restrict its overall novelty and impact. The absence of real-world validation also lowers the confidence in the conclusions.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 6/10

### DiffusionRIR: Room Impulse Response Interpolation using Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20625v1)
- **Authors**: Sagi Della Torre, Mirco Pezzoli, Fabio Antonacci, Sharon Gannot
- **Abstract**: Room Impulse Responses (RIRs) characterize acoustic environments and are crucial in multiple audio signal processing tasks. High-quality RIR estimates drive applications such as virtual microphones, sound source localization, augmented reality, and data augmentation. However, obtaining RIR measurements with high spatial resolution is resource-intensive, making it impractical for large spaces or when dense sampling is required. This research addresses the challenge of estimating RIRs at unmeasured locations within a room using Denoising Diffusion Probabilistic Models (DDPM). Our method leverages the analogy between RIR matrices and image inpainting, transforming RIR data into a format suitable for diffusion-based reconstruction. Using simulated RIR data based on the image method, we demonstrate our approach's effectiveness on microphone arrays of different curvatures, from linear to semi-circular. Our method successfully reconstructs missing RIRs, even in large gaps between microphones. Under these conditions, it achieves accurate reconstruction, significantly outperforming baseline Spline Cubic Interpolation in terms of Normalized Mean Square Error and Cosine Distance between actual and interpolated RIRs. This research highlights the potential of using generative models for effective RIR interpolation, paving the way for generating additional data from limited real-world measurements.
- **Summary**: **Summary of the Paper:** The paper titled "DiffusionRIR: Room Impulse Response Interpolation using Diffusion Models" addresses the challenge of estimating Room Impulse Responses (RIRs) in acoustic environments, which are essential for audio signal processing tasks like sound source localization and augmented reality. The authors propose a novel approach that utilizes Denoising Diffusion Probabilistic Models (DDPM) to interpolate RIRs at unmeasured locations, framing RIR interpolation as a problem analogous to image inpainting. The method is tested using simulated RIR data derived from the image method and demonstrates significant performance improvements over traditional Spline Cubic Interpolation, achieving better accuracy in reconstructing RIRs with large gaps between measurement locations. This research highlights the potential of leveraging generative models in the field of acoustic data interpolation, thus enabling more efficient data augmentation from limited real-world measurements. **Critical Evaluation:** **Novelty:**  The introduction of diffusion models to the task of RIR interpolation marks a notable shift in methodology. While the application of generative models is not entirely new in other fields, their specific application to RIR estimation and the analogy drawn to image inpainting is an innovative approach. This positions the work as a fresh contribution within the audio signal processing domain. **Significance:**  The significance of this research is underscored by its potential applications across various technological domains that rely on spatial audio and augmented reality systems. High-quality RIR interpolation could substantially reduce resource expenditure associated with dense sampling in large environments. However, the impact is somewhat tempered by existing methods, which, while less sophisticated, may still perform adequately in many practical applications. **Strengths:** 1. **Innovative Approach:** Leveraging diffusion models for RIR interpolation is a novel idea that could lead to new methodologies in the audio processing field. 2. **Performance Gains:** The paper reports significant improvements over existing interpolation techniques, providing a quantitative measure of its efficacy (improvements in Normalized Mean Square Error and Cosine Distance). 3. **Broad Applicability:** The results could benefit a range of applications in audio processing and AR/VR, appealing to both academic and industry audiences. **Weaknesses:** 1. **Dependence on Simulated Data:** The reliance on simulated RIR data, while understandable for early-stage research, raises questions about the robustness of results when applied to genuine real-world scenarios. The authors could potentially strengthen their case by including results from real RIR measurements. 2. **Complexity and Accessibility:** The application of diffusion models may introduce complexity that could be a barrier for practitioners who are not well-versed in advanced machine learning techniques. 3. **Generalizability:** The study primarily focuses on mic arrays of specific curvatures. The generalizability of the method to other configurations or more complex environments remains to be validated. In conclusion, while this paper offers a significant advance in the use of generative models for RIR interpolation, the limitations noted diminish its immediate applicability and necessitate further validation in real-world contexts. Overall, the research shows promise and could influence future work in the field, albeit with caution regarding its practical adoption. **Score: 7**
- **Classification**: cs.SD
- **Score**: 7/10

### Cooking Up Creativity: A Cognitively-Inspired Approach for Enhancing LLM Creativity through Structured Representations
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20643v1)
- **Authors**: Moran Mizrahi, Chen Shani, Gabriel Stanovsky, Dan Jurafsky, Dafna Shahaf
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) excel at countless tasks, yet struggle with creativity. In this paper, we introduce a novel approach that couples LLMs with structured representations and cognitively inspired manipulations to generate more creative and diverse ideas. Our notion of creativity goes beyond superficial token-level variations; rather, we explicitly recombine structured representations of existing ideas, allowing our algorithm to effectively explore the more abstract landscape of ideas. We demonstrate our approach in the culinary domain with DishCOVER, a model that generates creative recipes. Experiments comparing our model's results to those of GPT-4o show greater diversity. Domain expert evaluations reveal that our outputs, which are mostly coherent and feasible culinary creations, significantly surpass GPT-4o in terms of novelty, thus outperforming it in creative generation. We hope our work inspires further research into structured creativity in AI.
- **Summary**: **Summary:** The paper "Cooking Up Creativity: A Cognitively-Inspired Approach for Enhancing LLM Creativity through Structured Representations" addresses the creative limitations of Large Language Models (LLMs) by proposing a method that integrates structured representations and cognitive manipulations to enhance the generation of creative ideas. Focusing on the culinary domain, the authors introduce DishCOVER, a model designed to generate inventive recipes by recombining structured representations of existing concepts. Experimental results indicate that DishCOVER outperforms GPT-4o, showing greater diversity and novelty in recipe generation based on evaluations from domain experts. The findings emphasize the potential of structured approaches to foster creativity in AI systems and invite further exploration into structured creativity frameworks. **Critical Evaluation:** The novelty of this paper lies in its combination of LLMs with structured representations influenced by cognitive theories, which is a relatively underexplored avenue within AI creativity research. By moving beyond token-level variations, the authors introduce a method that could redefine how creative outputs are generated in AI systems, especially in fields requiring high creativity, like culinary arts. Strengths of the paper include: 1. **Innovative Approach:** The coupling of cognitive theory with LLMs is a fresh perspective that can inspire new methodologies in AI creativity. 2. **Experimental Validation:** The authors provide clear experimental comparisons that substantiate their claims, showcasing DishCOVER's superiority over an advanced LLM (GPT-4o). 3. **Potential for Broad Application:** While centered on culinary applications, the underlying concepts could be adapted for diverse fields, enhancing the relevance of the research. However, the paper does have its weaknesses: 1. **Specific Domain Focus:** The study is limited to the culinary domain, which may restrict generalizability across other creative tasks, reducing its overall applicability. 2. **Evaluation Criteria:** The criteria used for evaluating novelty and expert assessments might be subjective and vary across different expert panels, potentially affecting the robustness of the conclusions. 3. **Lack of Theoretical Depth:** While the practical implementation is highlighted, further discussion on the theoretical grounding of the cognitive manipulations utilized could strengthen the paper's scholarly contribution. In conclusion, while the paper demonstrates a promising approach to enhancing creativity in LLMs through structured representations, its focus on a niche application and potential limitations in evaluation criteria might limit its impact. Despite this, it significantly contributes to the ongoing discussion about structuring creativity in AI, making it a notable addition to the literature. **Score: 8**
- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Combatting Dimensional Collapse in LLM Pre-Training Data via Diversified File Selection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20644v1)
- **Authors**: Ziqing Fan, Siyuan Du, Shengchao Hu, Pingjie Wang, Li Shen, Ya Zhang, Dacheng Tao, Yanfeng Wang
- **Abstract**: Selecting high-quality pre-training data for large language models (LLMs) is crucial for enhancing their overall performance under limited computation budget, improving both training and sample efficiency. Recent advancements in file selection primarily rely on using an existing or trained proxy model to assess the similarity of samples to a target domain, such as high quality sources BookCorpus and Wikipedia. However, upon revisiting these methods, the domain-similarity selection criteria demonstrates a diversity dilemma, i.e.dimensional collapse in the feature space, improving performance on the domain-related tasks but causing severe degradation on generic performance. To prevent collapse and enhance diversity, we propose a DiverSified File selection algorithm (DiSF), which selects the most decorrelated text files in the feature space. We approach this with a classical greedy algorithm to achieve more uniform eigenvalues in the feature covariance matrix of the selected texts, analyzing its approximation to the optimal solution under a formulation of $\gamma$-weakly submodular optimization problem. Empirically, we establish a benchmark and conduct extensive experiments on the TinyLlama architecture with models from 120M to 1.1B parameters. Evaluating across nine tasks from the Harness framework, DiSF demonstrates a significant improvement on overall performance. Specifically, DiSF saves 98.5% of 590M training files in SlimPajama, outperforming the full-data pre-training within a 50B training budget, and achieving about 1.5x training efficiency and 5x data efficiency.
- **Summary**: ### Summary The paper "Combatting Dimensional Collapse in LLM Pre-Training Data via Diversified File Selection" addresses the challenge of selecting high-quality pre-training data for large language models (LLMs) to enhance their performance, particularly under limited computational resources. Traditional methods for file selection often rely on domain similarity, which can lead to a "diversity dilemma" or dimensional collapse, improving performance on domain-specific tasks at the cost of overall generic performance. To tackle this issue, the authors propose a novel algorithm called DiverSified File selection (DiSF), which focuses on selecting decorrelated text files in the feature space. This is achieved through a classical greedy algorithm that aims for more uniform eigenvalues in the feature covariance matrix. The authors validate their approach using extensive experiments with the TinyLlama architecture across various tasks, demonstrating significant performance improvements. Noteworthy results include a drastic reduction of 98.5% in the training dataset while outperforming the conventional full-data pre-training within a 50 billion training budget, achieving approximately 1.5 times training efficiency and 5 times data efficiency. ### Rigorous and Critical Evaluation #### Novelty The paper presents a significant and novel contribution by addressing the often-overlooked issue of dimensional collapse that arises from conventional file selection methods based on domain similarity. The introduction of DiSF, which emphasizes diversity over mere similarity, offers a fresh perspective on data selection strategies for LLM pre-training. This is an important topic, as LLMs require a diverse training set to generalize effectively. #### Strengths 1. **Innovative Approach**: The greedy algorithm for diversifying file selection is well-justified and clearly addresses the dimensional collapse issue. 2. **Empirical Validation**: The authors provide robust empirical evidence, showcasing improvements across multiple tasks with varying model sizes, which enhances the credibility of their claims. 3. **Efficiency Results**: Achievements in terms of data efficiency and training performance are impressive, suggesting practical implications for future LLM developments. #### Weaknesses 1. **Limited Benchmarking**: While the paper mentions a benchmark, details on the comparability with existing methods could be further elaborated to clarify the relative improvement. 2. **Scope of Tasks**: The evaluation across only nine tasks, although varying, may limit the generalizability of the findings. More diverse task evaluations could strengthen the arguments. 3. **Algorithm Complexity**: The greedy approach may also raise questions about scalability and computational complexity in extremely large datasets, which could present challenges in real-world applications. #### Influence on the Field The approach taken by the authors could catalyze discussions around the importance of diversity in training data for model robustness and generalizability. This could inspire further research into alternative selection strategies in LLM training, making the work relevant and potentially influential in advancing the understanding of effective data use in machine learning. ### Score: 8 The paper is rated an 8 due to its substantial novelty, solid empirical support, and practical implications within the field of LLM development. While it has its weaknesses, they do not detract significantly from the overall contribution and importance of the research. Higher scores would require broader benchmarking and exploration of the method's long-term viability across diverse applications.
- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 8/10

### LDPoly: Latent Diffusion for Polygonal Road Outline Extraction in Large-Scale Topographic Mapping
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20645v1)
- **Authors**: Weiqin Jiao, Hao Cheng, George Vosselman, Claudio Persello
- **Abstract**: Polygonal road outline extraction from high-resolution aerial images is an important task in large-scale topographic mapping, where roads are represented as vectorized polygons, capturing essential geometric features with minimal vertex redundancy. Despite its importance, no existing method has been explicitly designed for this task. While polygonal building outline extraction has been extensively studied, the unique characteristics of roads, such as branching structures and topological connectivity, pose challenges to these methods. To address this gap, we introduce LDPoly, the first dedicated framework for extracting polygonal road outlines from high-resolution aerial images. Our method leverages a novel Dual-Latent Diffusion Model with a Channel-Embedded Fusion Module, enabling the model to simultaneously generate road masks and vertex heatmaps. A tailored polygonization method is then applied to obtain accurate vectorized road polygons with minimal vertex redundancy. We evaluate LDPoly on a new benchmark dataset, Map2ImLas, which contains detailed polygonal annotations for various topographic objects in several Dutch regions. Our experiments include both in-region and cross-region evaluations, with the latter designed to assess the model's generalization performance on unseen regions. Quantitative and qualitative results demonstrate that LDPoly outperforms state-of-the-art polygon extraction methods across various metrics, including pixel-level coverage, vertex efficiency, polygon regularity, and road connectivity. We also design two new metrics to assess polygon simplicity and boundary smoothness. Moreover, this work represents the first application of diffusion models for extracting precise vectorized object outlines without redundant vertices from remote-sensing imagery, paving the way for future advancements in this field.
- **Summary**: ### Summary of the Paper The paper titled "LDPoly: Latent Diffusion for Polygonal Road Outline Extraction in Large-Scale Topographic Mapping" presents a novel framework called LDPoly aimed at extracting polygonal road outlines from high-resolution aerial imagery for topographic mapping. The authors note a significant gap in existing methods, which largely address polygonal building outlines but fail to adequately accommodate the unique characteristics of roads, such as branching structures and topological connectivity. LDPoly utilizes a Dual-Latent Diffusion Model coupled with a Channel-Embedded Fusion Module to simultaneously generate detailed road masks and vertex heatmaps. A specific polygonization technique ensures that the resulting vectorized road polygons maintain accuracy while minimizing redundant vertices. The performance of LDPoly is evaluated on a newly created benchmark dataset, Map2ImLas, which offers comprehensive annotations for various topographic elements across different regions in the Netherlands. Through both in-region and cross-region evaluations, the authors demonstrate that LDPoly consistently outperforms existing state-of-the-art methods across multiple metrics, including pixel-level coverage and vertex efficiency. Additionally, they propose two new evaluation metrics to measure polygon simplicity and boundary smoothness. Overall, LDPoly marks the inaugural application of diffusion models in the precise extraction of vectorized object outlines from remote-sensing imagery, setting a foundation for future developments in this domain. ### Critical Evaluation **Novelty and Significance:** 1. **Innovative Approach**: The introduction of LDPoly as the first dedicated framework for polygonal road extraction from aerial imagery represents a significant shift in methodology. The employment of a Dual-Latent Diffusion Model specifically tailored for this task shows a forward-thinking application of diffusion models which is generally underexplored in the context of road mapping. 2. **Addressing a Real Gap**: The authors effectively identify a gap in the existing literature regarding polygonal extraction techniques that focus on roads, which differ substantially from building outlines due to their structural complexity. This specificity highlights the necessity of LDPoly and its potential implications for enhancing topographic mapping accuracy. 3. **Comprehensive Evaluation**: By evaluating the model against a new dataset with intricate annotations and introducing new metrics to assess polygon attributes, the researchers contribute to a more nuanced understanding of the challenges in polygon extraction and how they can be quantified. **Strengths:** - The paper is well-structured, with a clear articulation of the problems it addresses. - It utilizes state-of-the-art technology in a novel context, which can pave the way for future research. - By providing both quantitative and qualitative results, the authors substantiate their claims about LDPoly’s efficacy. **Weaknesses:** - While the proposed method shows improved metrics, the paper could benefit from a more thorough discussion on the limitations and potential pitfalls of LDPoly, such as its performance in varying geographic and environmental contexts beyond the tested regions. - The introduction of new metrics, though beneficial, raises questions about the validity and reliability of these measures in different scenarios, which the authors do not fully address. **Potential Influence on the Field:** The method presented in this paper promises to influence the field of remote sensing and geographic information systems significantly by providing a reliable framework for ongoing topographic mapping efforts. The key innovation here is not just the method itself but also the dataset and metrics which could spur further research into related areas of study, such as urban planning and disaster management. Considering all these factors, the paper makes a compelling case for its contribution to the field of computer vision and geospatial analysis. Its innovative approach and positive results lead to a high assessment of its novelty and significance. **Score: 8**
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### ComplexVCoder: An LLM-Driven Framework for Systematic Generation of Complex Verilog Code
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20653v1)
- **Authors**: Jian Zuo, Junzhe Liu, Xianyong Wang, Yicheng Liu, Navya Goli, Tong Xu, Hao Zhang, Umamaheswara Rao Tida, Zhenge Jia, Mengying Zhao
- **Abstract**: Recent advances have demonstrated the promising capabilities of large language models (LLMs) in generating register-transfer level (RTL) code, such as Verilog. However, existing LLM-based frameworks still face significant challenges in accurately handling the complexity of real-world RTL designs, particularly those that are large-scale and involve multi-level module instantiations. To address this issue, we present ComplexVCoder, an open-source LLM-driven framework that enhances both the generation quality and efficiency of complex Verilog code. Specifically, we introduce a two-stage generation mechanism, which leverages an intermediate representation to enable a more accurate and structured transition from natural language descriptions to intricate Verilog designs. In addition, we introduce a rule-based alignment method and a domain-specific retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) to further improve the correctness of the synthesized code by incorporating relevant design knowledge during generation. To evaluate our approach, we construct a comprehensive dataset comprising 55 complex Verilog designs derived from real-world implementations. We also release an open-source benchmark suite for systematically assessing the quality of auto-generated RTL code together with the ComplexVCoder framework. Experimental results show that ComplexVCoder outperforms SOTA frameworks such as CodeV and RTLCoder by 14.6% and 22.2%, respectively, in terms of function correctness on complex Verilog benchmarks. Furthermore, ComplexVcoder achieves comparable generation performances in terms of functionality correctness using a lightweight 32B model (Qwen2.5), rivaling larger-scale models such as GPT-3.5 and DeepSeek-V3.
- **Summary**: ### Summary: The paper presents ComplexVCoder, an innovative open-source framework designed to enhance the generation of complex Verilog code using large language models (LLMs). While current LLM frameworks struggle with accurately generating complex register-transfer level (RTL) designs, ComplexVCoder employs a two-stage generation mechanism that utilizes an intermediate representation to improve the transition from natural language to detailed Verilog implementations. The framework also implements a rule-based alignment method and a domain-specific retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) to refine the accuracy of the produced code by incorporating relevant design knowledge. The authors constructed a dataset of 55 complex Verilog designs from real-world examples to evaluate their methodology, demonstrating that ComplexVCoder surpasses existing frameworks like CodeV and RTLCoder in function correctness, achieving improvements of 14.6% and 22.2%, respectively. Notably, it also shows competitive performance with smaller model sizes, such as the 32B Qwen2.5, against larger models like GPT-3.5. ### Evaluation: **Novelty:** The introduction of a two-stage mechanism with an intermediate representation is relatively novel in the context of LLM-driven code generation, particularly for handling complex RTL designs. The combination of RAG with rule-based alignment also adds a layer of sophistication to the approach, suggesting a thoughtful integration of established principles into a modern context. **Significance:** This contribution is significant as it addresses a real gap in the existing literature regarding the challenges associated with generating complex Verilog designs. By releasing an open-source framework and benchmark suite, the authors foster a collaborative environment for future research, which can facilitate advancements in the field. **Strengths:** 1. **Innovative Approach**: The two-stage generation process and the incorporation of design knowledge are strong points that address previous limitations. 2. **Comprehensive Evaluation**: The inclusion of a robust dataset derived from real-world applications bolsters the validity of the experimental results. 3. **Open-source Contribution**: The provision of both the framework and the benchmark suite encourages further exploration and replication of results within the community. **Weaknesses:** 1. **Scope of Evaluation**: Although the paper claims significant improvements, it would benefit from a broader evaluation that includes more diverse or challenging design cases. 2. **Comparison with a Wider Range of Existing Solutions**: While comparisons are made with a few frameworks, examining additional state-of-the-art methods could help contextualize the performance claims more thoroughly. **Overall Impact:** The paper contributes meaningful advancements to the field of automated code generation for hardware design, particularly in managing complexity. Its approaches and findings seem poised to influence future research directions, especially in the realm of RTL design and LLM applications. Given the balance of novelty, strength, shortcomings, and the impact it potentially holds for the future, I would assign this paper a score of **8**. This score reflects its innovative contributions and practical implications, while acknowledging the need for broader application and comparison to fully substantiate its claims. **Score: 8**
- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 8/10

### SFi-Former: Sparse Flow Induced Attention for Graph Transformer
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20666v1)
- **Authors**: Zhonghao Li, Ji Shi, Xinming Zhang, Miao Zhang, Bo Li
- **Abstract**: Graph Transformers (GTs) have demonstrated superior performance compared to traditional message-passing graph neural networks in many studies, especially in processing graph data with long-range dependencies. However, GTs tend to suffer from weak inductive bias, overfitting and over-globalizing problems due to the dense attention. In this paper, we introduce SFi-attention, a novel attention mechanism designed to learn sparse pattern by minimizing an energy function based on network flows with l1-norm regularization, to relieve those issues caused by dense attention. Furthermore, SFi-Former is accordingly devised which can leverage the sparse attention pattern of SFi-attention to generate sparse network flows beyond adjacency matrix of graph data. Specifically, SFi-Former aggregates features selectively from other nodes through flexible adaptation of the sparse attention, leading to a more robust model. We validate our SFi-Former on various graph datasets, especially those graph data exhibiting long-range dependencies. Experimental results show that our SFi-Former obtains competitive performance on GNN Benchmark datasets and SOTA performance on LongRange Graph Benchmark (LRGB) datasets. Additionally, our model gives rise to smaller generalization gaps, which indicates that it is less prone to over-fitting. Click here for codes.
- **Summary**: **Summary of the Paper:** The paper introduces SFi-Former, a novel architecture for Graph Transformers (GTs) that addresses the issues of dense attention mechanisms, such as weak inductive bias and susceptibility to overfitting. The authors propose SFi-attention, which employs an energy minimization strategy based on network flows with l1-norm regularization to learn sparse attention patterns. This mechanism allows SFi-Former to aggregate features from other nodes selectively, enhancing model robustness, particularly for graphs with long-range dependencies. The effectiveness of SFi-Former is demonstrated through experiments on various graph datasets, showcasing competitive performance on GNN Benchmark datasets and superior results on LongRange Graph Benchmark (LRGB) datasets, while also exhibiting reduced overfitting. **Critical Evaluation:** Novelty: The introduction of SFi-attention is a notable contribution, as it addresses key shortcomings of existing GTs related to dense attention. By leveraging sparse attention patterns derived from network flows, the paper presents a fresh perspective in the search for improved graph representation learning. However, the field has seen other attempts at introducing sparsity into attention mechanisms. Thus, while the approach is innovative, its distinctiveness might not be substantial when viewed against the broader landscape of sparse attention methodologies. Significance: The results reported in the paper demonstrate improved performance on benchmark datasets, especially on graphs characterized by long-range dependencies, which are often challenging to model due to complex relational structures. The reduction in generalization gaps indicates practical applicability and robustness, which are critical for advancing graph representation learning in real-world applications. However, the paper could have expanded its discussion on the implications of these findings within the context of existing literature, thus strengthening its significance. Strengths: 1. **Innovative Approach**: Introducing a sparsity-driven attention mechanism that shows promise in addressing the overfitting and globalizing issues in GTs. 2. **Robust Validation**: Comprehensive evaluations on various datasets lend credence to the effectiveness of the proposed model. Weaknesses: 1. **Limited Novelty Against Existing Techniques**: While the idea is interesting, it is not entirely ground-breaking in the context of existing sparse attention models. 2. **Theoretical Justification**: Further theoretical insights into the advantages of network flow-based sparsity could provide stronger grounding for the method. In conclusion, while the paper presents a valuable contribution with practical improvements in graph neural network performance, its impact may be somewhat muted by the presence of similar approaches in the literature. The novelty lies primarily in the specific application of network flows to attention sparsity. Thus, I would assign a score of 7. **Score: 7**
- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### A Generative-AI-Driven Claim Retrieval System Capable of Detecting and Retrieving Claims from Social Media Platforms in Multiple Languages
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20668v1)
- **Authors**: Ivan Vykopal, Martin Hyben, Robert Moro, Michal Gregor, Jakub Simko
- **Abstract**: Online disinformation poses a global challenge, placing significant demands on fact-checkers who must verify claims efficiently to prevent the spread of false information. A major issue in this process is the redundant verification of already fact-checked claims, which increases workload and delays responses to newly emerging claims. This research introduces an approach that retrieves previously fact-checked claims, evaluates their relevance to a given input, and provides supplementary information to support fact-checkers. Our method employs large language models (LLMs) to filter irrelevant fact-checks and generate concise summaries and explanations, enabling fact-checkers to faster assess whether a claim has been verified before. In addition, we evaluate our approach through both automatic and human assessments, where humans interact with the developed tool to review its effectiveness. Our results demonstrate that LLMs are able to filter out many irrelevant fact-checks and, therefore, reduce effort and streamline the fact-checking process.
- **Summary**: ### Summary of the Paper The paper addresses the significant issue of online disinformation, highlighting the challenges faced by fact-checkers in efficiently verifying claims across social media platforms. It proposes a generative AI-driven claim retrieval system that utilizes large language models (LLMs) to filter out irrelevant prior fact-checks, assess their relevance regarding new claims, and generate concise summaries for quicker human evaluation. The tool is designed to minimize redundant efforts in verifying previously fact-checked claims, thus speeding up response times in the fact-checking process. The effectiveness of the system is evaluated via both automated metrics and human assessments, showing promise in reducing the workload for fact-checkers and enhancing the overall efficiency of claim verification. ### Evaluation of Novelty and Significance **Strengths:** 1. **Relevance:** The paper tackles a pressing global issue—online disinformation—making its contribution highly relevant to the current landscape. 2. **Innovative Approach:** The use of LLMs for filtering and retrieving previously fact-checked claims is a fresh application that has potential for significant impact, given the vast amounts of data shared on social media. 3. **Empirical Validation:** The dual approach of using both automated and human assessments to validate the tool’s effectiveness strengthens its credibility and reliability. **Weaknesses:** 1. **Limited Scope:** The paper may not fully address challenges related to multi-language support or varying levels of digital literacy among fact-checkers that could affect the system's usability. 2. **Potential Biases in LLMs:** The heavy reliance on LLMs raises questions about biases inherent in the models, which could affect fact-checking outcomes and fairness. 3. **Generalizability:** The specifics of how the system performs across different platforms and languages are not deeply explored, which could limit its wide applicability. **Impact and Influence on the Field:** The integration of AI tools into the fact-checking process has the potential to transform how misinformation is managed online. By reducing redundant work and streamlining the retrieval of verified claims, the system could facilitate quicker responses and more accurate debunking of false information. **Score Justification:** While the approach presented is innovative and relevant, it also demonstrates limitations in addressing broader contextual issues of implementation and potential AI biases. Nevertheless, its practical applicability and the empirical evaluation conducted provide a strong foundation for future research in this space. This leads to a balanced score reflecting the significant strides made by the paper, along with the areas needing more exploration. **Score: 7**
- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Advance Fake Video Detection via Vision Transformers
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20669v1)
- **Authors**: Joy Battocchio, Stefano Dell'Anna, Andrea Montibeller, Giulia Boato
- **Abstract**: Recent advancements in AI-based multimedia generation have enabled the creation of hyper-realistic images and videos, raising concerns about their potential use in spreading misinformation. The widespread accessibility of generative techniques, which allow for the production of fake multimedia from prompts or existing media, along with their continuous refinement, underscores the urgent need for highly accurate and generalizable AI-generated media detection methods, underlined also by new regulations like the European Digital AI Act. In this paper, we draw inspiration from Vision Transformer (ViT)-based fake image detection and extend this idea to video. We propose an {original} %innovative framework that effectively integrates ViT embeddings over time to enhance detection performance. Our method shows promising accuracy, generalization, and few-shot learning capabilities across a new, large and diverse dataset of videos generated using five open source generative techniques from the state-of-the-art, as well as a separate dataset containing videos produced by proprietary generative methods.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel approach for detecting AI-generated fake videos using Vision Transformers (ViTs).  It builds upon existing work in ViT-based fake image detection and extends it to video by integrating ViT embeddings over time to capture temporal information. The core contributions include a new video fake detection framework leveraging a pre-trained ViT for video captioning, a classification head to refine the ViT features, and a new large-scale and diverse dataset called VideoDiffusion, containing videos generated using multiple open-source generative techniques and levels of compression. The authors conduct experiments comparing their method against state-of-the-art techniques, demonstrating promising accuracy, generalization, and few-shot learning capabilities, especially regarding resilience to H.264 compression and generalization across different generative pipelines.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a relatively incremental, though practically valuable, advance. Applying ViTs to video fake detection isn't entirely new, and the idea of leveraging a pre-trained ViT for video captioning to extract features has precedents in fake image detection (adapting models like CLIP). The primary novelty lies in the specific architecture chosen (video captioning ViT, combined with a classifier using learned prototypes) and the creation of the VideoDiffusion dataset. The temporal awareness aspect, while claimed, could have been stronger, as it's implemented through a relatively simple frame sequence processing. It's more of an efficient exploitation of pre-trained representation capabilities rather than a genuinely new way to handle temporal information.
*   **Significance:** The significance of the paper is mainly in its practical contributions. The VideoDiffusion dataset is a valuable resource for the community, addressing the scarcity of large-scale, diverse datasets for fake video detection. The empirical results demonstrate improved generalization and robustness to compression compared to some existing methods, which are important for real-world applications. However, the architectural contribution is less significant as it is a relatively straightforward extension and combination of existing concepts. The insights regarding the challenges in detecting SEINE-generated videos are also interesting.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Dataset:** The creation of the VideoDiffusion dataset is a major strength. It is large, diverse, and addresses a key limitation in the field.
    *   **Empirical Evaluation:** The paper includes a comprehensive evaluation with comparisons against SoA methods and analysis across various compression levels, generative techniques, and a disjoint dataset.
    *   **Generalization:** The experiments demonstrate better generalization to unseen generative pipelines and compression levels compared to some existing methods.
    *   **Few-Shot Learning:** The study of few-shot learning capabilities highlights the potential of the approach for adapting to new types of fake videos with limited training data.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Incremental Architectural Novelty:** The proposed architecture is an adaptation and combination of existing techniques, rather than a groundbreaking innovation. The claim of a "temporally aware" approach could be stronger.
    *   **Limited Ablation Studies:** The paper could benefit from more ablation studies to assess the contribution of individual components of the proposed framework (e.g., the learned prototypes).
    *   **Lack of deeper Explanations:** While better TPR and TNR are achieved, there aren't strong explanations as to *why* this is achieved beyond claims regarding high level semantic analysis.
*   **Impact on the Field:**  The paper contributes to the growing body of work on AI-generated media detection. The VideoDiffusion dataset will likely be used by other researchers in the field. The findings regarding the effectiveness of ViT-based approaches and the challenges in detecting certain types of generative models can inform future research directions. However, the relatively incremental architectural contribution limits its overall impact.

**Score: 6.5/10**

**Justification:** The paper offers valuable practical contributions through the VideoDiffusion dataset and the empirical demonstration of improved generalization and robustness to compression. The few-shot learning results are also promising. However, the architectural novelty is limited, and the theoretical contributions are somewhat incremental. The lack of deeper ablations and justifications slightly detracts from the overall impact. While a solid contribution to the field, it doesn't represent a major breakthrough. The score of 6.5 reflects a valuable contribution, but with room for more architectural innovation and in-depth analysis. The dataset is the main reason the score is above a 6.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### CoCo-Bench: A Comprehensive Code Benchmark For Multi-task Large Language Model Evaluation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20673v1)
- **Authors**: Wenjing Yin, Tianze Sun, Yijiong Yu, Jiawei Fang, Guangyao Su, Jiancheng Wang, Zekun Wang, Wei Wang, Ran Chen, Ziyun Dai, Shuai Yuan, Menghang Dong, Peng Luo, Dong Cao, Da Lei, Yajun Zhang, Hao Chen, Xiang Ma, Yong Liu, Weifeng Liu, Yuanjian Xu, Ji Pei
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) play a crucial role in software engineering, excelling in tasks like code generation and maintenance. However, existing benchmarks are often narrow in scope, focusing on a specific task and lack a comprehensive evaluation framework that reflects real-world applications. To address these gaps, we introduce CoCo-Bench (Comprehensive Code Benchmark), designed to evaluate LLMs across four critical dimensions: code understanding, code generation, code modification, and code review. These dimensions capture essential developer needs, ensuring a more systematic and representative evaluation. CoCo-Bench includes multiple programming languages and varying task difficulties, with rigorous manual review to ensure data quality and accuracy. Empirical results show that CoCo-Bench aligns with existing benchmarks while uncovering significant variations in model performance, effectively highlighting strengths and weaknesses. By offering a holistic and objective evaluation, CoCo-Bench provides valuable insights to guide future research and technological advancements in code-oriented LLMs, establishing a reliable benchmark for the field.
