- **Score**: 7/10

### Chain-of-Defensive-Thought: Structured Reasoning Elicits Robustness in Large Language Models against Reference Corruption
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20769v1)
- **Authors**: Wenxiao Wang, Parsa Hosseini, Soheil Feizi
- **Abstract**: Chain-of-thought prompting has demonstrated great success in facilitating the reasoning abilities of large language models. In this work, we explore how these enhanced reasoning abilities can be exploited to improve the robustness of large language models in tasks that are not necessarily reasoning-focused. In particular, we show how a wide range of large language models exhibit significantly improved robustness against reference corruption using a simple method called chain-of-defensive-thought, where only a few exemplars with structured and defensive reasoning are provided as demonstrations. Empirically, the improvements can be astounding, especially given the simplicity and applicability of the method. For example, in the Natural Questions task, the accuracy of GPT-4o degrades from 60% to as low as 3% with standard prompting when 1 out of 10 references provided is corrupted with prompt injection attacks. In contrast, GPT-4o using chain-of-defensive-thought prompting maintains an accuracy of 50%.
- **Summary**: **Summary of the Paper:** The paper titled "Chain-of-Defensive-Thought: Structured Reasoning Elicits Robustness in Large Language Models against Reference Corruption" investigates the application of chain-of-thought prompting to enhance the resilience of large language models (LLMs) against specific types of data corruption known as reference corruption. This approach employs "chain-of-defensive-thought," which involves providing structured and defensive reasoning through a few well-chosen exemplars. The authors demonstrate significant improvements in robustness across various LLMs, showcasing the effectiveness of their method particularly in the Natural Questions task. For instance, while standard prompting severely decreases the accuracy of models under attack conditions (from 60% to 3%), the chain-of-defensive-thought method allows models like GPT-4o to retain a notable accuracy level of 50%. **Critical Evaluation:** 1. **Novelty:** The concept of using structured reasoning to improve model robustness is indeed a novel approach, as most techniques focus on enhancing reasoning abilities separately from robustness. Here, the authors link two critical aspects of LLM performance, establishing a new paradigm in tackling reference corruption problems. 2. **Significance:** The paper addresses a pressing issue within the field concerning the reliability and resilience of LLMs when faced with corrupted inputs. By presenting a simple yet effective method that can be broadly applied, the findings could have substantial implications for the deployment of LLMs in real-world applications where data integrity is crucial. 3. **Strengths:**    - The empirical results demonstrate a clear improvement in model performance, signifying that structured reasoning can play a vital role in countering input corruption.    - The method outlined is straightforward and easy to implement, which could encourage adoption in various LLM-based applications.    - The study contributes to the understanding of LLM vulnerabilities and suggests practical solutions, thereby enriching the discourse on model robustness. 4. **Weaknesses:**    - The paper may require a deeper exploration of the underlying mechanisms that explain why and how structured reasoning enhances robustness, particularly in a broader array of tasks beyond the ones studied.    - The generalizability of the findings remains an open question. It would be beneficial to assess the performance across different model architectures and diverse types of corruption beyond reference attacks.    - The reliance on only a few exemplars for demonstration may raise questions about the scalability and adaptability of the solution in more complex real-world settings. In light of these considerations, I assign this paper a score of **8**. It presents a significant contribution to the field by integrating reasoning with robustness while remaining accessible for implementation. However, further exploration into broader applications and the principles behind the observed improvements would enhance its completeness and impact. **Score: 8**
- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### JTreeformer: Graph-Transformer via Latent-Diffusion Model for Molecular Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20770v1)
- **Authors**: Ji Shi, Chengxun Xie, Zhonghao Li, Xinming Zhang, Miao Zhang
- **Abstract**: The discovery of new molecules based on the original chemical molecule distributions is of great importance in medicine. The graph transformer, with its advantages of high performance and scalability compared to traditional graph networks, has been widely explored in recent research for applications of graph structures. However, current transformer-based graph decoders struggle to effectively utilize graph information, which limits their capacity to leverage only sequences of nodes rather than the complex topological structures of molecule graphs. This paper focuses on building a graph transformer-based framework for molecular generation, which we call \textbf{JTreeformer} as it transforms graph generation into junction tree generation. It combines GCN parallel with multi-head attention as the encoder. It integrates a directed acyclic GCN into a graph-based Transformer to serve as a decoder, which can iteratively synthesize the entire molecule by leveraging information from the partially constructed molecular structure at each step. In addition, a diffusion model is inserted in the latent space generated by the encoder, to enhance the efficiency and effectiveness of sampling further. The empirical results demonstrate that our novel framework outperforms existing molecule generation methods, thus offering a promising tool to advance drug discovery (https://anonymous.4open.science/r/JTreeformer-C74C).
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "JTreeformer: Graph-Transformer via Latent-Diffusion Model for Molecular Generation":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces JTreeformer, a novel framework for molecular generation that combines graph transformers and latent diffusion models. It aims to improve the generation of valid and diverse molecules by addressing the limitations of existing transformer-based graph decoders in utilizing graph information.  JTreeformer transforms graph generation into junction tree generation. It uses a hybrid encoder (GCNs and Transformers) to learn robust molecular representations, a directed acyclic GCN (DAGCN) paralleled with masked multi-head attention as a decoder to iteratively synthesize molecules, and a latent diffusion model to enhance sampling efficiency.  The authors demonstrate through experiments that JTreeformer outperforms existing methods in molecular generation tasks, potentially offering a valuable tool for drug discovery.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper demonstrates novelty in several aspects:

    *   **Hybrid Encoder:** The integration of GCNs and Transformers in the encoder to capture both local and global graph information is a reasonable and potentially effective approach.
    *   **DAGCN Decoder:** The use of a directed acyclic GCN (DAGCN) within a transformer decoder for iterative molecular synthesis is a core contribution.  DAGCN addresses a key limitation of standard GCNs when used in a generative setting, by explicitly modeling the acyclic nature of node prediction sequences during graph generation.
    *   **Latent Diffusion Model:** The introduction of a latent diffusion model (LDM) in the latent space of molecular generation is a significant contribution. By replacing random sampling with a structured denoising process, the LDM likely enhances the stability and validity of generated molecular structures. This aspect resonates with the broader trend of using diffusion models for generative tasks.
    *   **Overall Architecture:** The integration of these components into a cohesive encoder-decoder framework is novel.

*   **Significance:**

    *   **Performance Improvement:** The experimental results suggest that JTreeformer outperforms existing molecule generation methods on standard datasets. This is important for demonstrating the practical utility of the proposed framework.
    *   **Addressing Limitations:** The paper targets significant limitations of previous approaches, such as the difficulties in capturing long-range dependencies in graphs and the challenges of generating chemically valid molecules.
    *   **Potential Impact:** Successful molecule generation tools have the potential to significantly impact drug discovery and materials science by enabling the design of novel compounds with desired properties.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the problems with existing methods and motivates the need for a new approach.
    *   **Well-Defined Architecture:** The architecture of JTreeformer is well-defined and explained, with clear descriptions of the individual components and their interactions.
    *   **Comprehensive Experiments:** The experiments are comprehensive, including comparisons with multiple baselines, ablation studies, and visualizations of the latent space.
    *   **Validation of Concepts:**  The inclusion of the diffusion model as a latent constraint to molecular validity is a smart direction and concept.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Computational Cost:**  The paper does not explicitly discuss the computational cost of JTreeformer compared to simpler baselines. Training diffusion models and transformers can be computationally expensive, which might limit the scalability of the approach.
    *   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** It's possible that the performance of JTreeformer is sensitive to the choice of hyperparameters, especially related to the training of the diffusion model. Further investigation into robustness and sensitivity to hyperparameters could improve the paper.
    *   **Specificity of Improvements:** While the paper demonstrates improved performance, the specific reasons *why* JTreeformer performs better could be investigated more deeply. Are the improvements primarily due to the DAGCN decoder, the diffusion model, or the combination of both?
    *   **MCTS Details**: While MCTS is mentioned for isomer selection, the specific implementation and reward function used are lacking, which would be needed for reproducibility.

*   **Rigorous Rationale:**

    *   The paper provides a strong rationale for the score assigned by providing details about the individual components that led to the overall performance, but lacks a deeper dive into why certain metrics improved. While the framework shows impressive results, further investigation into hyperparameter sensitivity, computational costs, and a clear breakdown of the individual contributions of the components is needed to further solidify the significance of the paper. While the individual components of the proposed model are well established, the novel aspect of leveraging a latent diffusion constraint is very beneficial to the overall outcome of valid and reliable molecular generation, which is important to note.

**Score: 7**

The JTreeformer presents a novel architecture that effectively integrates graph transformers and latent diffusion models for molecular generation, demonstrating performance improvements and providing valuable insights. Further exploration into specific components and the costs will refine the significance of the work.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Turing Machine Evaluation for Large Language Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20771v1)
- **Authors**: Haitao Wu, Zongbo Han, Huaxi Huang, Changqing Zhang
- **Abstract**: With the rapid development and widespread application of Large Language Models (LLMs), rigorous evaluation has become particularly crucial. This research adopts a novel perspective, focusing on evaluating the core computational reasoning ability of LLMs, defined as the capacity of model to accurately understand rules, and execute logically computing operations. This capability assesses the reliability of LLMs as precise executors, and is critical to advanced tasks such as complex code generation and multi-step problem-solving. We propose an evaluation framework based on Universal Turing Machine (UTM) simulation. This framework requires LLMs to strictly follow instructions and track dynamic states, such as tape content and read/write head position, during multi-step computations. To enable standardized evaluation, we developed TMBench, a benchmark for systematically studying the computational reasoning capabilities of LLMs. TMBench provides several key advantages, including knowledge-agnostic evaluation, adjustable difficulty, foundational coverage through Turing machine encoding, and unlimited capacity for instance generation, ensuring scalability as models continue to evolve. We find that model performance on TMBench correlates strongly with performance on other recognized reasoning benchmarks (Pearson correlation coefficient is 0.73), clearly demonstrating that computational reasoning is a significant dimension for measuring the deep capabilities of LLMs. Code and data are available at https://github.com/HaitaoWuTJU/Turing-Machine-Bench.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Turing Machine Evaluation for Large Language Models":

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a new benchmark, TMBench, to evaluate the computational reasoning capabilities of large language models (LLMs). Unlike existing benchmarks that often conflate knowledge retrieval with reasoning, TMBench focuses on the ability of LLMs to accurately follow formal rules and execute multi-step computational operations using Turing machine simulation. It involves simulating m-Tag systems, a simplified version of Turing machines, and requires LLMs to track dynamic states (tape content, read/write head position) during computation. The authors argue that this approach provides a knowledge-agnostic, adjustable, foundational, and scalable way to assess LLM's core reasoning abilities. They evaluate several open-source and proprietary LLMs using TMBench and find a high correlation between TMBench performance and standard reasoning benchmarks, which highlights the practical relevance of this approach. The paper also presents ablation studies that demonstrate the robustness and scalability of the proposed benchmark.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper introduces a genuinely novel approach to evaluating LLMs by focusing on their ability to simulate Turing machines. This perspective differs from many existing benchmarks, which primarily test knowledge recall and comprehension, which is a new way to evaluate reasoning within LLMs.
*   **Knowledge-Agnostic:** TMBench effectively eliminates the need for external knowledge, allowing a more focused assessment of the model's ability to follow instructions and perform deterministic computations. This is important as it helps isolating reasoning from knowledge.
*   **Adjustable Difficulty:** The benchmark's difficulty can be adjusted by modifying the rule sets, input lengths, and other parameters, making it suitable for evaluating models of varying capabilities.
*   **Foundational Coverage:** The reliance on Turing machine simulation provides a foundationally sound approach, as any computable function can be encoded as a Turing machine.
*   **Scalability:** The benchmark can generate an unlimited number of instances, ensuring scalability as LLMs continue to evolve.
*   **Strong Correlation:** The observed correlation between TMBench performance and existing reasoning benchmarks provides empirical support for its relevance.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Real-World Applicability:** While evaluating core computational reasoning is valuable, the direct application of TMBench to solving real-world problems is not immediately obvious. A stronger argument relating the performance on the benchmark to performance in tasks requiring complex and multi-step reasoning would be beneficial.
*   **Computational Cost:** Simulating Turing machines, especially for longer sequences of steps, can be computationally intensive, potentially limiting the scalability of the benchmark for resource-constrained environments.
*   **Oversimplification:** The 2-Tag system, while Turing-complete, is a relatively simplified model of computation. It might not fully capture the nuances and complexities of real-world reasoning tasks.
*   **Ablation Results:** The discussion around some of the ablation studies, especially regarding alphabet choice, would benefit from more analysis of *why* particular choices lead to performance differences. A more theoretical explanation of these changes would be beneficial.
*   **Benchmark design:** The success of the benchmark is heavily reliant on the specific design choices of the m-Tag system and the method of instance generation. The paper would benefit from a more thorough discussion of the rationale behind these choices and the potential impact on the results. It is likely that this is a local maxima of usefulness.

**Significance:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution by offering a new and well-defined approach to assess core reasoning in LLMs. This approach can help researchers better understand the underlying capabilities of these models and how to improve them. By isolating the computational aspect of reasoning, TMBench enables a more targeted evaluation and development of reasoning abilities in LLMs. It pushes the field to think more about the fundamental requirements for true general-purpose LLMs.

**Overall Score:**

Score: 7

**Rationale:**

The paper's novelty and technical soundness are significant strengths, and the presented benchmark has the potential to become a useful tool for the research community. However, the limited real-world applicability and the potential for oversimplification are notable weaknesses. A score of 7 reflects the significant contribution while acknowledging the need for further exploration and refinement to broaden the impact of this work.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Using LLMs in Generating Design Rationale for Software Architecture Decisions
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20781v1)
- **Authors**: Xiyu Zhou, Ruiyin Li, Peng Liang, Beiqi Zhang, Mojtaba Shahin, Zengyang Li, Chen Yang
- **Abstract**: Design Rationale (DR) for software architecture decisions refers to the reasoning underlying architectural choices, which provides valuable insights into the different phases of the architecting process throughout software development. However, in practice, DR is often inadequately documented due to a lack of motivation and effort from developers. With the recent advancements in Large Language Models (LLMs), their capabilities in text comprehension, reasoning, and generation may enable the generation and recovery of DR for architecture decisions. In this study, we evaluated the performance of LLMs in generating DR for architecture decisions. First, we collected 50 Stack Overflow (SO) posts, 25 GitHub issues, and 25 GitHub discussions related to architecture decisions to construct a dataset of 100 architecture-related problems. Then, we selected five LLMs to generate DR for the architecture decisions with three prompting strategies, including zero-shot, chain of thought (CoT), and LLM-based agents. With the DR provided by human experts as ground truth, the Precision of LLM-generated DR with the three prompting strategies ranges from 0.267 to 0.278, Recall from 0.627 to 0.715, and F1-score from 0.351 to 0.389. Additionally, 64.45% to 69.42% of the arguments of DR not mentioned by human experts are also helpful, 4.12% to 4.87% of the arguments have uncertain correctness, and 1.59% to 3.24% of the arguments are potentially misleading. Based on the results, we further discussed the pros and cons of the three prompting strategies and the strengths and limitations of the DR generated by LLMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the use of Large Language Models (LLMs) for automatically generating design rationale (DR) for software architecture decisions.  The authors construct a dataset of 100 architecture-related problems from Stack Overflow and GitHub, then evaluate five LLMs (gpt-3.5-turbo, gpt-4-0613, gemini-1.0-pro, llama3-8B, and mistral-7B) using three prompting strategies: zero-shot, chain of thought (CoT), and LLM-based agents. The quality of the generated DR is assessed using accuracy metrics (Precision, Recall, F1-score) and a newly defined qualitative classification called IHUM-category (Insightful, Helpful, Uncertain, Misleading). The study analyzes the effectiveness of each LLM and prompting strategy, highlighting the strengths and limitations of LLM-generated DR compared to human-provided DR.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Timely and Relevant Topic:** Automating DR generation is a significant problem. This paper directly addresses this and it has high potential in the software engineering field since LLMs have become popular recently. It's a well-motivated problem, given the documented lack of DR in practice and the potential of LLMs to alleviate this.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper employs a multi-faceted evaluation approach, using both quantitative metrics (Precision, Recall, F1-score) and a novel qualitative assessment (IHUM-category). The IHUM-category classification is a good contribution. This is crucial to understanding the nature of the LLM-generated DR beyond simple accuracy scores. They evaluate not only whether the LLM produces information aligned with human experts but also whether the generated information is "Helpful," and whether it may be misleading.
    *   **Well-Defined Methodology:** The paper describes its methodology clearly, including dataset construction, prompting strategy design, agent implementation, and evaluation procedures. The inter-rater reliability assessment is very important to reduce human bias during data labeling, extraction, and IHUM-category classification.
    *   **Comparative Analysis:** The study compares different LLMs and prompting strategies.
    *   **Identifies Strengths and Limitations:** The paper does a good job of identifying both the strengths (e.g., recall, potential for identifying key DR factors, providing diverse arguments) and limitations (e.g., lack of practical experience, context-specific details, potentially misleading information) of LLM-generated DR.
    *   **Publicly Available Dataset:** The authors provide a publicly available dataset, which fosters reproducibility and further research in the area.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Low Precision:** The biggest weakness is the relatively low precision (around 0.27) across all approaches. This means that a significant portion of the arguments generated by LLMs are either irrelevant or incorrect when compared to the human-provided DR. However, their qualitative evaluation addresses this and reveals that many of those arguments are still "helpful."
    *   **Limited Scope of Dataset:** While the dataset includes SO posts, GitHub issues, and discussions, its size (100 entries) is relatively small. A larger and more diverse dataset would provide a more robust evaluation.  The authors acknowledge that their dataset might not be comprehensive.
    *   **Evaluation as a Reference:** Some arguments in LLM-generated DR do not align with human-provided DR, and they may offer new insights.

**Novelty and Significance:**

The paper is a valuable contribution to the field for the following reasons:

*   **Addresses a Practical Need:** It tackles the practical problem of DR scarcity by exploring LLM-based automation, which has significant implications for software maintenance and evolution.
*   **Comprehensive Study:** It presents a thorough and systematic evaluation of several LLMs and prompting strategies for DR generation.
*   **Novel Evaluation Framework:** The introduction of the IHUM-category provides a more nuanced assessment of LLM-generated DR, moving beyond simple accuracy metrics to consider usefulness and potential for misleading information.
*   **Findings Illuminate Future Research:** The identified strengths and limitations of LLM-generated DR offer important insights for future research directions, such as improving contextual understanding and knowledge integration.

However, the low precision and limited dataset somewhat detract from the overall impact.

**Score:** 7

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a well-executed study on a timely and relevant topic. The use of LLMs to generate DR is a promising avenue for addressing the challenges of software architecture documentation.  The methodology is sound, the evaluation is multi-faceted (accuracy metrics and IHUM-category), and the authors clearly identify the strengths and weaknesses of the various approaches. The availability of the dataset is also a plus.

The main reason for not assigning a higher score is the relatively low precision across all LLM approaches. While the IHUM-category analysis provides some justification, a higher precision would significantly increase the practical value of the generated DR. Furthermore, the relatively small dataset limits the generalizability of the findings. A score of 7 reflects a solid contribution with practical relevance, a good methodology, but limitations in quantitative performance and scope that warrant further research.

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 7/10

### Q-Fusion: Diffusing Quantum Circuits
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20794v1)
- **Authors**: Collin Beaudoin, Swaroop Ghosh
- **Abstract**: Quantum computing holds great potential for solving socially relevant and computationally complex problems. Furthermore, quantum machine learning (QML) promises to rapidly improve our current machine learning capabilities. However, current noisy intermediate-scale quantum (NISQ) devices are constrained by limitations in the number of qubits and gate counts, which hinder their full capabilities. Furthermore, the design of quantum algorithms remains a laborious task, requiring significant domain expertise and time. Quantum Architecture Search (QAS) aims to streamline this process by automatically generating novel quantum circuits, reducing the need for manual intervention. In this paper, we propose a diffusion-based algorithm leveraging the LayerDAG framework to generate new quantum circuits. This method contrasts with other approaches that utilize large language models (LLMs), reinforcement learning (RL), variational autoencoders (VAE), and similar techniques. Our results demonstrate that the proposed model consistently generates 100% valid quantum circuit outputs.
- **Summary**: Okay, I'll provide a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Q-Fusion: Diffusing Quantum Circuits".

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Q-Fusion, a novel approach for quantum architecture search (QAS) based on a diffusion model. Q-Fusion leverages the LayerDAG framework, representing quantum circuits as directed acyclic graphs (DAGs) where nodes are quantum gates and edges represent qubit connections. The method involves a forward diffusion process that progressively adds noise to a quantum circuit's graph representation and a reverse diffusion process, where a neural network learns to denoise and reconstruct valid quantum circuits. The authors demonstrate that Q-Fusion can generate valid quantum circuits, including both non-parametric and parametric circuits, and analyze the circuits' functionality using density matrices and expressibility metrics. The study seeks to address limitations of other QAS approaches like LLMs, reinforcement learning, and variational autoencoders, aiming to streamline quantum algorithm development.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The application of diffusion models, specifically within the LayerDAG framework, to quantum circuit generation is a novel combination. Diffusion models have demonstrated impressive performance in image generation, and their adaptation to graph-structured quantum circuits is a reasonable extension. The use of virtual start/end nodes to maintain graph structure is also an interesting architectural design choice. However, other existing QAS strategies already explore graph-based or generative models. The crucial element is whether Q-Fusion offers advantages over them.

*   **Significance:** Quantum Architecture Search is an important area, because manual quantum programming is difficult. If Q-Fusion could reliably generate performant quantum circuits, especially for relevant tasks in quantum machine learning or other domains, it would be a significant contribution. The paper takes a step towards automating the design of quantum circuits, which is important considering the current shortage of quantum algorithm designers and the limitations imposed by NISQ devices.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Guaranteed Validity:** The paper claims to generate 100% valid quantum circuits consistently, which is a notable achievement and a key requirement for any QAS system.
    *   **Adaptation of Diffusion Models:** Successfully adapts a well-established machine learning technique (diffusion models) to a new domain, demonstrating its versatility.
    *   **Meaningful Circuits:** The inclusion of density matrix analysis to filter for "meaningful" circuits offers insight into whether the generated circuits have non-trivial functionality.
    *   **Experiments with Parametric Circuits:** The inclusion of tests using parametric circuits (PQCs) is valuable because PQCs are essential for variational quantum algorithms and QML.
    *   **IBM Heron gate set:** The use of the IBM Heron gate set is a relevant choice.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Evaluation:** The performance metrics used, such as the percentage of valid circuits and the percentage of meaningful circuits (based on a simplistic density matrix threshold), are not strong indicators of the generated circuits' utility. The true test is how well these circuits perform on benchmark quantum algorithms.
    *   **Lack of Comparison:** The paper lacks a direct comparison to other state-of-the-art QAS methods (e.g., reinforcement learning, VAEs, LLMs). Without a comparison, it is difficult to assess whether Q-Fusion offers any real advantages.
    *   **Small Circuit Sizes:** The experiments are limited to relatively small circuits (up to 5 qubits and 32 gates). The scalability of the approach to larger, more complex circuits needs to be demonstrated.
    *   **Limited "Quantum Knowledge":** The authors acknowledge that the model lacks inherent quantum knowledge, which could affect its ability to generate functional and optimized circuits.
    *   **Meaning of Labels:** Relying on summations of elements from density matrices is likely to introduce noise and imprecision.

*   **Potential Influence:** If the scalability and performance issues are addressed, Q-Fusion could influence the field by providing a new, viable approach for automated quantum circuit design. However, the current state of the paper limits its potential impact.

**Justification for Score:**

While Q-Fusion presents a novel combination of diffusion models and the LayerDAG framework for QAS, several limitations hinder its potential. The limited evaluation metrics, the lack of comparison with existing methods, and the small circuit sizes make it difficult to assess the true impact of this work. The limitations in "quantum knowledge" are also a concern. The guaranteed validity of the circuit generation is encouraging but insufficient to overcome these concerns. This paper's novelty is moderate, but the significance and validation are lacking.

### Hallucination by Code Generation LLMs: Taxonomy, Benchmarks, Mitigation, and Challenges
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20799v1)
- **Authors**: Yunseo Lee, John Youngeun Song, Dongsun Kim, Jindae Kim, Mijung Kim, Jaechang Nam
- **Abstract**: Recent technical breakthroughs in large language models (LLMs) have enabled them to fluently generate source code. Software developers often leverage both general-purpose and code-specialized LLMs to revise existing code or even generate a whole function from scratch. These capabilities are also beneficial in no-code or low-code contexts, in which one can write programs without a technical background. However, due to their internal design, LLMs are prone to generating hallucinations, which are incorrect, nonsensical, and not justifiable information but difficult to identify its presence. This problem also occurs when generating source code. Once hallucinated code is produced, it is often challenging for users to identify and fix it, especially when such hallucinations can be identified under specific execution paths. As a result, the hallucinated code may remain unnoticed within the codebase. This survey investigates recent studies and techniques relevant to hallucinations generated by CodeLLMs. We categorize the types of hallucinations in the code generated by CodeLLMs, review existing benchmarks and mitigation strategies, and identify open challenges. Based on these findings, this survey outlines further research directions in the detection and removal of hallucinations produced by CodeLLMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents a survey on hallucinations in code generation by Large Language Models (LLMs), termed CodeLLMs. It categorizes types of hallucinations, reviews existing benchmarks and mitigation strategies, and identifies open challenges. The paper structures a detailed taxonomy of code hallucinations, categorizes existing benchmarks, consolidates root causes, and surveys current mitigation strategies. It differentiates itself from general LLM hallucination surveys by focusing specifically on code generation, including a specialized taxonomy, reviewing relevant benchmarks, and exploring code-specific mitigation approaches.  The survey aims to provide a comprehensive analysis to facilitate further research in detecting and removing hallucinations in CodeLLMs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  While surveys on LLMs and code generation exist, this paper's specific focus on *hallucinations in code generation* attempts to fill a gap. However, a key question is the extent to which the identified taxonomy and mitigation strategies truly differ from what might be gleaned from general LLM hallucination research *with a coding lens*. The taxonomy, while detailed, largely extends existing classifications rather than introducing radically new concepts. The survey's coverage of benchmarks is also valuable. Its main claim to novelty is that it synthesizes existing knowledge into a code-specific analysis of code-hallucinations.

*   **Significance:** Addressing hallucinations in code generation is undoubtedly important. The survey attempts to highlight the severity of the problem by stating they can lead to bugs, security vulnerabilities, and functional incorrectness. Providing a structured overview of the problem space, the existing approaches, and the open challenges is valuable to researchers and practitioners entering the field. It attempts to create a useful frame of reference.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear categorization of code hallucinations.
    *   Review of relevant benchmarks.
    *   Identification of causes of hallucinations.
    *   Structured overview of mitigation strategies.
    *   Comprehensive framework with taxonomy, benchmarks, causes, and mitigation techniques.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The novelty of the proposed taxonomy is incremental, not transformative. It could be argued that many aspects of the taxonomy are simply specializations of existing LLM hallucination classifications.
    *   The paper relies heavily on Google Scholar and citation snowballing. This can be a good starting point but may miss relevant information indexed elsewhere (e.g., in specialized libraries of research papers).
    *   A greater critical analysis of the *limitations* of existing benchmarks and mitigation strategies would enhance the paper. While the paper mentions their shortcomings, a more in-depth discussion on why these approaches are insufficient and the direction of future progress would be very helpful.
    *   The mitigation strategies are not discussed in enough detail to fully understand their implementation and effectiveness. A more detailed analysis and a comparison of their performance based on specific metrics could significantly add more value to the work.
    *   The survey methodology could be improved by specifying exclusion criteria (e.g., paper age, language, accessibility, etc.).
    *   The analysis could benefit from insights beyond the papers studied. Including interviews with researchers and practitioners in the field could enrich and validate the identified categories and challenges.

*   **Potential Influence:** The survey is likely to be a useful starting point for researchers investigating hallucinations in CodeLLMs, particularly in identifying relevant benchmarks and mitigation strategies. The taxonomy also provides a useful framework for further research. However, its incremental contribution reduces its potential for truly groundbreaking impact.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper addresses an important and timely topic, and its structured overview of hallucinations in code generation is valuable. However, the limited novelty and the relatively shallow depth of analysis, especially in relation to the limitations of existing approaches and possible future directions, hold it back. Also, while aiming to be comprehensive, improvements in literature search and critical validation with external insights could have increased its impact. Therefore, while useful as a resource, it doesn't represent a major breakthrough.

### SoccerDiffusion: Toward Learning End-to-End Humanoid Robot Soccer from Gameplay Recordings
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20808v1)
- **Authors**: Florian Vahl, Jörn Griepenburg, Jan Gutsche, Jasper Güldenstein, Jianwei Zhang
- **Abstract**: This paper introduces SoccerDiffusion, a transformer-based diffusion model designed to learn end-to-end control policies for humanoid robot soccer directly from real-world gameplay recordings. Using data collected from RoboCup competitions, the model predicts joint command trajectories from multi-modal sensor inputs, including vision, proprioception, and game state. We employ a distillation technique to enable real-time inference on embedded platforms that reduces the multi-step diffusion process to a single step. Our results demonstrate the model's ability to replicate complex motion behaviors such as walking, kicking, and fall recovery both in simulation and on physical robots. Although high-level tactical behavior remains limited, this work provides a robust foundation for subsequent reinforcement learning or preference optimization methods. We release the dataset, pretrained models, and code under: https://bit-bots.github.io/SoccerDiffusion
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "SoccerDiffusion: Toward Learning End-to-End Humanoid Robot Soccer from Gameplay Recordings":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SoccerDiffusion, a transformer-based diffusion model designed to learn end-to-end control policies for humanoid robots in soccer directly from real-world gameplay recordings. The model takes multi-modal sensor inputs (vision, proprioception, game state) and predicts joint command trajectories.  The authors employ a distillation technique to enable real-time inference on embedded platforms. They demonstrate the model's ability to replicate complex motions like walking, kicking, and fall recovery in simulation and on physical robots, providing a foundation for subsequent reinforcement learning or preference optimization.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in the application of transformer-based diffusion models to the specific domain of *full* humanoid robot soccer, trained directly from real-world gameplay recordings.  While diffusion models and transformers have been used in robotics previously, the scale and complexity of the humanoid soccer task, alongside learning from raw gameplay data, sets it apart.  The end-to-end approach, aiming to circumvent hand-crafted interfaces, is also a step forward.  The use of distillation for real-time deployment, while a standard technique, is practically relevant in this context. A significant aspect is that the researchers have openly shared the dataset and code, further enhancing the study's value and fostering reproducibility.

* **Significance:** The significance of the work rests on several factors. First, it demonstrates the feasibility of learning complex, dynamic robot behaviors directly from observed gameplay. This bypasses the need for explicit reward function engineering or laborious manual programming of basic skills. It opens the door to more data-driven approaches in humanoid robotics. Second, it establishes a robust base model that can potentially be used for transfer learning or as a starting point for more sophisticated RL-based training. Third, making the dataset publicly available encourages further research and comparison in this area.

* **Strengths:**
    * **End-to-end learning:**  Learning directly from raw sensor data to joint commands is a significant challenge and strength.
    * **Complex task:** Humanoid robot soccer is a highly complex and dynamic task, showcasing the capabilities of the approach.
    * **Real-world data:** Using real-world gameplay recordings makes the learned behaviors relevant and transferable.
    * **Distillation:** Addressing the computational cost with distillation is a crucial step towards practical deployment.
    * **Open source:** The release of the dataset and code is a major strength, promoting reproducibility and further research.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited high-level behavior:**  The authors acknowledge that tactical behavior remains limited. This is a significant constraint as robot soccer requires strategic decision-making.
    * **Bias towards stillness:** The model exhibits a tendency to remain stationary. This is likely due to the data distribution and is a notable limitation.
    * **Lack of comparative benchmarking:** While the authors mention that they couldn’t reach the same level of performance as the Hamburg Bit-Bots, including more concrete benchmark comparisons with existing methods would have strengthened the evaluation.
    * **Limited raw IMU data:** Lack of raw IMU data potentially limits the model's ability to learn precise movements and dynamic stability.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper provides a solid foundation for future research in end-to-end humanoid robot control. The open-sourced dataset and models can be used by other researchers to develop more advanced control strategies, including those incorporating reinforcement learning or preference optimization. The findings could influence the design of future robot learning systems, particularly in domains where large datasets of expert behavior are available.

**Rigorous Rationale:**

The paper presents a compelling application of diffusion models to a challenging robotics problem. The novelty lies in the specific combination of techniques applied to humanoid robot soccer using real-world gameplay data, and the demonstration of end-to-end control.  While the limitations in high-level behavior and the bias towards stillness are important constraints, the overall approach and the release of resources make it a valuable contribution to the field. It presents a significant step towards more data-driven learning in robotics, and the presented methodology is extensible to other robotics domains. This motivates a good rating and indicates that the authors have done excellent work in this space.

### Secure Coding with AI, From Creation to Inspection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20814v1)
- **Authors**: Vladislav Belozerov, Peter J Barclay, Ashkan Sami
- **Abstract**: While prior studies have explored security in code generated by ChatGPT and other Large Language Models, they were conducted in controlled experimental settings and did not use code generated or provided from actual developer interactions. This paper not only examines the security of code generated by ChatGPT based on real developer interactions, curated in the DevGPT dataset, but also assesses ChatGPT's capability to find and fix these vulnerabilities. We analysed 1,586 C, C++, and C# code snippets using static scanners, which detected potential issues in 124 files. After manual analysis, we selected 26 files with 32 confirmed vulnerabilities for further investigation. We submitted these files to ChatGPT via the OpenAI API, asking it to detect security issues, identify the corresponding Common Weakness Enumeration numbers, and propose fixes. The responses and modified code were manually reviewed and re-scanned for vulnerabilities. ChatGPT successfully detected 18 out of 32 security issues and resolved 17 issues but failed to recognize or fix the remainder. Interestingly, only 10 vulnerabilities were resulted from the user prompts, while 22 were introduced by ChatGPT itself. We highlight for developers that code generated by ChatGPT is more likely to contain vulnerabilities compared to their own code. Furthermore, at times ChatGPT reports incorrect information with apparent confidence, which may mislead less experienced developers. Our findings confirm previous studies in demonstrating that ChatGPT is not sufficiently reliable for generating secure code nor identifying all vulnerabilities, highlighting the continuing importance of static scanners and manual review.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Secure Coding with AI—From Creation to Inspection: A Security Analysis of ChatGPT's Coding and Vulnerability Detection Skills":

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the security implications of using ChatGPT for code generation and vulnerability detection.  Unlike prior studies using artificial prompts, this research leverages the DevGPT dataset, which comprises real-world interactions between developers and ChatGPT. The authors analyze C, C++, and C# code snippets from this dataset using static analysis tools and manual review to identify vulnerabilities.  They then assess ChatGPT's ability to detect and fix these vulnerabilities when explicitly prompted. The study reveals that ChatGPT often introduces new vulnerabilities during code generation and exhibits limitations in consistently identifying and correcting security issues.  The authors emphasize the need for manual code review and caution against over-reliance on AI tools for secure coding.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its use of the DevGPT dataset, which provides a more realistic evaluation of ChatGPT's performance compared to studies using synthetic prompts. While prior research has examined ChatGPT's code generation capabilities, this study uniquely centers on real-world developer interactions. The combination of static analysis coupled with ChatGPT's self-diagnosis of the flagged code segments using real-world developer prompts and code is a more robust experiment than similar past research.

*   **Significance:** The findings have significant practical implications for developers considering integrating ChatGPT into their workflows. The paper highlights the risks associated with relying solely on ChatGPT for secure coding and emphasizes the importance of human oversight. The study serves as a cautionary tale, demonstrating that AI-generated code can be more vulnerable than human-written code and that ChatGPT is not a reliable replacement for traditional security practices.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The use of the DevGPT dataset enhances the ecological validity of the study.
    *   The combination of static analysis, manual review, and ChatGPT's self-diagnosis provides a comprehensive evaluation.
    *   The paper identifies specific weaknesses in ChatGPT's ability to detect and fix vulnerabilities, offering concrete insights for developers and tool designers.
    *   The study systematically investigates the rate at which it introduces vulnerabilities during the editing process, while providing its own analysis, filling a gap in similar experiments.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The dataset is limited to 26 files, representing a relatively small sample size compared to the extensive code that ChatGPT can generate. This limits the generalizability of the results.
    *   The study focuses on C-family languages, which reduces the scope of the findings.
    *   The subjective nature of manual review introduces a potential source of bias. Although addressed by multiple reviews from the authors, the limitations are still present.
    *   The study only tested one version of ChatGPT and one style of prompting. Results likely could vary across different versions and different prompting approaches.

*   **Impact:** The paper will likely influence developer practices, leading to more cautious adoption of AI code generation tools and a greater emphasis on security training. It may also stimulate further research into AI-assisted security analysis, focusing on developing more robust and reliable tools.

*   **Rigorous Rationale:** The choice of the DevGPT dataset and the real-world usage cases it represents gives the experiment high external validity, which enhances its significance and justifies a relatively high score. The static analysis and manual review ensure that the conclusions are well founded. However, due to the limited dataset size and prompts, the novelty and validity are constrained, meaning it can't receive a higher score.

### Ascendra: Dynamic Request Prioritization for Efficient LLM Serving
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20828v1)
- **Authors**: Azam Ikram, Xiang Li, Sameh Elnikety, Saurabh Bagchi
- **Abstract**: The rapid advancement of Large Language Models (LLMs) has driven the need for more efficient serving strategies. In this context, efficiency refers to the proportion of requests that meet their Service Level Objectives (SLOs), particularly for Time To First Token (TTFT) and Time Between Tokens (TBT). However, existing systems often prioritize one metric at the cost of the other. We present Ascendra, an LLM serving system designed to meet both TTFT and TBT SLOs simultaneously. The core insight behind Ascendra is that a request's urgency evolves as it approaches its deadline. To leverage this, Ascendra partitions GPU resources into two types of instances: low-priority and high-priority. Low-priority instances maximize throughput by processing requests out of arrival order, but at the risk of request starvation. To address this, Ascendra employs a performance model to predict requests at risk of missing their SLOs and proactively offloads them to high-priority instances. High-priority instances are optimized for low-latency execution and handle urgent requests nearing their deadlines. This partitioned architecture enables Ascendra to effectively balance high throughput and low latency. Extensive evaluation shows that Ascendra improves system throughput by up to 1.7x compared to vLLM and Sarathi-Serve while meeting both TTFT and TBT SLOs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Ascendra: Dynamic Request Prioritization for Efficient LLM Serving":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Ascendra, a novel LLM serving system designed to optimize both Time To First Token (TTFT) and Time Between Tokens (TBT) Service Level Objectives (SLOs) simultaneously.  Ascendra partitions GPU resources into low-priority (LP) and high-priority (HP) instances. LP instances prioritize throughput and are optimized for efficient batching. HP instances focus on minimizing latency for urgent requests. The system uses a performance model to proactively offload requests from LP to HP instances when they are at risk of violating their SLOs. The authors demonstrate that Ascendra improves throughput while meeting both TTFT and TBT SLOs compared to vLLM and Sarathi-Serve.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of dynamically prioritizing requests based on their evolving urgency (time remaining before SLO violation) is a significant contribution. Partitioning resources into throughput-optimized and latency-optimized instances is also a valuable approach, although not entirely new in other contexts. The proactive offloading mechanism based on a performance model is a key differentiating factor.
*   **Significance:**  The work addresses a crucial challenge in LLM serving: balancing latency and throughput under SLO constraints.  The experimental results show substantial improvements over existing systems, suggesting Ascendra could have a real-world impact on LLM deployment efficiency.  The framework's adaptability to various models and SLOs is also a notable advantage. However, the performance advantage with smaller models appears less substantial which calls for a deeper analysis on models that fall between small and large.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and motivation.
    *   Well-designed architecture with a novel dynamic prioritization scheme.
    *   Comprehensive experimental evaluation comparing against strong baselines (vLLM and Sarathi-Serve).
    *   Demonstrated improvements in both throughput and SLO adherence.
    *   The approach doesn't rely on expensive network interconnects.
    *   Presents analysis of the computational operations (FLOPs) and memory accesses
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on a performance model could be a potential bottleneck. The model's accuracy directly impacts the effectiveness of the offloading mechanism. More details on the training data, model complexity, and adaptation strategy would be beneficial.
    *   The system could explore more advanced scheduling algorithms beyond EDF (Earliest Deadline First) to further optimize performance under varying workload conditions.
    *   The system primarily focuses on TTFT and TBT SLOs. Other important factors, such as fairness across users and resource allocation based on user priorities, are not explicitly addressed.
    *   Lack of a clear strategy on model size - performance advantages for LLAMA3.1-8B are not as pronounced in comparison to Mistral-7B - it would have been useful to see an explanation to address the performance delta.
    *   Dropping request under fluctuation might be a crude solution to addressing high system loads - a more nuanced approach is required to prioritize dropping request from those that may have had bad behaviors, vs, wholesale dropping of request.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the design of future LLM serving systems, particularly those that aim to provide SLO guarantees. The dynamic prioritization and resource partitioning techniques could be adopted and extended by other researchers and practitioners.
*   **Reproducibility:** The open-source nature of vLLM and the detailed description of Ascendra's design and implementation should facilitate reproducibility.

**Rigorous Rationale:**
The paper presents a well-engineered and practically relevant solution to a critical problem in LLM serving. The dynamic prioritization and resource partitioning strategies are novel and demonstrably effective. However, the reliance on a performance model, limited exploration of scheduling algorithms, and exclusion of some important system aspects (fairness, user priorities) prevent it from achieving a higher score.

### Reinforcement Learning for LLM Reasoning Under Memory Constraints
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20834v1)
- **Authors**: Alan Lee, Harry Tong
- **Abstract**: We explore reinforcement learning (RL) techniques to enhance reasoning within targeted problem spaces in large language models (LLMs) under memory and compute constraints. Our focus is on critic-free methods compatible with LoRA fine-tuning on a single 40GB GPU, a common limitation in academic settings. We introduce S-GRPO, a memory-efficient variant of Group Relative Policy Optimization, and T-SPMO, a token-level prefix matching strategy for fine-grained credit assignment. Despite limited resources, when used to fine-tune Qwen2-1.5B both methods significantly improve SVAMP benchmark accuracy from 46% to above 70% using LoRA training. T-SPMO also excels in multi-digit multiplication tasks, underscoring the potential of RL fine-tuning under hardware constraints. Additionally, we find that our full-token GRPO baseline under LoRA fine-tuning did not improve model performance (compared to base model) on either task, suggesting that our memory-efficient methods may act as a form of regularization that stabilizes training when only a small subset of parameters are updated.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores reinforcement learning (RL) techniques to improve reasoning capabilities of large language models (LLMs) under limited computational resources (specifically, a single 40GB GPU and LoRA fine-tuning). The authors introduce two novel critic-free RL methods: Stochastic Group Relative Policy Optimization (S-GRPO), a memory-efficient variant of GRPO, and Token-Specific Prefix Matching Optimization (T-SPMO), a token-level credit assignment strategy.  Experiments using Qwen2-1.5B on SVAMP and a multi-digit multiplication task demonstrate significant improvements compared to the base model, especially with T-SPMO.  Surprisingly, the full-token GRPO baseline did not perform better than the base model, suggesting that memory-efficient methods may act as regularization in low-resource fine-tuning.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The introduction of S-GRPO and T-SPMO constitutes the primary novelty.  S-GRPO presents a fairly straightforward modification of GRPO to reduce memory footprint by token sampling. T-SPMO, the more innovative of the two, introduces token-specific credit assignment which is more novel. The finding that a full GRPO baseline actually *underperforms* compared to the base model, and the hypothesis that constrained methods lead to better regularization when only a small subset of parameters is updated, is a valuable and somewhat unexpected observation. The adaptation of RL methods to work within the confines of LoRA fine-tuning on resource-constrained hardware is a practical contribution.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from the increased accessibility of RL fine-tuning for reasoning in LLMs to researchers with limited computational resources. The paper effectively demonstrates that meaningful improvements can be achieved using readily available hardware, which democratizes access to advanced fine-tuning techniques. The positive results on SVAMP, which is acknowledged as an out-of-distribution benchmark for many LLMs, further increases the significance. The relative success of T-SPMO in comparison to S-GRPO suggests a novel potential for fine-grained credit assignment in downstream performance in terms of memory and accuracy.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem statement and well-defined objectives.
    *   Novel algorithm design (S-GRPO and T-SPMO).
    *   Demonstration of the methods on challenging reasoning benchmarks.
    *   Strong empirical results showing substantial improvements compared to the base model and SOTA approaches.
    *   Careful ablation studies to understand the impact of hyper-parameters.
    *   Honest discussion of limitations.
    *   Addresses a resource constraint and offers practical solutions to democratize the RL process.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Limited comparison to other parameter-efficient fine-tuning methods (besides the GRPO baseline).  Comparing against other LoRA fine-tuning methods tailored for mathematical or logical reasoning would further contextualize the contribution.
    *   The algorithms are evaluated on a single base model.
    *   While the hypothesis about regularization with sparse updates is interesting, it needs more rigorous validation. For instance, examining the learned weights to verify the hypothesis.
    *   The ablation study on the GRPO baseline could be more exhaustive.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence researchers working on LLM fine-tuning, especially those with limited computational resources. The proposed methods and insights into regularization during constrained fine-tuning could guide future research on efficient RL algorithms for LLMs. The modular T-SPMO design may open up doors to future methods by incorporating prefix trees or latent representations into existing RL frameworks.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field by introducing two novel, memory-efficient RL methods for fine-tuning LLMs for reasoning under resource constraints. The empirical results are compelling, demonstrating substantial improvements over the base model and the baseline GRPO. The findings on regularization during constrained fine-tuning are intriguing and warrant further investigation. Although the paper has some limitations, the strengths outweigh the weaknesses, and the potential influence on the field is significant.

### Enhancing Non-Core Language Instruction-Following in Speech LLMs via Semi-Implicit Cross-Lingual CoT Reasoning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20835v1)
- **Authors**: Hongfei Xue, Yufeng Tang, Hexin Liu, Jun Zhang, Xuelong Geng, Lei Xie
- **Abstract**: Large language models have been extended to the speech domain, leading to the development of speech large language models (SLLMs). While existing SLLMs demonstrate strong performance in speech instruction-following for core languages (e.g., English), they often struggle with non-core languages due to the scarcity of paired speech-text data and limited multilingual semantic reasoning capabilities. To address this, we propose the semi-implicit Cross-lingual Speech Chain-of-Thought (XS-CoT) framework, which integrates speech-to-text translation into the reasoning process of SLLMs. The XS-CoT generates four types of tokens: instruction and response tokens in both core and non-core languages, enabling cross-lingual transfer of reasoning capabilities. To mitigate inference latency in generating target non-core response tokens, we incorporate a semi-implicit CoT scheme into XS-CoT, which progressively compresses the first three types of intermediate reasoning tokens while retaining global reasoning logic during training. By leveraging the robust reasoning capabilities of the core language, XS-CoT improves responses for non-core languages by up to 45\% in GPT-4 score when compared to direct supervised fine-tuning on two representative SLLMs, Qwen2-Audio and SALMONN. Moreover, the semi-implicit XS-CoT reduces token delay by more than 50\% with a slight drop in GPT-4 scores. Importantly, XS-CoT requires only a small amount of high-quality training data for non-core languages by leveraging the reasoning capabilities of core languages. To support training, we also develop a data pipeline and open-source speech instruction-following datasets in Japanese, German, and French.
- **Summary**: ### Summary of the Paper The paper presents a new framework called the semi-implicit Cross-lingual Speech Chain-of-Thought (XS-CoT) for enhancing the instruction-following capabilities of speech large language models (SLLMs) in non-core languages. Current SLLMs perform well in core languages like English but face challenges in non-core languages due to limited training data and semantic reasoning issues. The XS-CoT framework integrates speech-to-text translation to facilitate cross-lingual reasoning. It generates tokens for both instructions and responses in multiple languages, enhancing the SLLMs' ability to transfer reasoning skills from core to non-core languages. By adopting a semi-implicit CoT approach, the framework compresses intermediate reasoning tokens, effectively improving response accuracy for non-core languages by up to 45% compared to traditional fine-tuning methods, while also cutting inference latency by over 50%. Furthermore, XS-CoT requires minimal high-quality training data for non-core languages and comes with an open-source data pipeline for training, featuring instruction-following datasets in Japanese, German, and French. ### Critical Evaluation #### Novelty and Significance The paper introduces a novel approach by combining speech-to-text translation with a reasoning framework specifically for SLLMs, addressing a significant limitation in the current landscape of language models related to non-core languages. The integration of semi-implicit reasoning is particularly inventive as it allows for more efficient processing and enhanced performance without a corresponding increase in computational burden. Furthermore, the decision to provide open-source datasets aimed at less-resourced languages contributes positively to the field by fostering further research and development in these areas. One of the notable strengths of the study is its empirical validation showing substantial improvements in performance metrics (GPT-4 scores) when employing XS-CoT across non-core languages. The approach's ability to reduce both token generation latency and the necessity for large datasets is a significant step towards making SLLMs more practical for diverse linguistic applications. #### Weaknesses However, there are several limitations that should be noted. The dependency on core language reasoning could limit the applicability of this method when transitioning to languages that are structurally different from the core languages used for training. The effectiveness of XS-CoT across a wider variety of non-core languages and dialects remains unexplored; thus, the generalizability of the findings isn't established. The paper also lacks a thorough analysis of the potential biases introduced through the cross-lingual transfer, which can have implications for the fairness and accuracy of the SLLMs in operation. Another concern is that while the reductions in token delay and training data requirements are impressive, the paper does not clearly quantify the trade-offs in performance, nor does it provide extensive scenario testing to illustrate the XS-CoT framework's consistency across varying input contexts. ### Conclusion Overall, the XS-CoT framework represents a significant step forward in addressing limitations faced by SLLMs in instruction-following tasks for non-core languages, showcasing creativity in its approach and potential to enhance multilingual applications. However, more comprehensive validation and exploration of its limitations are essential for establishing its broader impact and replicability across diverse languages. **Score: 8**
- **Classification**: cs.SD
- **Score**: 8/10

### AI-GenBench: A New Ongoing Benchmark for AI-Generated Image Detection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20865v1)
- **Authors**: Lorenzo Pellegrini, Davide Cozzolino, Serafino Pandolfini, Davide Maltoni, Matteo Ferrara, Luisa Verdoliva, Marco Prati, Marco Ramilli
- **Abstract**: The rapid advancement of generative AI has revolutionized image creation, enabling high-quality synthesis from text prompts while raising critical challenges for media authenticity. We present Ai-GenBench, a novel benchmark designed to address the urgent need for robust detection of AI-generated images in real-world scenarios. Unlike existing solutions that evaluate models on static datasets, Ai-GenBench introduces a temporal evaluation framework where detection methods are incrementally trained on synthetic images, historically ordered by their generative models, to test their ability to generalize to new generative models, such as the transition from GANs to diffusion models. Our benchmark focuses on high-quality, diverse visual content and overcomes key limitations of current approaches, including arbitrary dataset splits, unfair comparisons, and excessive computational demands. Ai-GenBench provides a comprehensive dataset, a standardized evaluation protocol, and accessible tools for both researchers and non-experts (e.g., journalists, fact-checkers), ensuring reproducibility while maintaining practical training requirements. By establishing clear evaluation rules and controlled augmentation strategies, Ai-GenBench enables meaningful comparison of detection methods and scalable solutions. Code and data are publicly available to ensure reproducibility and to support the development of robust forensic detectors to keep pace with the rise of new synthetic generators.
- **Summary**: ### Summary The paper introduces Ai-GenBench, a new benchmark to tackle the detection of AI-generated images amidst the rapid advancements in generative models. Unlike existing benchmarks that rely on static datasets, Ai-GenBench employs a temporal evaluation framework, allowing detection models to be trained on a chronological sequence of synthetic images produced by various generative models, such as GANs and diffusion models. This approach diminishes issues found in prior methodologies, including arbitrary dataset splits and computational inefficiency. The benchmark includes a comprehensive dataset, a standardized evaluation protocol, and tools that cater to both academia and non-expert practitioners like journalists. By promoting reproducibility, clear evaluation criteria, and controlled augmentation methods, Ai-GenBench aims to enhance the comparability of detection techniques and offers scalable solutions amid the evolving landscape of synthetic image generation. ### Critical Evaluation **Novelty and Contribution** Ai-GenBench introduces a significant methodology shift by focusing on temporal evaluation rather than static datasets. This temporal approach is vital as it reflects the evolution of generative models in real-world applications, thus providing a more relevant context for the development of detection methods. The emphasis on high-quality, diverse visual content also addresses a notable gap in current evaluation benchmarks that often rely on lower-quality or homogeneous datasets. **Strengths** 1. **Innovative Framework**: The introduction of a temporal evaluation allows for a more realistic assessment of AI-generated image detection models, addressing the evolving nature of generative AI technologies. 2. **Comprehensive Approach**: By providing a standardized protocol, dataset, and tools, the project encourages reproducibility and accessibility, fostering collaboration across different fields, including journalism and fact-checking. 3. **Practical Application**: The availability of tools aimed at non-experts enhances the impact, enabling broader applications beyond academia. **Weaknesses and Limitations** 1. **Complexity in Real-World Adaptation**: While the temporal evaluation is innovative, its actual implementation in real-world scenarios, where images may come from diverse sources and contexts, may still present challenges. 2. **Dependence on Dataset Quality**: The effectiveness of Ai-GenBench's methods will hinge on the quality and diversity of the synthetic datasets used, which may not fully represent all potential generative scenarios. 3. **Potential Overfitting**: Training models incrementally on temporally ordered data could lead to overfitting on specific generative trends, possibly hindering generalization to entirely new generative methodologies. **Potential Influence** This benchmark has the potential to significantly influence detection methods in the field of AI-generated media, setting a new standard for how AI image detection is conducted moving forward. By addressing both academic concerns and practical applications, Ai-GenBench may pave the way for improved forensic tools that can better handle the complexities introduced by varying generative models. ### Score: 8 In summary, Ai-GenBench presents a commendable advance in benchmarking AI-generated image detection, establishing a foundation for future research and practical applications. While its innovative temporal evaluation framework and focus on comprehensive, standardized evaluation protocols are strengths, challenges in real-world adaptation and dependency on dataset quality limit its immediate applicability. Nevertheless, its significance in fostering reliable detection methods amid rapid advancements in generative AI merits a high score for novelty and relevance in the field.
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### LELANTE: LEveraging LLM for Automated ANdroid TEsting
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.20896v1)
- **Authors**: Shamit Fatin, Mehbubul Hasan Al-Quvi, Haz Sameen Shahgir, Sukarna Barua, Anindya Iqbal, Sadia Sharmin, Md. Mostofa Akbar, Kallol Kumar Pal, A. Asif Al Rashid
- **Abstract**: Given natural language test case description for an Android application, existing testing approaches require developers to manually write scripts using tools such as Appium and Espresso to execute the corresponding test case. This process is labor-intensive and demands significant effort to maintain as UI interfaces evolve throughout development. In this work, we introduce LELANTE, a novel framework that utilizes large language models (LLMs) to automate test case execution without requiring pre-written scripts. LELANTE interprets natural language test case descriptions, iteratively generate action plans, and perform the actions directly on the Android screen using its GUI. LELANTE employs a screen refinement process to enhance LLM interpretability, constructs a structured prompt for LLMs, and implements an action generation mechanism based on chain-of-thought reasoning of LLMs. To further reduce computational cost and enhance scalability, LELANTE utilizes model distillation using a foundational LLM. In experiments across 390 test cases spanning 10 popular Android applications, LELANTE achieved a 73% test execution success rate. Our results demonstrate that LLMs can effectively bridge the gap between natural language test case description and automated execution, making mobile testing more scalable and adaptable.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the LELANTE paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LELANTE, a novel framework leveraging large language models (LLMs) to automate Android application testing directly from natural language test case descriptions.  LELANTE eliminates the need for manually written test scripts by interpreting these descriptions, iteratively generating action plans, and executing them directly on the Android GUI.  Key components include a screen refinement process to enhance LLM interpretability, a structured prompt design, and a chain-of-thought reasoning-based action generation mechanism.  To improve efficiency, LELANTE employs model distillation using a foundational LLM. Experiments across 390 test cases in 10 popular Android applications demonstrate a 73% test execution success rate.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core idea of using LLMs to directly execute Android test cases based on natural language descriptions has been explored before (e.g., AutoDroid). However, LELANTE brings several novel contributions:

    * **Structured Prompting and Chain-of-Thought Reasoning:**  The paper emphasizes a *structured* prompt tailored to the Android UI interaction domain and explicitly uses chain-of-thought reasoning. This moves beyond simply asking the LLM "what to do next" and provides a framework for sequential decision making within the testing context.
    * **GUI Refinement:** The screen refinement process (simplifying the XML representation) is crucial.  It makes the raw GUI structure more manageable for the LLM and filters out irrelevant information. This enhances the LLM's ability to understand the screen and make informed decisions.
    * **Error Recovery:**  The built-in 'Back' button and the LLM's ability to reason about past actions and backtrack demonstrate a significant advance over naive execution strategies. This is a crucial component for making the system more robust in the face of unexpected UI changes or LLM errors. While recovery is not perfect, the high recovery rate emphasizes this improvement.
    * **Model Distillation:** This is a pragmatic and important contribution. Using computationally demanding foundational LLMs for every test case step would be prohibitively expensive. Model distillation is key to enabling wider adoption and scalability by using a smaller, more efficient model that can run locally.
    * **Automated Detection of Incorrect Actions:** The capacity to analyze the execution history and current screen to identify any inaccurate or unintended actions indicates a more sophisticated approach to test automation.

* **Significance:** The paper has the potential to significantly impact the field of mobile application testing.

    * **Reduced Manual Effort:** Eliminating the need for writing and maintaining Appium or Espresso scripts would greatly reduce the cost and effort of testing, especially for complex applications.
    * **Improved Scalability and Adaptability:**  LELANTE can adapt to changes in the UI without requiring manual script updates. This is essential for agile development environments.
    * **Enabling More Comprehensive Testing:**  By making testing cheaper and easier, the framework could enable teams to conduct more thorough testing, leading to higher software quality.

* **Weaknesses:**

    * **Dependence on Test Case Quality:** As the paper itself acknowledges, the quality of the natural language test case description is crucial. Ambiguous or poorly written descriptions can reduce accuracy. While the paper provides some guidelines, further research is needed on automatically refining test descriptions.
    * **Limited Evaluation of Success Rate:** While 73% is reasonably good, understanding *why* the 27% of cases failed would be valuable.  Were the failures due to LLM reasoning errors, limitations in the screen refinement process, difficulty mapping actions to UI elements, or something else? A deeper dive into the error types and their causes would strengthen the evaluation.
    * **Automated validation of Test Results:** The lack of automated verification of the final test result is a significant limitation. The framework can automate *execution*, but it still relies on human testers to verify the outcome. This greatly limits the scalability of the approach.
    * **Incremental Learning:** The paper proposes that improvements of prompt-based or incremental learning techniques will improve the accuracy of the results but lacks experimentation and analysis to demonstrate its value.
